# Decision Log

Record every non-trivial decision made during the build: what, why, alternatives
considered. Append-only.

| # | Phase | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0 | Money = integer cents, branded type; round-half-away-from-zero | Auditability; floats forbidden by spec |
| 2 | 0 | Business dates are date-only (YYYY-MM-DD), single dates.ts utility | Timezone bugs are the top fintech date hazard |
| 3 | 0 | Obligation timeline computed on demand, not stored | Single source of truth; avoids cache-invalidation bugs |
| 4 | 0 | Weekend/holiday due dates adjust to PRIOR business day (conservative) | Funds present early is safe; late is not |
| 5 | 0 | v1 minimum-path interest = simple monthly (carried × APR/12), labeled approximate **[SUPERSEDED by #29: now average-daily-balance]** | Hand-verifiable; ADB method is roadmap |
| 6 | 1 | SQLite for local dev/test via Prisma 7 (`prisma-client` generator → `src/generated/prisma`); schema kept Postgres-portable (no SQLite-only features; enums/Json represented as String columns with documented values) | Postgres unavailable in build env; CLAUDE.md explicitly allows SQLite |
| 7 | 1 | Business dates stored as String `YYYY-MM-DD` (not DateTime @db.Date) | SQLite has no DATE type; lexicographic compare works; single dates.ts utility validates |
| 8 | 1 | Added `BalanceSnapshot` model (month-end balance per account) | Drives the net-worth trend chart deterministically from seeded history; additive over the Phase 0 draft shape |
| 9 | 1 | "This cycle" = obligations from GENERATED statements; a not-yet-generated statement (estimate) is next cycle — shown as upcoming, excluded from headline/projection — unless no generated statement exists at all (then the estimate IS the answer) | An un-generated statement closes in the future and is due even later, so it can never bind before current statements; keeps the headline answer and its hand math stable |
| 10 | 1 | Within a projected day: scheduled flows post BEFORE card payments are drawn | ACH credits typically post before card-payment cutoffs; deterministic, tested, surfaced in assumptions |
| 11 | 1 | Seed's engineered intra-period dip placed between the two due dates (rent 06-24, payroll 06-26): endpoints positive, mid-period −$1,012.33 | With checking ≈$3,400 and total due $5,412.33 fixed by SEED_SPEC, a pre-06-15 below-zero dip that fully recovers is arithmetically impossible; this placement is the exact mirror of edge case H (endpoint fine, intra-period negative) |
| 12 | 1 | Demo mode pins "today" via `DEMO_TODAY` env (default = seed asOf 2026-06-10) | Engines take `today` as input; keeps the seeded dataset coherent and e2e deterministic |
| 13 | 1 | Phase 1 auth = Auth.js v5 Credentials "demo sign-in" (one click, demo user only); magic-link/Google land in Phase 4 with the security pass | Demo mode must work with zero secrets; full auth is a Phase 4 acceptance item |
| 14 | 1 | Seed statement history balances are plausible PRNG values, not exact sums of that cycle's card transactions | Reconciliation adds heavy complexity with no Phase 1–3 test value; noted as a known limitation in STATUS |
| 15 | 1 | Transfer recommendation byDate clamped to `today` (critic P1-1) | "Transfer by yesterday" is an impossible instruction; same-day transfer is the correct remedy |
| 16 | 1 | Current statement = most recent with dueDate ≥ today OR an unpaid remainder (critic P1-2) | A delinquent statement is real debt due NOW, never a next-cycle estimate |
| 17 | 2 | Mid-band amounts on a merchant with user amount-banded rules go to review | The user declared the merchant context-dependent; the gap between bands is genuinely ambiguous (EDGE_CASES §Categorization) |
| 18 | 2 | "Possibly unused" subscription heuristic = fitness-category memberships (usage is not observable in transaction data) | Surfaced as a question to the user, never an accusation; consistent with coach guardrails |
| 19 | R1 | Cash-needed engine `notes`/`assumptions` ARE presentation strings (formatCents/formatISODate inside the engine) — a deliberate exemption from the "format only at the UI boundary" convention, same as coach-copy.ts | The engine's auditability story requires self-describing output; structured note objects are a localization-era refactor |
| 20 | R1 | TanStack Query (named in CLAUDE.md conventions) is intentionally NOT used | Server components + server actions made client-side query caching unnecessary; zero client data-fetching exists |
| 21 | R1 | v1 minimum-path interest remains simple-monthly; PHASE_0 §4's "binding" ADB wording is superseded by DECISIONS #5 **[now itself superseded by #29 — ADB implemented, matching PHASE_0 §4's original intent]** | The in-app label, STATUS #2 and ROADMAP #3 all agree; PHASE_0 annotated in place |
| 22 | R2 | Required ingestion pipeline order for ANY future transaction source (Plaid, CSV, manual): normalizeMerchant → merchant upsert → loadUserRules → categorize(rules) → persist category/confidence/needsReview → detectTransfers → detectRecurring → ScheduledTransaction refresh | Without this single documented order, every new source re-creates the severed-rules-loop bug (review cycle 1, C2); ROADMAP #1 references it |
| 23 | R2 | Batch "apply to all" scopes to the EXACT raw descriptor for aggregate pseudo-merchants (Zelle/checks), to the merchant otherwise; aggregate rules are also dropped at the rule READ path | "All 6 payments to J. Park" is a sane batch; "all Zelle ever" is not (review cycle 2) |
| 24 | R4 | A manually-entered transaction records ACTIVITY only — it does NOT mutate the owning account's `currentBalanceCents` | Balances are provider-authoritative (the institution/seed is the source of truth); rewriting one from a freeform entry would desync the reconciled snapshot AND perturb the cash-needed engine + the pinned golden net-worth/headline values. A dedicated derived-balance "Cash" account is the clean future path (ROADMAP). The entry still flows into all transaction-based views (register, spending, insights) |
| 25 | R4 | Manual entry runs the categorize() arm of the #22 pipeline (normalizeMerchant → loadUserRules → categorize → persist category/confidence/needsReview, with isTransfer set from the verdict/explicit category) but intentionally skips merchant-table upsert and recurring re-detection | A single user-entered row has no cadence to detect, and the register's display name already falls back to the cleaned descriptor when no Merchant row exists — so the skipped steps add cost without changing behavior for one row. Full #22 order still binds for bulk sources (Plaid/CSV) |
| 26 | R4 | Plaid integration split into a TESTED pure mapping layer (plaid-map.ts: sign flip, account-type, liability→statement, per-row categorize) and an UNVERIFIED network layer (plaid.ts). Access tokens + sync cursor live in a dedicated `PlaidItem` table, not packed into Account.providerRef | The mapping is where silent ledger-corrupting bugs hide (sign flips, float→cents, liability classification) so it must be unit-tested even though live calls can't be in this env; the network layer is honestly labeled UNVERIFIED rather than claimed working. `PlaidItem` is the clean store ROADMAP #1 called for. Plaid amounts are floats — plaid-map is the single boundary where a float becomes cents (rounded once), preserving the no-float-money rule everywhere downstream |
| 27 | R4 | CSV import is the third ingest source (DECISIONS #22), fully testable without credentials. Contract: required `date`/`description`/`amount` (+ optional `category`) headers with aliases; amount SIGNED in Pulse convention (negative = out); dates as ISO or US MM/DD/YYYY; malformed rows skipped with per-line errors, good rows still imported. Like manual entry it records activity only, never mutates balances (#24) | A real user can get real transactions in TODAY by exporting a bank CSV — no Plaid, no keys — which is the highest-value go-live step that is verifiable in this environment. Signed-amount + explicit date formats avoid the ambiguity that breaks Mint/Simplifi imports; per-row error reporting means one bad line never silently drops a whole file |
| 29 | R5 | Minimum-path interest upgraded from the labeled simple-monthly approximation (carried × APR/12) to the **average-daily-balance method** (ROADMAP #3, supersedes #5/#21). Per card not paid in full: interest = round(DPR × Σ daily balances) over the next cycle [close → close+1mo], DPR = APR/10000/365, balance = full statement until the minimum posts on the due date then the carried balance after; grace-gated (paid in full → $0); new purchases not projected. Pure primitive `averageDailyBalanceInterestCents` in money.ts (own known-answer tests); the engine derives cycleDays/daysUntilDue from the statement's close+due dates (estimate path uses nextCycleClose/nextDue). Every pinned value recomputed by hand and updated with its test (EDGE_CASES §I; seed $65.76→$67.36, §I $59.30→$61.08, critic5 §I $58.81, S8 $12.23, N6 $19.17/$18.74) | The v1 formula was always labeled a documented approximation awaiting ADB (#5/#21, STATUS #2). ADB is the method real issuers use and the constitution's "financial math is sacred" priority; it is hand-verifiable and e2e-verifiable. The value is cycle-date dependent (two §I anchors differ), which is correct — the doc and tests now pin both. Grace-gating preserves the autopay-in-full = $0 behavior the old `carried` formula gave for free; modeling interest on the full balance until the minimum posts captures the lost-grace reality the flat formula missed |
| 28 | R5 | Money Dials settings/onboarding UI (`/settings`) edits the five per-user dials the engines already read but nothing could set: payment account, SWR, expected return, hourly wage, money dials. Validation lives in a pure engine module (`engine/settings/dials.ts`, 80 unit cases) — string-only parse, all-fields-at-once errors, bounds chosen so the FI engine stays defined (SWR rejected ≤ 0 because `fiNumberCents` divides by it; SWR ≤ 10%, return 0–15%, wage ≤ $10k/hr). The e2e mutates ONLY `moneyDials` (the one dial coupled to no golden value) and restores it; numeric dials are only ever written back at seed values, so the pinned cash-needed/FI numbers are never perturbed under the fullyParallel shared-DB e2e. Payment account is restricted to owned CHECKING/SAVINGS (you fund card payments from cash). Onboarding nudge on the dashboard is gated on `paymentAccountId == null` → dormant in demo (zero DOM change for existing e2e), and activates for real new users once auth/signup lands | These dials were hardcoded in the seed with no way for a real user to change them — the credential-free half of ROADMAP #2. Engine-first (per CLAUDE.md) means the risky part (parse/bounds/normalize) is exhaustively unit-tested in isolation; the server action is a thin ownership-scoped persist + audit. Gating onboarding on the payment account (the single input the whole cash-needed answer is built on) is both the semantically correct "you're not set up yet" signal and the choice that keeps the demo dashboard byte-identical for the golden above-the-fold e2e |
| 30 | R5 | Budget-targets UI (ROADMAP #7): set/clear a per-category monthly target against actuals on `/budgets`. Pure engine `engine/budgets/status.ts` (`summarizeBudgets` over the union of spent+target categories with over/remaining/pct; `netSpendByCategory` nets refunds; `isBudgetable`/`parseBudgetTargetCents`). `setBudget` is an atomic `prisma.budget.upsert` on a new `@@unique([userId, categoryId])`; `clearBudget` is an ownership-scoped deleteMany; both audit-logged. Budget targets are DISPLAY-ONLY — they feed nothing but the /budgets view (not cash-needed/FI/net-worth), so writes perturb no golden value. **Budgets net refunds locally** (signed-sum per category, keep net-outflow) even though app-wide refund-netting is deferred (ROADMAP #4 / STATUS #9): this is the FIRST surface to turn category spend into an over/under verdict + colored bar, so a gross number would mislabel a net-under-target category "over" | Found by the budget Hostile Critic (two P1s, both fixed): (1) refund-blindness would fire a false "over target" amber bar — fixed by netting within the budgets spend calc (scoped to display; the income/savings-rate aggregations stay gross per the documented convention); (2) the overwrite path was untested with no DB uniqueness guard — fixed with `@@unique` + `upsert` (structurally one row, no race) plus an e2e overwrite step. Category picker + server validation share one `isBudgetable` allow-list so the accepted set equals the offered set (no off-menu income/transfer targets). Throwing on invalid input matches the sibling `createGoal` |
| 31 | R5 | Account deletion / "delete my data" UI (ROADMAP #10, PRIVACY.md §Deletion). Typed-confirmation gate (`confirmationMatches`, pure-tested) → ownership-scoped `prisma.user.delete` that cascades every user-owned row (`onDelete: Cascade` on all relations; shared Merchant/Category left intact) → best-effort Plaid token-revoke first → `signOut`. The action is IDEMPOTENT: an existence guard skips the audit+delete when the row is already gone (double-submit / stale session / unseeded deploy) and still signs out, instead of an unhandled FK(P2003)/not-found(P2025) 500. A live deletion summary shows the high-signal counts + an honest catch-all for derived history. The destructive EXECUTION is verified by an integration test that calls the real action against throwaway users (gate-reject → no deletion; exact phrase → scoped wipe + signOut; idempotent re-run) — NOT e2e'd against the shared demo user (would break the fullyParallel suite); the e2e covers the gate + summary only. Added `(app)/error.tsx` so a post-deletion no-accounts render (or any action throw) degrades to a recoverable screen, not a raw 500 | Found/hardened by the deletion Hostile Critic (4 P1s, all fixed; cascade correctness verified down to live PRAGMA foreign_keys): the cascade was correct but (1) the action had zero execution coverage, (2) it crashed non-idempotently on an absent row, (3) post-deletion demo re-sign-in 500'd with no error boundary. Deletion is genuinely exposed now (the typed gate is the accident-safety, replacing the prior "Phase-5-deferred" stance); demo data is reseedable via `npx prisma db seed`. Multi-device session invalidation (JWT strategy) + a non-cascading compliance deletion-record are noted for the real-auth release (STATUS) |
| 32 | R5 | Offline PWA service worker (ROADMAP #5): `public/sw.js` + a precached `/offline` shell + production-only registration (`sw-register.tsx`). Deliberately minimal/conservative: navigations are NETWORK-FIRST (online always fresh, navigation responses NEVER cached → no stale/cross-user data online or on a shared device; offline → precached `/offline`); a tiny fixed icon/manifest allowlist is cache-first with a `res.ok` guard; hashed `/_next/static/*` is PASSTHROUGH (immutable + content-addressed → left to the browser HTTP cache, so SW storage stays bounded — no per-deploy accumulation). Precache is per-asset (`Promise.allSettled`) so one 404 can't void the shell; the `/offline` page is inline-styled (self-contained offline). Middleware excludes `/sw.js` (must serve as JS) and `/offline` (public shell), anchored (`offline$`/`sw\.js$`) so prefix collisions can't skip auth. `/sw.js` registered → scope `/` | Found/shaped by the PWA Hostile Critic (the 3 review-phase "P1"s — fixed cache name / atomic-precache-swallow / cache-first-stale-offline — were all adversarially downgraded to P2: no online stale-serving, no leak, no suite destabilization, verified to live PRAGMA-equivalent checks). The conservative design (network-first nav, passthrough hashed assets) is what keeps it from the classic SW footguns; the critic-flagged P2s were fixed proactively (res.ok guard, resilient precache, network-first /offline, self-contained shell, anchored matcher). A build-stamped cache name + an in-app update affordance are deferred (documented) — not needed while hashed assets are passthrough and online is network-first |
| 33 | R5 | App-wide refund netting (ROADMAP #4, supersedes STATUS #8's "refunds count as inflows"): in `monthlyFlows` (the single income/expense classifier feeding savings rate + FI), a positive transaction in a NON-income category is a refund → it reduces that month's expenses instead of counting as income; payroll/income (category `income`) still counts as income; a positive with no/unknown category stays income (ambiguous inflow not netted); a month's spend floors at 0. Backward-compatible — callers that don't pass `categoryId` are unchanged (positives stay income). 4 known-answer fixture tests; the demo provider returns `categoryId` so it's active in-app | The savings-rate/FI numbers should reflect what you actually spent, not gross-spend-plus-phantom-income when you return something. Implemented at the ONE shared classifier (`incomeExcludingTransfers` is test-only, so no parallel path to drift). Scoped/cheap because it conflicts with NO pinned golden value (the seed's FI/savings cents aren't pinned to exact values anywhere — only the cash-needed seed values are), so the engine-wide change needed no golden recompute. Reviewed by a focused self-check rather than a full multi-agent critic, proportionate to a 6-line single-path change with known-answer tests + a green suite |
| 34 | R5 | Recurring detection is refund-robust (ROADMAP #4 tail, fixes STATUS #7's "refund+rebill drops the series"): `detectRecurring` now analyzes only the DOMINANT sign within each merchant group — a stray opposite-sign txn (a refund inside an expense subscription) is a one-off, excluded from cadence/amount analysis. Pure-signed groups (every seed series + payroll) are unchanged (the minority list is empty), so no seed-detected series shifts. Fixes two real defects the existing critic2-recurring probes had pinned as ACTUAL behavior: (a) a refund made amounts non-plateaued (firstNewIdx=0) → the whole subscription was dropped; (b) a trailing refund flipped the series to "income" with an inverted plateau. Those two probes now assert the survived behavior. 2 new known-answer fixture tests (refund excluded → series kept; <3 same-signed charges → no fabricated series) | Pairs with refund-netting (#33): a refund shouldn't both vanish from spend AND destroy the subscription/price-increase insight. The dominant-sign rule is the minimal correct model (a recurring series is same-signed charges; refunds are one-offs) and is provably seed-safe (only mixed-sign merchant groups change, and the seed's lone refund is on Amazon, which is variable spend and never a detected series). Focused self-review + green suite, proportionate to the contained engine change |
| 35 | R5 | Postgres deploy path (ROADMAP, "engine before UI" extended to infra): the app runs on **SQLite locally + in every test** (zero credentials, `file:./dev.db`) and on **Postgres in production**, chosen at runtime by connection-string scheme in `src/lib/db-adapter.ts` (`isPostgresUrl` → `PrismaPg` else `PrismaBetterSqlite3`). ONE canonical schema (`prisma/schema.prisma`, provider sqlite); `scripts/gen-pg-schema.mjs` deterministically derives `prisma/.generated.postgres.prisma` (pure `provider` swap — the schema is already PG-portable: Int cents, String dates, no enum/Json/Decimal) so there is no second hand-maintained schema to drift. `prisma.config.ts` `schema` is env-driven (`PRISMA_SCHEMA ?? canonical`); `vercel.json` `buildCommand` = derive → `prisma generate` → `prisma db push` → `next build`. Drivers kept out of the server bundle via `serverExternalPackages`. Deployed DB = a dedicated `pulse` database on the user's existing Neon instance (separate from VitaForge's `neondb`, created with `CREATE DATABASE`; VitaForge untouched). Runtime URL is the Neon **direct (non-pooled) endpoint**, not the `-pooler` one. Required Vercel env: `DATABASE_URL` (pulse direct), `AUTH_SECRET`. Pure unit test on the scheme detector + adapter type (`tests/unit/db-adapter.test.ts`); the Postgres path was provisioned + seeded for real (842 txns, 9 accounts, 11 series) as proof, not assumed | A stateful app cannot run on Vercel's read-only/ephemeral serverless FS with a bundled SQLite file — the deferred "needs Postgres" step. Adapter-by-URL keeps the demo-mode invariant (local/tests stay credential-free SQLite) while production gets real Postgres, with the generated client's provider matched to the runtime adapter (Prisma validates this). The **direct endpoint** is deliberate: Neon's pooled endpoint is PgBouncer in transaction mode, which breaks node-postgres prepared statements (`@prisma/adapter-pg`); for a low-traffic demo the direct endpoint's lower connection ceiling is a non-issue, and switching to `@prisma/adapter-neon` (WebSocket pooling) is the documented path if scale ever needs the pooler. Reusing VitaForge's `neondb` was rejected outright — overlapping table names (`User`) mean `prisma db push` would alter/clobber the other project; a separate database is free and fully isolated |
| 36 | R5 | Inline recategorization on the transaction register (user request). The pipeline auto-files transactions and routes only low-confidence ones (`needsReview`) to the triage inbox — so a CONFIDENT-but-wrong guess was uncorrectable from the register (read-only category). Now every register row's category is a tappable editor (`category-editor.tsx`): pick a new category, then **"Just this once"** (single reversible Correction, no rule) or **"Always · all <merchant>"** (re-files EVERY transaction of that merchant — already-filed ones included — and creates a durable priority-100 CategorizationRule, so past + future are fixed in one action). Reuses the existing triage machinery: single path calls `applyCategory`; merchant path mirrors `applyToAllSimilar` + rule creation. Generalized `similarTransactionsWhere` with `onlyNeedsReview` (default true → triage unchanged; register passes false to reach already-filed rows) — ONE shared scope so the two surfaces can't drift (#23). "Always" is gated to real merchants with a known merchantId (never Zelle/checks aggregates). Pure helpers `ASSIGNABLE_CATEGORIES` + `isRuleEligibleMerchant` (`engine/categorize/assign.ts`) with known-answer tests; the destructive action is proven by an integration test driving the REAL action against throwaway users (scope 'one' = single + no rule; scope 'merchant' = all 3 incl. needsReview=false + priority-100 rule + ownership isolation); e2e recategorizes an isolated manual row (golden-safe per #24). This is the user-facing half of "AI learns categorization": corrections→rules is the existing supervised loop; surfacing it on the register is what makes confident misclassifications fixable. LLM-assist + accuracy/Brier surfacing (options B/C) deferred — not built | The register being read-only for categories meant the single most important correction path (fixing a wrong high-confidence guess) had no UI; triage only ever shows the <5% the pipeline already doubts. Reusing `applyCategory`/`applyToAllSimilar` (not a parallel mutation path) keeps the correction/rule/undo invariants intact — the same Correction rows, the same priority-100 rules the engine already honors, all reversible from triage. Engine-first per CLAUDE.md: the risky parts (assignable set, rule-eligibility, the generalized scope) are unit/integration-tested in isolation; the UI is thin wiring. Scoped to A (toggle only) per the user's explicit choice |
| 37 | R5 | Categorization accuracy + calibration metric (user request, option B; CLAUDE.md §adaptive-runtime "log every prediction, track Brier, surface in UI"). New `CategoryPrediction` table logs one row per transaction — predictedCategoryId + confidenceBps, plus actualCategoryId (null until ground truth known). Pure engine `engine/accuracy/score.ts`: `scorePredictions` → {n, correct, accuracyBps, brierMilli}; Brier = mean((confidence/10000 − hit)²)×1000, so it scores CALIBRATION (a confident miss is punished harder than an unsure one), not just hit-rate. Known-answer tested (hand-verified 2/3 correct → Brier 0.300). Seeded: every transaction gets a prediction; actual is set only where genuinely known (a known merchant's canonical category), so the demo shows an honest measured number (~high, since known-merchant hits score well and low-confidence review items count as not-yet-correct with low confidence = good calibration); unknown/ambiguous rows stay unlabeled until reviewed. Ground truth accrues live: applyCategory / applyToAllSimilar / recategorize set actualCategoryId when the user confirms or corrects. Surfaced as an AccuracyCard on /triage (accuracy % + Brier + N, or a "no data yet" state). Integration-tested (recategorize labels the prediction; getCategorizationAccuracy reflects it) + e2e (card shows a %). Predictions cascade-delete with the user. NOT logged for manual/CSV ingest (user-/import-dictated categories aren't model predictions) — documented gap, those rows just aren't scored | "Improves over time" must be measurable, not asserted — this is the instrument. A separate log table (not derived from Corrections) avoids the conflation where a triage-accept and an AI-miss both look like "corrections"; predicted/confidence are frozen at predict-time and actual arrives later, which is exactly what a Brier score needs. Seeding actual only for known merchants keeps it honest (no fabricated ground truth for genuinely ambiguous rows). Brier×1000 as an int keeps the money/no-float discipline at the display boundary; the metric itself is a ratio so fractions are inherent and formatted at the UI |
| 38 | R5 | LLM-assisted categorization behind a tested no-key fallback (user request, option C). For UNKNOWN merchants the deterministic pipeline routes to review; an optional LLM can propose a category instead. Pure pieces (`engine/categorize/llm.ts`): `parseLlmCategory` validates the model's JSON against the known category set (our schema — unknown id / bad confidence / non-object → null), `buildCategorizePrompt`, and `pickAssistedCategory` — the LLM may override ONLY when the deterministic result was unsure (review/uncategorized) AND the model is confident (≥ AUTO_FLAGGED 7000bps); a confident deterministic call (known merchant, user rule) is never overridden. Server `suggestCategoryViaLLM` (Anthropic Messages API, model via env, default haiku): no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` → returns null WITHOUT a network call; any network/parse/validation failure → null; never throws. Wired into manual entry (when the user didn't dictate a category). Fully no-key-testable: pure parse/prompt/decision unit-tested; server no-key path asserts null + no fetch, plus mocked-fetch error→null and a well-formed response→parsed. The LLM only ever SUGGESTS — it flows through the same confidence routing + the user's confirmation→rule loop (and the accuracy metric #37 scores it), so it can't silently mislabel | The demo-mode invariant (runs with zero credentials) is preserved exactly — with no key, behavior is byte-identical to before. The risk in an LLM categorizer is a hallucinated/garbage category reaching the ledger; gating on schema validation + "only assist when deterministic is unsure AND model is confident" + routing through review means a bad suggestion is caught, not trusted. Manual entry is the wired surface (single-row, cheap); CSV/seed deliberately stay deterministic (seed must be reproducible; bulk LLM calls are a cost concern) — documented |
| 39 | R5 | Manual net-worth items — assets & liabilities a bank feed can't see (user request: "housing, assets… net worth calculator like Mint"). Users add a home/vehicle/cash/other ASSET and a mortgage/other LIABILITY, stored as Account rows with provider 'manual' and new `type`s (REAL_ESTATE/VEHICLE/CASH/OTHER_ASSET/MORTGAGE/OTHER_LIABILITY) — no schema change (Account.type is a free String). They flow into the EXISTING net worth automatically. Two net-worth classifiers existed (groupAccounts for /accounts, the inline one in cash-needed/assemble.ts for the dashboard golden) — unified through one `isLiabilityType` (query.ts), extended with MORTGAGE + OTHER_LIABILITY. The new types are absent from the seed, so the golden net worth ($144,804.74) is unchanged (verified). Pure engine `engine/networth/manual.ts` (type catalog + parseManualAccount/parseManualValueCents, all-errors-at-once, positive cents, $1B ceiling) with known-answer tests incl. a groupAccounts net-worth check. Server actions (add/update/delete) are ownership-scoped AND guarded to provider='manual' — a LINKED (seed/Plaid) account can NEVER be edited/deleted through them (integration-tested against throwaway users, incl. the net-worth math and the linked-account guard). UI: /accounts is now interactive — manual rows are inline value-editable + deletable, "Add asset/liability" forms; linked rows still link to transactions. e2e adds a $100k home (net worth $144,804.74 → $244,804.74), then deletes it (revert, golden-safe via Playwright retries). The brokerage balance was ALREADY in net worth (INVESTMENT account, asset); this completes the picture with non-bank items | A net worth "like Mint" needs the things Plaid can't return — home value, vehicles, private debts. Modeling them as manual Account rows reuses the entire net-worth/grouping machinery (one source of truth) instead of a parallel table, and the provider flag cleanly separates "you maintain this value" from "the institution does" (the #24 balance-authority rule still holds for linked accounts). Unifying the two net-worth classifiers removes a latent drift bug (the dashboard would have ignored manual liabilities). Net-worth-over-time/trend deferred (needs dated snapshots) — offered as a follow-up |
| 40 | R5 | Net worth over time on /accounts + one shared series engine (user request). A trend already existed on the dashboard but was computed inline in finance.ts with a THIRD hardcoded liability set (['CREDIT','LOAN']) — blind to manual MORTGAGE/OTHER_LIABILITY. Extracted `engine/networth/series.ts` `netWorthSeries({snapshots, accounts, today})`: month-end net worth via the single `isLiabilityType`, the live "today" point computed from current balances over ALL accounts (manual items included, so the latest point == headline), future-dated snapshots dropped, snapshots for unknown accounts ignored. Known-answer tested. finance.ts (dashboard) now calls it (kills the drift bug); getAccountsView returns the same series; /accounts renders an area-chart trend card (Recharts) with a "vs last month-end" delta, keeping the accounts-net-worth-amount testid. e2e asserts the trend renders on /accounts (the dashboard trend stays covered by phase1/phase3). | 48 | R5 | Production hardening — split-race concurrency (#9) + durable rate limiting/throttle (#8). (#9) `splitTransaction` had a DOUBLE-SPLIT race: it read `isSplitParent` BEFORE its transaction, so two concurrent splits both passed the pre-read and each created a set of children (doubling the txn in every aggregate). Fixed by CLAIMING the parent atomically INSIDE the `prisma.$transaction` with a conditional `updateMany` (`where id, isSplitParent:false, splitParentId:null`); if it affects 0 rows the tx aborts BEFORE creating any children, so a racing loser produces no orphan children (the pre-read stays only as a fast UX fail). Regression `tests/unit/split-race.test.ts` drives two PARALLEL splits and asserts exactly ONE set of children — the critic verified it FAILS on the pre-fix code (old → fulfilled=2/children=4). (#8) the in-memory `rateLimit` is per-instance (a no-op across serverless); added a `RateLimit` table + `rateLimitDurable` wired into the export route (replacing in-memory) and a NEW per-account sign-in throttle (8/60s, checked before `signIn`). HOSTILE CRITIC (4 dimension critics + adversarial verification): the split fix scored 10/10 (proven 20/20, loser rejected by the claim every time); but it found **3 P1s in the limiter, all FIXED before sign-off**: (CONC-1/SEC-1) the first/reset branch did `upsert{count:1}` + `return true` UNCONDITIONALLY, so a CONCURRENT burst of N first-hits ALL bypassed (50/50 at limit 8) — fully defeating the brute-force throttle it exists to provide; fixed by making the decision ALWAYS read the count from an atomic increment-or-create (conditional `updateMany` resets only an EXPIRED window, then an `upsert` with `update:{count:{increment:1}}` whose returned row carries the authoritative count) so N concurrent first-hits resolve to counts 1..N and only the first `limit` pass; regression: a `Promise.all` burst of 12 at limit 4 asserts exactly 4 allowed (would be 12 on the old code). (OPS-1) the `RateLimit` table had no prune, no index, and an attacker-controlled `signin:<email>` key space → unbounded storage growth (CWE-770) reachable UNAUTHENTICATED; fixed with `@@index([resetAt])` + an exported `pruneExpiredRateLimits()` called time-bucketed (≤ once/min/instance) from the limiter, so the table self-prunes without depending on a scheduled cron. P2s also fixed: export-route 401/200/429 unit coverage (CQ-1), an `undoSplit → re-split` round-trip test (CQ-4), an honest "in-memory rateLimit now has no prod caller, retained for its regression" comment (CQ-2), explicit fail-CLOSED comments at both limiter call sites (OPS-3), and a SQLite-only-test note on the split race (CONC-3). Accepted/documented P2s: the email-keyed sign-in throttle enables a BOUNDED (≤60s) targeted-account lockout — an IP-scoped dimension is the documented next step (SEC-2); the limiter is two Prisma statements rather than a single raw `INSERT…ON CONFLICT…CASE` (OPS-2 — the portable form fixes the P1; the single-statement raw upsert is a Postgres-only latency optimization); the Always/Undo orphan-rule race stays DEFERRED (STATUS #10 — no UI path, non-financial, needs the same conditional-claim treatment on rule deletion). | These are the deferred P2s gating a real multi-user launch. The split race CORRUPTS financial aggregates (a double-counted transaction), so it is the concurrency fix worth doing now; claiming the parent inside the transaction is the minimal correct guard and works identically on SQLite's single-writer and Postgres's row lock. A rate limiter's entire job is to resist CONCURRENT bursts (that is exactly how brute-force + scraping operate), so a limiter that bypasses under concurrency is worse than none — CONC-1/SEC-1 was the most important finding of the session, and the fix is to decide from an atomically-incremented count, never a hardcoded allow. Self-pruning beats a cron the operator might never schedule, and bounds an unauthenticated storage-exhaustion vector. The email-keyed lockout is the standard account-throttle tradeoff — bounded to 60s and documented rather than crossed silently. |
| 49 | R5 | Finished the deferred hardening (ROADMAP #9 tail + the Critic SEC-2 item #48 documented-and-deferred). (A) **Always/Undo orphan-rule race** (STATUS #10): `undoCorrections` now deletes a correction's rule with a CONDITIONAL-CLAIM — `deleteMany where { id: becameRuleId, userId, createdFrom: correction.id }` — so it removes the rule ONLY while it still belongs to THIS correction's lineage. A concurrent re-apply that reassigned the rule (`createdFrom` now points elsewhere) can no longer be orphaned by the undo. Regression `tests/unit/undo-orphan-rule.test.ts`: the normal delete still works (the owning correction's `createdFrom` equals its id), and a rule whose lineage was reassigned SURVIVES the undo. (B) **Sign-in throttle redesigned to eliminate the targeted-account lockout-DoS** that #48 left open. Two durable dimensions instead of one email-keyed before-auth throttle: a PER-IP cap (`signin-ip:<ip>`, 20/60s) checked BEFORE any auth work — the brute-force volume stop, keyed on the CALLER so it can never lock a victim out of their own account; and a PER-ACCOUNT-FAIL cap (`signin-fail:<email>`, 8/60s) consumed ONLY on a failed attempt and checked AFTER signIn — so a correct password is NEVER blocked. New `src/lib/request-ip.ts` `clientIp` reads `x-forwarded-for` (first hop) via a dynamic `next/headers` import, falls back to `'unknown'`, never throws. Regression `tests/unit/rate-limit-durable.test.ts`: the 9th failed attempt is throttled; a CORRECT password succeeds even after the fail budget is spent (the no-lockout proof); the per-IP cap blocks before `signIn` is called. Adversarial review (focused security/concurrency reviewer, every claim reproduced against the code): both changes PASS, **0 P0/P1**; one accepted P2 — without `x-forwarded-for` (local dev only; Vercel always sets it) users share the `'unknown'` bucket, a documented degrade. Supersedes #48's lockout deferral. STILL deferred (genuinely low-impact, no UI path): the `alreadyUndone` pre-read in `undoCorrections` is a TOCTOU two concurrent undos of the SAME correction could both pass (a duplicate inverse-correction) — corrections are append-only AUDIT history, not a financial aggregate, so it self-heals in every sum; noted, not fixed. | The orphan fix is the conditional-claim pattern (same shape as the split fix in #48) applied to rule deletion: scope the destructive op to the lineage it owns. The throttle's lockout-DoS was the one real residual from #48 — an email-keyed before-auth throttle inherently lets an attacker who knows a victim's address lock them out. Splitting into per-IP (caller-keyed, cannot lock a victim) + per-account-fail (failures only, never blocks a correct password) resolves it while keeping a hard pre-auth volume cap. The per-IP cap is the primary brute-force stop; the per-account-fail is a secondary signal — defense-in-depth without the lockout. Tested at the action level (real `signInWithPassword` with a mocked `signIn`) rather than e2e, since a throttle e2e would need 20+ rapid sign-ins against the shared demo and risk the suite; the existing auth e2e already proves the real password path still works after the restructure. |
| 50 | R5 | Closed the last STATUS #10 concurrency item — the double-undo TOCTOU. `undoCorrections` had a pre-read (`alreadyUndone`) outside its transaction, so two concurrent undos of the SAME correction could both pass it and each write an inverse correction (a duplicate audit row). Fix: the inverse correction now carries `undoesId` (the id of the correction it undoes) with a field-level `@@unique`. The racing loser's `correction.create` violates the unique → its whole transaction rolls back (the winner already did the restore) → the P2002 is caught and skipped idempotently. The heuristic pre-read stays as a sequential fast-path. Schema change (one nullable `undoesId @unique`, applied via `prisma db push --accept-data-loss` — no real loss, the column is new and all-null; multiple NULLs are allowed so ordinary corrections are unaffected; PG-portable as a pure provider swap). Regression `tests/unit/undo-orphan-rule.test.ts`: two concurrent `undoCorrections([c])` both resolve and exactly ONE inverse (`undoesId = c.id`) is recorded. Gate: `VERIFY_E2E=1 bash scripts/verify.sh` → GREEN (703 unit / 52 files, 35 e2e). | Low-impact (append-only audit, no UI path) but cheap to close correctly, and it completes #10. A unique constraint is the right primitive — it makes idempotency a database invariant rather than relying on a read-then-write window, and it works identically on SQLite and Postgres (unlike moving the pre-read inside the transaction, which read-committed Postgres wouldn't fully serialize). Reviewed by a focused self-check + green suite, proportionate to a contained, constraint-guaranteed fix. |

| 52 | R5 | Plaid webhook JWT verification (ROADMAP #1c). The webhook handler verified NOTHING; DECISIONS #44 added an interim 404-unless-`DATA_PROVIDER=plaid` gate, but in Plaid mode any request could trigger a sync. Now `verifyPlaidWebhook` (`src/lib/plaid-webhook.ts`, via `jose`) validates the `Plaid-Verification` JWT BEFORE any DB work: ES256-only (PINNED via `algorithms:['ES256']` + `importJWK(jwk,'ES256')` — no alg-confusion / `none` downgrade), signature against Plaid's published key (resolved by the header `kid`), `request_body_sha256` === SHA-256 of the exact RAW body bytes (a tampered body fails), and `iat` freshness (anti-replay, 5-min window). It NEVER throws — a forged/replayed/malformed webhook becomes a clean 401. The key resolver is INJECTED, so the security-critical logic is fully unit-tested with a real ES256 keypair (6 cases: happy + wrong-key + tampered-body + stale + no-key + missing-token); production passes `fetchPlaidWebhookKey` (plaid.ts → /webhook_verification_key/get, caches non-expired keys by kid) which is UNVERIFIED against live Plaid (no sandbox creds here — honestly labeled). The route reads the RAW body (`request.text()`) for the hash, verifies, THEN parses; route wiring is tested (404 in demo, 401 unsigned in Plaid mode). Added `jose` (^6.2.3) as a DIRECT dependency (it was transitive via Auth.js). The 404-unless-Plaid gate stays (the key fetch needs Plaid creds anyway), so demo deploys never expose the endpoint and Plaid deploys require a valid signature. Gate: `VERIFY_E2E=1 bash scripts/verify.sh` → GREEN (715 unit / 54 files, 36 e2e). | An unauthenticated, DB-touching, sync-triggering endpoint is a real attack surface once Plaid is live; the JWT check is Plaid's recommended defense. Injecting the key resolver is what makes the logic testable WITHOUT credentials (the live fetch can't be) — so the part that matters is proven, and the unprovable network fetch is labeled UNVERIFIED, consistent with the codebase's honest Plaid stance. Pinning ES256 in BOTH `importJWK` and `jwtVerify` closes algorithm-confusion; the body-hash closes tampering; the `iat` window closes replay. Reading the raw body once (and parsing it ourselves) is required because the hash is over the exact bytes Plaid signed. |

Re-sync is idempotent + race-safe via a new `@@unique([accountId, providerRef])` atomic upsert (the seed/Plaid goldens are unaffected — providerRef is nullable so existing NULLs coexist), with a 5-day overlap window so late-posting rows aren't missed, then cross-account transfer pairing runs (parity with Plaid). HARDENING from a 4-dimension hostile-critic pass (all 5 confirmed P1s fixed + tested): SSRF guard is https-only, blocks loopback/private/link-local in IPv4 AND IPv6 (ULA fc00::/7, fe80::/10, ::ffff: mapped), re-validates EVERY redirect hop (default redirect:'follow' would let a 30x reach an internal host), and drops the Authorization header on a cross-host redirect; a malformed row is skipped not fatal; action error messages are fixed strings (never echo `e.message`, which could leak the credentialed URL); an ambiguously-named account with a NEGATIVE balance is classified as a liability so its net-worth sign can't silently invert, with a UI notice to double-check. VERIFICATION: 20 mapper/orchestration unit tests (mapper fixtures + the real connect/sync actions against a MOCKED SimpleFIN server: encrypted-at-rest, correct signs/categories, idempotent re-sync, SSRF rejection incl. an internal access URL returned BY the claim server, IPv6 internal tokens, CQ-1/3/4 regressions); full `VERIFY_E2E=1 verify.sh` GREEN (740 unit + 37 e2e + typecheck + lint + build). HONEST CAVEAT (documented, not hidden, docs/SIMPLEFIN_WALKTHROUGH.md): the LIVE network path has NEVER run against a real SimpleFIN server here (no token in env) — labeled UNVERIFIED with the exact field-shapes to confirm vs the current spec before trusting real money data; like Plaid it also waits on real multi-user auth (ROADMAP #2) before a real bank links to a deployed app | A real user kept hitting Plaid's approval/cost wall; SimpleFIN is the credential-light path that's actually reachable, and writing the ledger-corrupting parts (sign, cents, dates, dedup, account-type) as pure unit-tested functions means the risky logic is proven even though the socket can't be in this env. | 73 | R5 | Real-bank bug: a checking/card account's expenses missing from the register (user: "you're missing Zone Pest Solutions… some expenses come from checking"). Diagnosed on live data via a temporary token-guarded owner endpoint that re-fetched the SimpleFIN feed and compared inferred type + live txn count vs. what was stored. Found: "ZONE PEST SOLUTIONS INC" is on a Chase card ("E. LEE …4034") and that account plus "M. LEE …4927" (8 live txns) had ZERO stored transactions — classified correctly (CREDIT, a spending type that shows), but their history was never ingested. Cause: accounts added to the SimpleFIN aggregator AFTER the first sync only ever received the 5-day incremental window, so any transaction older than ~5 days was silently skipped forever (the first-sync 90-day pull only covered accounts present at connect time). Fix: `syncFromSimplefin` now BACKFILLS a 90-day history for any spending account first seen on an incremental sync (deferred + a second wide fetch for just the new refs), and takes an optional `fullLookbackDays` for a forced full refresh. One-time owner POST resync (365d) recovered the missed rows — added 9 (Zone Pest + the 8 on M. LEE), modified 906; verified E. LEE 0→1 and M. LEE 0→8 stored. Regression test: a new account whose only txn predates the 5-day overlap is still ingested (mock branches on start-date). Temp endpoint removed in the same commit. | The 5-day incremental window is correct for KNOWN accounts but wrong for newly-appearing ones — the asymmetry was invisible because the first sync hid it. Backfilling only the NEW refs (not re-pulling everyone every time) keeps incremental syncs cheap while never losing a new account's history. Confirmed against the user's real data rather than guessing, then proved it can't recur with a regression test. |

Anchoring on `scheduled` (not detected-recurring) avoids double-counting payroll (which is both seeded-scheduled AND detected) and works for real users since detection populates scheduled at ingest. Scoped v1 to recurring income/bills only and SAID so in the UI rather than over-claiming. Verified green: +2 e2e (43), new unit suite, axe AA intact. |

| 74 | R5 | Spending Trends / insights (surpass feature #7 — the "what changed & what to look at" lens Copilot/Cleo/Monarch lead with, which our category/recurring/forecast views never exposed). New PURE engine `engine/trends/trends.ts` is a thin, exact layer ON TOP of the tested `spendingByCategory` (ONE spend definition, integer cents, NO model calls — every threshold a constant): (a) PACE = the in-progress month projected to month end at the current daily rate (round(spentSoFar/daysElapsed×daysInMonth)) vs last month, stated as a projection not a prediction; (b) MOVERS = the last COMPLETED month vs the average of up to 3 prior completed months that have spend (no partial-month distortion, no pre-history zero-padding), surfaced only at ≥$20 AND ≥20% (new ≥$20), sorted by \|Δ\|; (c) LARGEST purchases this month; (d) NEW merchants vs the prior 6 months. Non-actionable money movement (the 'Transfers & Other' group: cash/ATM, transfer, credit-card-payment, uncategorized) is kept OUT of movers/largest/new; aggregate pseudo-merchants (Zelle/checks/ATM via `normalizeMerchant.aggregate`) out of new-merchants — but PACE alone keeps the full reports total so the headline matches /reports & /spending-plan. Server `getSpendingTrends` derives from the SAME ownership-scoped snapshot (stored category wins; merchant + aggregate from the shared normalizer, the coach pattern). `/trends` page + a dashboard `SpendingInsightsCard` (paired with Top-spending; recurring moved full-width) + a reciprocal /reports→/trends link — NO 8th nav icon (the 380px bar is full at 7, #71). 16 known-answer unit tests (hand-derived synthetic + a real-seed pinned run + an integrated normalize→engine path) + 3 e2e (discovery, render with Costco as the seed's biggest June buy, WCAG-AA axe). Gate (real output 2026-06-23): `VERIFY_E2E=1 bash scripts/verify.sh` → ✅ GREEN, **807 unit / 65 files, 46 e2e**, typecheck/lint/build clean. | The category/recurring/forecast work produced data with no "why did my spending change" lens — the single biggest "feels like a real product" gap left. Building it as a thin layer over the tested reports engine keeps ONE definition of spend (no drift) and makes every figure hand-verifiable; separating actionable insight (movers, which drop money-movement) from total money-out (pace, which keeps it) is exactly why each number is honest rather than a third spend definition. The confirmed critic P1 ("Store Card Purchase" surfaced as a new merchant) was traced to a docstring OVER-CLAIM, not a code bug: this codebase deliberately treats "Store Card Purchase" as a real, rule-eligible merchant (`assign.ts isRuleEligibleMerchant` + the triage flow assert `rule-always` for it), so flagging it aggregate would have broken that tested decision — fixed by correcting the doc + adding an INTEGRATED test (derives the aggregate flag via `normalizeMerchant` like the server, proves genuine aggregates Zelle/Check are excluded while Store Card legitimately appears). Cheap P2s fixed: deterministic largest tie-break (amount→date→merchant), no-history vs steady empty-state copy, pace `h2` for heading order, reciprocal /reports link, new-merchant amount doc clarified. Accepted P2s (documented in STATUS): pace counts money-movement to match /reports; largest excludes uncategorized to avoid Unknown-Merchant noise; refunds not netted for the new-merchant total; day-1/2 pace is volatile but caveated; baseline averages over months-with-any-spend (a true monthly average). Discovery via a dashboard card (not an 8th icon) follows the recurring precedent and protects the mobile-nav regression. |
| 75 | R5 | Ask Aimplifi — grounded natural-language assistant (surpass feature #8; the conversational surface the app is literally named for, which none of #1–#7 provided). Cardinal design: the LLM NEVER originates a fact. A PURE rule-based parser (engine/assistant/intent.ts) maps a question to a typed intent with NO model call (LOOP rule #5) — timeframe + category/group resolution over the real taxonomy + a hand-written validation gate (the parseLlmCategory convention, no new zod dep). The server answers each intent from the SAME tested engines/read-paths the dedicated views use — spendingByCategory (== /reports), getSpendingPlan, getCashNeeded, getRecurring, getCashFlowForecast, monthlyFlows, netWorthCents, getCoachData — so numbers can't be invented or drift. Pure formatters render via formatCents only. The LLM (server/assistant-llm.ts + engine/assistant/llm.ts) is an OPTIONAL fallback that only CLASSIFIES a genuinely-unknown question into a KIND (provider-agnostic xAI then Anthropic then null; 7s AbortController timeout; per-user rateLimitDurable budget; can abstain via "none"); every parameter is re-derived deterministically + re-validated before any data is read, and the answer carries interpreted:true so the inference is never silent. Zero-key demo fully functional. Discovery via a dashboard AskAimplifiCard + /ask page, NO 8th nav icon (#71/#74). Intents: net_worth, account_balance, spend_total/by_category/top_categories, largest_purchases, income, safe_to_spend, cash_needed, subscriptions, forecast, savings_rate, unknown. Gate (real 2026-06-24): VERIFY_E2E=1 bash scripts/verify.sh → GREEN, 900 unit/70 files (+93: parser routing, formatter known-answers, seed grounding incl. net worth $144,804.74 / Costco $158.44 / May income $7,400, LLM routing + no-key no-network, F3 refund-netting), 51 e2e (+5: discovery, grounded answers, suggestion chip, off-topic resilience via the 7s-timeout fallback, WCAG-AA axe), typecheck/lint/build clean. Hostile critic (2 cycles, 16 agents): cycle 1 (fin7/sec8/code6/UX8) found 6 P1s ALL FIXED + regression-locked — truncated liability set to canonical isLiabilityType; income/savings dropped categoryId+isSplitParent to full rows + getCoachData delegation; largest missing POSTED filter; off-topic misroute to "none" abstention + rate limit + interpreted flag. Confirmation cycle (93/95/88/88) confirmed all 6 and found 1 more P1 (largest diverged from /trends on the date-<=-today guard + locale-vs-codepoint tie-break) to FIXED, mirroring computeLargest exactly + a non-tautological test. | The app is named Aimplifi yet had no AI surface; an assistant that fabricates a single number would betray the project, so the only safe design is deterministic routing into already-tested engines with the LLM confined to understanding the question, never answering it. Reusing the exact view read-paths (not re-deriving) makes every figure hand-verifiable and guarantees /ask agrees with /reports, /coach, /trends. The hostile critic earned its keep: it caught four real drift bugs (liability set, refund-netting, POSTED, /trends parity) that the seed-only tests masked — all fixed with tests that exercise the divergent paths on categorized/POSTED/future-dated data. Accepted/deferred (documented): a shared toFlowTxns/isPurchaseRow/MONTH_TITLE extraction across coach/trends/assistant is a future DRY refactor (kept surgical for now); monthlyFlows' income rule (a positive counts as income only for categoryId null or "income", else it nets) is pre-existing and unchanged. |
| 76 | R5 | SimpleFIN connect test flake hardened — the parallel unit suite (71 files) shares ONE SQLite dev.db across worker processes; in the default rollback journal a writer is starved by concurrent readers past the 15s busy_timeout → SQLITE_BUSY, which connectSimplefin's intentional credential-safe catch masks as added:0 (surfacing as the flaky "expected 0 to be 2"). Fix is TEST-ONLY (production is Postgres, DECISIONS #35, untouched): a vitest globalSetup (tests/setup/wal-global-setup.ts) switches dev.db to WAL (persistent on the file, so every worker inherits it) — WAL lets concurrent readers and a single writer proceed without blocking; the SimpleFIN test now also asserts r.error is undefined so a swallowed sync throw fails legibly, not as a wrong count; a retry-bounded regression test (tests/unit/db-wal.test.ts) locks WAL on. Mechanism proven fail-before/pass-after (rollback write → SQLITE_BUSY; WAL write succeeds). Independent hostile Checker: 0 P0 / 0 P1 / 4 P2 (accepted/documented). Gate (real 2026-06-24): bash scripts/verify.sh → GREEN, 901 unit / 71 files, typecheck/lint/build clean; e2e 51 passed; 10/10 consecutive full-suite runs clean. | The first post-restart verify failed once because the codegraph daemon was re-indexing and the I/O spike tipped a latent, DOCUMENTED contention (the single-file parallel suite) past busy_timeout; the code was never wrong (proven by 23+ clean reruns). WAL attacks the root cause (reader-blocks-writer) at the test layer without touching the sacred Postgres data path; the fail-loud assertion converts the masking into a legible signal. Accepted P2s (STATUS): the regression test can't catch an unwired globalSetup on a stale-WAL dev machine (fresh/CI is covered); e2e setup doesn't separately enforce WAL (low contention, inherits the persistent file); OneDrive can still hold a transient OS lock that WAL can't prevent — a future blip there is not a WAL regression. Deferred bigger fix: move the test DB out of the OneDrive-synced tree (e.g. %TEMP%). |
| 77 | R5 | Investments engine — pure portfolio valuation + performance math (src/lib/engine/investments/portfolio.ts), the FIRST increment closing the one dimension an honest competitive review scored a clear loss to Simplifi (and the app's own declared gap): holdings → market value, cost basis, unrealized gain, allocation weights; time-weighted return (TWR — geometric link of sub-period HPRs, removes cash-flow timing); money-weighted return (XIRR — Newton + full-domain bracketing bisection). Pure/deterministic, integer cents, round-half-away-from-zero once per materialized value; returns are unitless decimals formatted to % only at the UI. 20 known-answer unit tests (hand-derived: $1,500 MV/+25%, 2.5sh×$33.33→$83.33 single-round, weights sum to 1, TWR +10%/+10%→21% and +10%/−50%→−45%, XIRR 10%/1yr and 2yr and −10% loss and the multi-flow NPV-zero property). Independent hostile critic: 2 P0 + 4 P1 found and FIXED + regression-locked — XIRR silently returned null on deep losses (root in (−1,−0.9999)) and could miss roots past the ceiling / between coarse 0.01 steps → rewritten to a residual-checked Newton + bracketing bisection over the whole (−1,∞) domain (deep loss invest $1,000 / recover $1 → −99.9% now resolves); Newton accepted on step not residual → now requires |NPV|≈0; missing safe-integer guard in valuePosition → added located throw (parity with money.ts); undefended flow ordering → defensive date sort. Docstrings narrowed to the honest conventional-flow scope (no over-claimed multi-root robustness; never returns a non-root). Gate (real 2026-06-24): bash scripts/verify.sh → GREEN, 921 unit/72 files, typecheck/lint/build clean. ENGINE-FIRST per CLAUDE.md §5: data model (Holding) + manual entry + /investments view + seed are the next increment. | Investments was the highest-leverage gap from the scorecard, and its core is exactly the pure, known-answer-testable math this project's discipline targets; building the engine first (no UI/persistence) kept it deterministic and let the hostile critic harden the XIRR solver before any data depended on it. The critic caught real over-claims in sacred financial math — the fixes both correct the solver and make the docstrings honest about scope. |

| 90 | R5 | iOS safe-area inset for the fixed mobile bottom tab bar (closes the item #86 reverted after 3 e2e failures). FIRST measured the assumption: a throwaway Playwright probe (Pixel 5 + 380px, the e2e device) showed `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` resolves to **0px** in the emulator (CSS.supports=true, the 77px fallback did not apply) — so #86's "nonzero emulator inset grew the bar" diagnosis was WRONG; valid safe-area CSS is INERT in the e2e and only grows on a notched device. Implemented with HAND-WRITTEN author CSS in globals.css (NOT Tailwind arbitrary `pb-[calc(...)]` values — the likely source of the earlier invalid-CSS breakage, since `calc()` requires spaces Tailwind must encode as underscores): `.pb-safe-bottom { padding-bottom: env(safe-area-inset-bottom) }` on the fixed bottom `<nav>` so its tappable row clears the home indicator while its bg still fills to the screen edge; `.pb-bottom-nav { padding-bottom: calc(5rem + env(...)) ; @media(min-width:640px){3rem} }` replacing the (app) layout outer div's `pb-20 sm:pb-12` so content clears the now-taller bar; `html { scroll-padding-bottom }` bumped to `calc(5rem + env(...))` so Playwright/scroll-into-view keeps targets above the bar; and `viewportFit: "cover"` added to the root viewport export so env() is nonzero on real iOS. Every env() term is 0 off iOS → the computed padding is byte-identical to the previous values in the emulator and on desktop. Gate (real 2026-06-24): VERIFY_E2E=1 bash scripts/verify.sh → GREEN in one pass, 931 unit/73 files, 53 e2e (incl. the dashboard bottom-nav clicks and the triage bottom-anchored action/swipe flows that failed before), typecheck/lint/build clean. A transient OneDrive/SQLite unit flake (2 tests) hit one interim run and cleared on a clean rerun (931/931) before the green single-pass verify. | The home-indicator overlap is a real defect on notched iPhones (standalone/PWA) that the emulator categorically cannot reproduce — which is why the prior attempts kept breaking the e2e for the wrong reason and were rightly reverted over shipping red. Measuring the inset first (0px) proved the correct fix is a no-op in tests, so the discipline was to (a) write CSS GUARANTEED valid (author rules, not Tailwind arbitrary-value compilation, which silently emits nothing on a malformed token) and (b) couple the bar height and the content clearance to the SAME env() expression so they can never drift. Invisible on every platform the tests run on, correct on the one they can't — only-change-if-markedly-better applied to a platform-specific polish gap. |
| 91 | R5 | Auto-sync SimpleFIN on page load (reported bug: real transactions "not populating / missing a bunch"). ROOT CAUSE (diagnosed, not assumed): the ONLY path that refreshed real bank data was the manual `syncSimplefinNow()` action (the "Sync now" button) — there was NO automation. `vercel.json` has no `crons` block and git history shows it never did, so `/api/cron/sync` was never scheduled (prod runtime logs: zero hits to it in 7d); and that route syncs via `getProvider()` (demo/Plaid), not `syncFromSimplefin`, so even if scheduled it wouldn't touch SimpleFIN. So nothing pulled new transactions unless the user clicked Sync now. FIX (per the owner's explicit "sync needs to be automatic on every refresh"): a new client `<AutoSync enabled>` mounted once in the (app) layout — on a full page load/refresh (NOT soft client navigations; the layout doesn't remount on those) it calls the real `syncSimplefinNow()` in the background (never blocks render) and `router.refresh()`es the server data only when new rows were ingested. The layout gates it with a cheap indexed `simpleFinConnection` lookup so demo/manual-only users never fire a sync. A 10s `sessionStorage` throttle coalesces rapid reloads / multiple tabs so refresh-spam can't trip the SimpleFIN bridge's rate-limit (which would break syncing — the opposite of the goal). Reverted a half-started Vercel-cron approach (rejected: needs CRON_SECRET + a plan-dependent schedule and only refreshes while the app is CLOSED — on-load was what was asked and has no env/plan deps). Gate (real 2026-06-24): VERIFY_E2E=1 bash scripts/verify.sh → GREEN in one pass, 931 unit/73 files, 53 e2e (demo user has no connection → AutoSync no-op, suite unaffected), typecheck/lint/build clean. HONEST LIMITS: the on-load incremental sync uses start = lastSyncedAt − 5d, so it auto-recovers everything since the last successful sync, but transactions older than each account's original 90-day backfill window are NOT recovered by this (a forced full backfill is a separate follow-up); and the live SimpleFIN network path stays UNVERIFIED in this sandbox — the sync LOGIC is covered by existing tests and the on-load wiring by build + the no-op demo e2e, but whether it pulls the owner's specific missing rows needs the owner to load the site and confirm. | The real defect was structural (manual-only sync), so the right fix makes a sync happen automatically exactly when the user is looking at the app. Client-triggered + non-blocking keeps the page fast (renders current data, then updates on change); refresh-only-on-change avoids needless re-renders; the throttle protects the bank link so the automation can't rate-limit itself into failure. Chose on-load over cron per the explicit request and because it needs no secret/plan and delivers freshness precisely when it's seen. |

| 95 | R5 | Coach Principles — Wave 3 debt payoff ENGINE (C9 · Ramsey; Conflict A). New pure module `src/lib/engine/debt/payoff.ts` `planDebtPayoff(input): DebtPayoffResult` — fixed-installment amortization with snowball (smallest balance first) and avalanche (highest APR first, the recommended default) ordering, an extra-$/mo applied to the focus debt, per-debt + overall debt-free month, first-payoff month (the snowball motivational signal), total interest/paid. Monthly periodic interest = round(balance×aprBps/10000/12) via roundHalfAwayFromZero; total monthly budget (Σ minimums + extra) held CONSTANT so a paid-off debt's minimum rolls into the focus debt (the snowball/avalanche mechanic). DELIBERATELY SEPARATE from the Cash-Needed engine, which the map proved is CREDIT-revolving-only and silently drops LOANs (assemble.ts filters type==='CREDIT'); fixed-payment amortization is genuinely different math, so this duplicates nothing — it shares only the integer-cents + roundHalfAwayFromZero conventions. Known-answer tests (tests/unit/payoff.test.ts, 6 cases) pinned to a hand-worked table in docs/EDGE_CASES.md §Debt-payoff: single $300@12%/$100/mo loan → 4 months / $6.14 interest; 0% loan = pure principal division; extra accelerates; snowball clears the small debt earlier (firstPayoffMonth) while avalanche never costs more interest. MAKER/CHECKER catch (regression ledger 2026-06-25): the negative-amortization case (minimum below interest) compounded the balance past Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER before the 1200-month cap and threw inside roundHalfAwayFromZero; fixed with a constant-budget divergence guard — if a month yields no net reduction in total balance it never will, so break and return monthsToDebtFree:null. Engine only this commit (no schema/UI yet — M5/M6 follow). Gate (real 2026-06-25): bash scripts/verify.sh → ✅ VERIFY GREEN, 983 unit/76 files, typecheck/lint/build clean. | Conflict A (snowball vs avalanche) is resolved as a user choice with avalanche as the default (least real interest, honest-math ethos) while the engine still surfaces the first-payoff date so the snowball's motivational signal isn't lost — both outcomes computable side by side. Separating from Cash-Needed honors "don't duplicate, but don't shoehorn" — revolving min-payment vs fixed installment are different models. Pinning to a hand-verified amortization table (not the engine's own output) keeps the math honest; the divergence guard makes an unpayable plan fail loudly as null rather than crash. |

| 101 | R6 | Plaid integration hardening pass (pre-sandbox, off the 6-agent audit: 1 P0, 8 P1). The integration was real code but had never run against a live Plaid — these fixes make the FIRST run correct + diagnosable rather than silently wrong. **P0 — Link product gating:** `createLinkToken` put `liabilities` in the required `products` array, which (per Plaid's initializing-products guidance) filters every depository-only institution out of Link entirely → moved to `required_if_supported_products: ['transactions']`+`['liabilities']`, so banks without liabilities still link and `/liabilities/get` still runs where supported. **P1s:** (a) `balances.current` was abs()'d via `plaidDollarsToPositiveCents`, inverting net worth for an overpaid card / overdrawn account → new pure `plaidSignedDollarsToCents` (sign-preserving, -0→0) used for `current` only; available/limit stay non-negative; safe because the net-worth engine applies the liability sign by `type` (series.ts) and the cash-needed engine floors at zero (engine.ts:70-114). (b) Failed Plaid calls threw only the HTTP status → exported pure `plaidErrorSummary` surfaces Plaid's RESPONSE error envelope (error_code/type/message/request_id — none secret; the token-bearing REQUEST body is never logged). (c) `exchangePublicToken` did `.create` → `.upsert` by itemId so a link that throws AFTER token storage stays retryable instead of hitting the unique constraint. (d) `syncTransactions` had no per-item isolation → each item wrapped in try/catch, a failing item (e.g. `ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED`) audited `plaid.item.sync.failed` with cursor left unadvanced, the rest still sync. (e) An account added to an item AFTER link was a permanent ledger gap (cursor advanced past its txns while it was unknown) → `/transactions/sync`'s echoed `accounts[]` are upserted mid-loop via new private `upsertPlaidAccounts`/`plaidAccountIdMap`; that upsert skips+audits an unmappable account (`plaid.account.skipped`) instead of aborting the whole item. (f) Expired webhook keys (`expired_at` is a Unix-timestamp number, not string) are now rejected, not cached; webhook body-hash compare made constant-time (`timingSafeEqual`). (g) Dead `development` host removed from `plaid.ts` + the validate script; `PLAID_ENV` now fails loud on a typo'd/stale value. Validate script (`npm run plaid:validate`): `/sandbox/public_token/create` initializes `['transactions','liabilities']`, drops the invalid `options.transactions.days_requested`, refuses any non-sandbox `PLAID_ENV`, retries sync 12×/2.5s, FAILS if a credit account yields 0 statements, stops swallowing the liabilities error, and warns loudly if `/item/remove` leaves an item leaked. PLAID_WALKTHROUGH.md corrected: the webhook JWT verification + recurring/scheduled refresh it listed PENDING are in fact wired (route calls `verifyPlaidWebhook`+`fetchPlaidWebhookKey`; `syncTransactions` calls `refreshRecurringForUser`); known limits stated (only credit-card liabilities mapped — student/mortgage not; paid-in-full null due date → estimate path). Gate (real 2026-06-25): core `bash scripts/verify.sh` → **VERIFY GREEN** (typecheck/lint clean, build clean); Plaid unit suites `plaid-map`+`plaid-errors`+`plaid-webhook` **33/33 pass** (new: 5 signed-cents + 1 negative-current mapping + 3 error-formatter cases). Still UNVERIFIED: all live network orchestration — only the owner running `npm run plaid:validate` with free sandbox keys flips that. | An integration that has never run is a liability dressed as a feature. The audit's job was to make the first contact with real Plaid data correct-by-construction: the P0 alone would have made Link unusable for any depository-only bank, and the signed-balance bug would have quietly mis-stated net worth for the exact edge (overpaid card) a careful user notices first. Everything here is either pure-and-tested or fault-isolated-and-audited; the genuinely unprovable part (live network) is gated behind a single owner-run script that now asserts hard instead of printing green on an empty result. |
| 102 | R6 | Plaid Link UI robustness pass (off a 6-agent adversarial audit of the never-executed browser Link flow; the wiring mapped clean but two P1s would bite a real session). **P1a — a successful link could report failure.** `linkPlaidAccount` ran exchange + syncTransactions + syncLiabilities in ONE try, so once #101 moved liabilities to `required_if_supported_products` (letting depository-only banks link), those banks initialize WITHOUT liabilities and `/liabilities/get` throws PRODUCTS_NOT_SUPPORTED — flipping an already-successful link (item persisted, accounts synced) to `{ok:false}`, skipping `revalidatePath`, so the client showed an error and never refreshed and the just-linked accounts stayed invisible. Fix: the EXCHANGE alone gates success; syncTransactions/syncLiabilities are now each best-effort (own try/catch), revalidate always runs, `{ok:true,added}` returns once exchange resolves. Also hardened `PlaidProvider.syncLiabilities` itself with per-item try/catch + `plaid.liabilities.failed` audit (mirrors the #101 syncTransactions per-item isolation) so the cron/webhook path is protected too, not just the link action. **P1b — silent data loss on a rejected action.** The client had no rejection handling and the actions threw (not `{ok:false}`) on the unauth path (requireUserId sat outside the try): in `onSuccess`, a rejected exchange left the button stuck on "Connecting…" AND silently discarded the public_token Plaid had just handed us (linked at Plaid, nothing persisted). Fix: requireUserId moved inside the try in both actions (always resolve the documented contract); client `start()` wrapped in try/finally (busy always clears, error always surfaces); `onSuccess` exchange call given a `.catch`; `onExit` now surfaces a real Link error (`display_message`/`error_message`) while a clean user-cancel (err=null) stays quiet (was swallowing both args). Regression lock: new tests/unit/plaid-link-besteffort.test.ts (4 cases, provider mocked) proves liabilities/transaction sync failures still link ok + revalidate, a lagging sandbox links with added:0, and a failed EXCHANGE is still a real ok:false with no revalidate. Corrected a wrong instruction I'd given the owner: DATA_PROVIDER does NOT need to be `plaid` to test Link — both actions use `new PlaidProvider()` directly (not getProvider()), gated only by plaidConfigured(); leave DATA_PROVIDER=demo. Honest scope: the cdn.plaid.com hosted iframe still needs a HUMAN click-through (not a maintainable Playwright target); only that final step is unproven. Gate (real 2026-06-25): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → **VERIFY GREEN** (typecheck/lint clean, **1052 unit / 81 files**, build clean). | The audit's value was catching that #101's own correctness fix (depository banks can link) created a NEW failure mode one layer up — exactly the kind of interaction a single-pass review misses. Best-effort post-exchange syncs encode the real invariant: once Plaid hands back an item, the link succeeded; transaction/liability lag or absence is normal and must never present as failure or, worse, silently drop a just-linked account. |
| 103 | R6 | Dev-mode CSP fix — the Plaid Link button (and ALL client interactivity) was dead under `npm run dev`. Root cause found by driving headless Chromium (Playwright) against the running dev server: the page threw `EvalError: ... 'unsafe-eval' is not an allowed source` — next.config.ts's CSP `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://cdn.plaid.com` omits 'unsafe-eval', but `next dev` compiles client code with eval-based source maps (eval-source-map), so the CSP blocked the ENTIRE client bundle from executing → no hydration → every button inert, the Connect handler never attached, no server-action POST ever fired. Production was unaffected (prod builds use no eval), which is why it deployed clean to aimplifi.app and went unnoticed — the Link UI had simply never been exercised in a browser. Fix: `const isDev = process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'` and emit 'unsafe-eval' in script-src ONLY in development; production CSP stays strict (no unsafe-eval), so the hardened header the privacy policy advertises is unchanged for real users. Proven by re-driving the browser after the fix: click → `POST /accounts` (createLinkToken mints `link-sandbox-…`) → Plaid Link iframe loads from `cdn.plaid.com/link/v2/stable/link.html?...token=link-sandbox-...` → Plaid sandbox `link/workflow/start` + heartbeat; zero pageerrors. Also independently confirmed the real `/link/token/create` endpoint works live (the one call `plaid:validate` never made, since it uses the sandbox public-token bypass). Gate (real 2026-06-25): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → **VERIFY GREEN** (typecheck/lint clean, 1052 unit / 81 files, build clean — prod build emits the strict CSP). | A security header that only bites in development is the worst kind of latent footgun: it passes every prod check, ships clean, and silently makes the local app look broken ("the button does nothing") with no error the user can see without DevTools. Driving the actual browser was the only way to surface it — unit tests and `next build` both pass because neither exercises the dev eval path. Scoping 'unsafe-eval' to dev keeps production's strict CSP intact while making the dev server actually usable for the manual Link test. |

The interactive Link iframe is Plaid-hosted (not browser-e2e'able — see plaid-actions.test.ts); the pure wiring is covered by tests/unit/plaid-oauth.test.ts. Gate (real 2026-06-26): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → ✅ VERIFY GREEN (typecheck/lint clean, 1061 vitest pass, next build clean incl. new `○ /plaid-oauth` route); E2E deferred. | Trial/production keys are imminent and most of the user's own banks (Chase, BofA) are OAuth; the prior `createLinkToken` sent no `redirect_uri`, so those logins would have dead-ended. Built fallback-first (dormant until the env var is set) per the constitution's "no feature blocks the keyless build" rule. |

Effect: future golf txns auto-file (conf ≥ 7000, no inbox), AND existing inbox rows for golf merchants now show "Entertainment" as the suggestion (getTriageItems recomputes categorize() live). No dedicated "Golf"/"Recreation" category exists — golf maps to Entertainment; offered to add a separate category if the user wants golf tracked as its own line. NOTE: this is a deterministic win for one merchant family; it does NOT address the ~515-row inbox backlog, whose real cause is the LLM categorizer being off in prod (XAI_API_KEY not set in Vercel) + assist running only at ingest (existing rows need a backfill). KNOWN LIMITATION (honest, follow-up): the Add/Edit transaction form, Budgets, and the Transactions FILTER dropdown still list all categories (filter intentionally so you can still filter historical data in a now-hidden category; add-form/budgets are a quick follow-up). Hidden categories also still appear in reports/trends for any transaction already filed under them — by design (you don't lose sight of past spend because you decluttered the picker). | Built-in categories can't be hard-deleted without breaking historical FKs and pinned golden values, so a per-user hide is the only safe "delete" — and it's also reversible. Shipping hide first (zero name-resolution risk) before custom-create (which needs the reports/trends engines made user-aware) keeps each step verify-green and avoids a half-built path where a custom "Golf" would render as "Uncategorized" in reports. |
| 111 | R7 | Custom (user-created) categories — architecture + foundation (R7b-1/2). User said "yes" to building real custom categories (e.g. a "Golf" line). A 20-file read-only audit workflow (21 agents) mapped EVERY category id→name/group/discretionary resolution site: the pure engines (reports.ts, trends.ts, fi/insights.ts, assistant/answer.ts+intent.ts) resolve via the static `CATEGORY_BY_ID`, so a custom id would render as "Uncategorized"/"Other" there; display files that read `transaction.category.name` from a Prisma join already work. **Decision: a per-request `Map<id,{name,group,discretionary}>` resolver = static system meta OVERLAID with the user's custom Category rows, threaded as an OPTIONAL param defaulting to `CATEGORY_BY_ID`** — so a user with no custom categories produces byte-identical engine output and the golden tests are never touched. Custom ids are cuids (never collide with system slugs), so the overlay only adds entries. This foundation commit: (1) schema — `Category` gains `userId?`, `group?`, `discretionary @default(true)`; `name @unique` → `@@unique([userId,name])` + `@@index([userId])` so two users can each own a "Golf" (resolver is built per-request from one user's customs, so no cross-user resolution); `User.customCategories`. Additive/nullable, so existing FK data + seed + golden categorization are untouched (db push clean). (2) `categories.ts`: `CategoryMeta` type (structural subset of SystemCategory, so `CATEGORY_BY_ID` IS a valid meta map), pure `mergeCategoryMeta(custom[])`, `categoryName(id, meta=CATEGORY_BY_ID)`. Cycle-2 confirmed all RESOLVED, zero P0/P1.** Key resolution: **F2 fixed BY CONSTRUCTION** — customs are restricted to SPENDING groups (`CUSTOM_CATEGORY_GROUPS` = assignable groups minus `Income` + `Transfers & Other`; group is immutable on rename), so a custom can never reach the income/refund classifier — chosen OVER threading group-based income detection into `monthlyFlows`, because that classifier is entangled with the group-`Income` `refund`/`tax-refund`/`reimbursement` categories (positive = refund-against-spend) and changing it would shift demo golden values; redefining the shared refund model is out of scope for "custom categories". F3: delete remaps the unconstrained string id columns (`Correction.from/toCategoryId`, `CategoryPrediction.predicted/actualCategoryId`) to `uncategorized` and calls `ensureCategories()` first so the FK target exists on a fresh Postgres deploy. 'Transfers & Other'` fallback in `getCustomCategories` names a now-excluded group (unreachable — create requires a valid spending group and group is immutable; `monthlyFlows` keys off id not group, so no mis-aggregation even if hit).
