| 116 | R7 | Backfill categorization — re-run the improved categorizer over the existing review pile. User asked to build a backfill (and to enable the prod LLM, handled separately as setup). Problem: the keyword/merchant layer runs only at INGEST, so transactions imported before a rule existed keep their old verdict — improvements like #115 (insurance carriers), the income/fees/Spotify keywords, golf (#109), and utility-epay never reach the backlog. Built engine-first. (1) PURE planner src/lib/engine/categorize/backfill.ts — planBackfill(rows, rules) re-runs categorize() over UNSURE rows (needsReview OR uncategorized) and returns the confident, concrete re-files, enforcing the safety rails so they're unit-testable: never touch a settled row or a split-parent container; the verdict must be confident (not needsReview / not uncategorized); and the #44 sign guard — an INFLOW is never booked into a spend category (only income/transfer are sign-appropriate). 6 unit tests. (2) Server action src/server/backfill-actions.ts backfillCategorization() — ownership-scoped (requireUserId, account.userId in every query AND in the updateMany WHERE), ensureCategories() first so re-filed subcategory ids (dental-insurance/vision-insurance) satisfy the Category FK on a fresh Postgres, assertOwnedCategory on each unique target (defense in depth), applies the plan as grouped updateMany inside one prisma.$transaction. It MIRRORS INGEST, not a user confirmation: stores the categorizer's REAL confidenceBps (so the subtle AI badge still shows) and clears needsReview, but writes NO CategoryPrediction ground truth and creates NO rule — so it cannot poison the accuracy metric (#37) or the rule loop. auditLog "categorize.backfill". 3-case real-DB integration test (Delta Dental→dental-insurance and payroll→income auto-filed; the inflow sign-guard row and an unknown descriptor stay in review; a settled row is untouched; ANOTHER user's pile is untouched; a second run is idempotent → 0 re-filed). (3) UI: a "Re-run categorizer" button (BackfillButton) in the /triage header with an honest result line (auto-filed N vs still-need-review M). Gate: bash scripts/verify.sh → ✅ VERIFY GREEN (/triage 8.21→8.89 kB). KNOWN LIMITATIONS (honest): only UNSURE rows are re-filed — a Delta Dental row that was already CONFIDENTLY filed as "dental" before #115 is NOT auto-moved, because overwriting settled categorizations could clobber user-meaningful data; the existing register "apply to all similar" (scope=merchant, #36) already re-files already-filed rows for exactly that case. Not batch-undoable (no Correction rows) — but only previously-unsure rows are touched, so nothing is lost and every row stays individually editable. Deterministic-only: the LLM second pass (assistUnsureRows + suggestCategoryViaLLM, already injectable) over the remainder is a clean follow-up; enabling XAI_API_KEY in Vercel makes the INGEST assist resolve NEW rows immediately. No Playwright e2e for the button (the vitest env is node / no DOM); the action is covered by the real-DB integration test and the button is a thin wrapper. | Categorization-quality work is wasted on a returning user if it only applies to future imports — the value is clearing the pile they already have, so a backfill is the natural complement to the rule improvements. Mirroring ingest (real confidence, no CategoryPrediction, no rule) keeps the semantics honest: the SYSTEM re-categorized, the user did not confirm, so the accuracy metric and rule loop stay clean. Restricting to unsure rows makes the operation safe by construction — it can only improve a row that had no good answer, never overwrite a settled one — which is why it needs no undo. Engine-first keeps the safety rails (sign guard, confident-only, split-parent skip) in a pure, exhaustively-tested function, with the server layer reduced to an ownership-scoped batch write. |

(3) BackfillButton reports the AI split ("Auto-filed N (M with AI)"). Ran an adversarial review workflow (4 lenses then verify) BEFORE commit; it confirmed ONE P1: the write re-asserted only ownership, not the UNSURE precondition, so between the snapshot read and the write (a window widened by pass 2 LLM network calls) a row the user settled via the triage/register confirm path (applyCategory: needsReview false + confidenceBps 9900 + CategoryPrediction.actualCategoryId ground truth) would be CLOBBERED by the system guess — destroying the user confirmed categorization and contradicting its own prediction record, violating the "never a settled row" rail (enforced at read time only). FIX: compare-and-set — the updateMany WHERE now carries the SAME unsure predicate the read used (isSplitParent false + needsReview/null/uncategorized) so a concurrently-settled row no longer matches and is skipped; refiled/llmRefiled/stillUnsure are summed from the ACTUAL updateMany .count (not the stale snapshot size), and groups are keyed by (source, category, confidence) so the rule-vs-AI split stays exact. Regression test backfill-race.test.ts uses the injected LLM stub as a deterministic race hook (settles the to-be-refiled row between read and write) — fails without the predicate, passes with it. Tests: +backfill-llm (LLM resolves the tail; sign guard blocks an inflow LLM pick; a declined row stays in review) and +backfill-race; existing backfill-action test hardened with vi.stubEnv to clear provider keys so it can never hit the network. Gate: bash scripts/verify.sh → VERIFY GREEN. The other 3 review lenses (tenancy, count math, ingest-parity) found nothing. Numbering: 113-114 remain reserved for the unmerged provider-cred-checks branch. | The LLM only ever ran at ingest, so without a second pass the backfill could not touch the genuinely-unknown long tail that is the bulk of a real backlog — and reusing assistUnsureRows (already injectable + sign-guarded) made the extension small and keeps the no-key path a pure no-op. Splitting the core out of the use-server action removes the auth-mock + serializable-param friction and lets the LLM path be tested with a plain stub. The adversarial review earned its keep: the read-time unsure-only rail was real, but the WRITE could still race a user confirm — compare-and-set is the standard fix and, by counting actual writes, also keeps the reported tallies honest under contention. Using the injected LLM as the race hook turns an otherwise-untestable TOCTOU into a deterministic regression test. |

| 122 | R7 | Retirement planner — pure DECUMULATION/accumulation projection (the declared investments gap, a clear Simplifi win). The FI engine (#3) already owns accumulation-to-FI (monthsToFI/coastFI/fiNumberCents); the genuine missing lens is "I want to spend $X/yr — will my money LAST, and if not when do I run out?" New pure `src/lib/engine/investments/retirement.ts::projectRetirement`: a deterministic month-by-month two-phase sim (accumulate: grow at the GEOMETRIC monthly rate then add a level contribution; decumulate: grow then take a level withdrawal; floor at zero; first month balance ≤ 0 = depletion). REUSES `geometricMonthlyRate` (#3 — one compounding convention, not a second); rounds once/month half-away-from-zero; integer cents; no `new Date()`. Returns balanceAtRetirement, endBalance, outcome(sustained|depleted), depletionAge, a sustainable-withdrawal (SWR × balance) reference, and a yearly balance path. Ages are STATED assumptions (RETIREMENT_ASSUMPTIONS = 40→65→95). Server `getRetirementOutlook` (in server/investments.ts) delegates to `getCoachData` so portfolio/savings/spending/return/SWR are byte-identical to /coach (cannot drift); negative monthly savings floors to a $0 contribution; `hasData` gates the UI. Tests: 17 engine known-answer (0%-return cases hand-verified exact; an INDEPENDENT closed-form annuity test pins grow-then-withdraw ordering under nonzero return; growth-extends-runway property; validation throws), 3 server-glue (mapping/no-drift/floor/gate, getCoachData called with the scoped userId), 3 e2e incl. axe AA. **INFLATION (no-fabrication):** the engine's documented convention is "pass a REAL return for today's dollars"; the server now feeds `annualReturnBps = max(0, expectedReturnBps − inflationBps)` (inflationBps=250, a disclosed assumption) so the projection IS in today's dollars — a level real withdrawal against real growth, internally consistent. Two hostile-critic Checkers (engine + integration) → 0 P0; engine 0 math-defect (independently reproduced the 30yr 7%/4% = 371,408,328¢ grow-then-withdraw value) with a P1 TEST-GAP (decumulation-with-growth unpinned) FIXED; integration 2 P1 grounding FIXED — (P1-1) "in today's dollars" had fed a NOMINAL return → now real; (P1-2) currentAge=40 was undisclosed → now stated in copy. P2s fixed: engine-specific overflow message fires before cents(), planned-echo drift documented, cross-check tolerance tightened to ±$2, amber darkened to amber-700 (AA ~5:1), sub-inflation copy says "no real growth assumed" rather than an implied negative rate. Accepted P2: getRetirementOutlook reuses the heavy getCoachData to read 5 scalars (recomputes recurring/blueprint/review) — a deliberate grounding-over-micro-perf tradeoff on a non-<10s-critical page. Gate (real, measured): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → GREEN (1162 unit/96 files, typecheck/lint/build clean); investments e2e 3/3 (incl. axe). | Closes the investments gap engine-first per rule #5 without duplicating the FI accumulation engine — the decumulation/"will it last" question is the planner's distinct value. Grounding through getCoachData (the Ask-Aimplifi pattern) makes drift from /coach structurally impossible, and feeding a real return makes the "today's dollars" label honest rather than an originated inflation adjustment — the cardinal no-fabrication rule applied at the most sensitive surface (a money projection). Ages/inflation are disclosed assumptions, not facts. The independent closed-form ordering test removes the "0%-return tests can't tell grow-then-withdraw from withdraw-then-grow" blind spot the critic proved (a reversed-ordering bug was $22k off over 30yr yet passed the old suite). |
| 123 | R7 | Retirement planner — user-editable planning inputs + interactive what-if (the #122 follow-up; owner-chosen). #122 hardcoded the planning ages/inflation (RETIREMENT_ASSUMPTIONS 40/65/95/250); this makes them PERSONAL. **Schema (additive, pg-safe):** four NULLABLE `User` Int columns — `currentAge`, `retirementAge`, `endAge`, `inflationBps` — where null = "unset → use the documented default". The demo user leaves all four null, so its projection is byte-identical to #122 and NO golden value (net worth / FI / cash-needed never read these) moves; the seed is untouched. **Validation (one engine):** extended the Money Dials engine `src/lib/engine/settings/dials.ts` — added age/inflation bounds to DIAL_LIMITS (currentAge 18–100, retirementAge 18–110, endAge 19–120, inflation 0–10%), a `wholeYearsFromString` parser (1–3 digits, no sign/decimal), inflation via the exact `bpsFromPercentString` (no float), and a CROSS-FIELD ordering check that resolves each empty field to its read-path default (RETIREMENT_ASSUMPTIONS) and requires currentAge ≤ retirementAge < endAge — strictly tighter than the engine invariant, so **whatever persists is always engine-valid** (a partial-fill can't produce a read-time throw). 4 RawDials/NormalizedDials fields (empty → null). **Persist:** `updateMoneyDials` writes the four together + audit `hasRetirementPlan` + `revalidatePath('/investments')`; ownership-scoped + validated-before-write unchanged. **Read:** `getRetirementOutlook` reads the four planning fields (a `prisma.user.findUnique`, coalesced to defaults) and feeds them — alongside the still-getCoachData-grounded financial figures — through a NEW shared pure builder `buildRetirementInputs(base, planning)` + `realReturnBps(nominal, inflation)=max(0,nominal−inflation)`; financial facts still come ONLY from /coach. **UI:** Settings gains a "Retirement plan (optional)" fieldset (3 ages + inflation, blank = default, stated inline); the /investments "Retirement outlook" card became a CLIENT island (`retirement-outlook-card.tsx`) with an interactive what-if — number inputs for retire/plan-through age + inflation that recompute the SAME pure `projectRetirement` LIVE via the SAME `buildRetirementInputs` (so the explorer at saved values is byte-identical to the server by construction), a reset-to-saved, and a Settings deep link. The what-if is EXPLORATORY (client-only, never persists), so it can't perturb the shared demo/golden data; persistence lives only in Settings. The invariant-maintaining lever logic (clamp + coordinated end-age bump + exact inflation parse) is extracted to a PURE module `src/lib/engine/investments/retirement-whatif.ts` (bounds sourced from DIAL_LIMITS so explorer ⊆ savable), fuzz-tested so no edit sequence (empty/NaN/huge/negative/decimal/retire>end) can make projectRetirement throw or show a value ≠ what's projected. Tests (+38 over baseline): dials known-answer (ages/inflation/ordering/clear-to-default), engine builder (realReturnBps/buildRetirementInputs/no-drift/runway-monotonic), a server-glue case (real user's saved plan flows through; null→default), the whatif fuzz, e2e (settings round-trips the plan at EXPLICIT defaults — golden-safe — then clears; investments what-if recomputes age-65→50 live without saving + axe AA stays clean). **Hostile critic — two independent Checkers (engine math + integration/grounding):** 0 P0; 1 P1 (the client lever logic had ZERO test coverage — a regression would 500 /investments or show wrong numbers while every test stayed green) → FIXED by the pure-module extraction + fuzz test (which provably catches each named regression: dropped end-bump, off-by-one end floor, raised age cap, missing inflation parse); P2s fixed: explorer bounds aligned to the savable validator bounds, exact inflation parse, "at or below inflation" wording, e2e asserts all four fields restored. Accepted P2s: inflation shows "2.50" in Settings vs "2.5" in the what-if (inherent to `<input type=number>`); the "Age now blank uses 40" ordering message doesn't surface the effective default. Gate (real, measured): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → GREEN (1200 unit/97 files, typecheck/lint/build clean); settings-dials + investments e2e 5/5 across two runs (incl. the new what-if + axe AA). NOTE: the four columns live in schema.prisma only (no migration file) — consistent with the documented db-push-is-source-of-truth convention (STATUS: init migration is vestigial); the production Vercel build's `prisma db push` adds them as nullable, no backfill. | Makes the just-shipped planner usable instead of a fixed projection, engine-first per rule #5: the editable inputs reuse the existing Money Dials persist/validate machinery (one settings surface, no second write path), and the what-if reuses the existing pure engine through one shared builder so the interactive numbers are grounded by construction — the no-fabrication rule extended to a live, user-driven money projection. Nullable-with-default columns make the change additive and demo/golden-safe with a zero-touch seed. Extracting the lever logic to a fuzz-tested pure module closes the one real risk (untested client invariant that gates a 500) and keeps the React island a thin display. |

P2 transient-empty data-loss → FIXED: only reconcile when the feed actually returns a holdings ARRAY — an ABSENT field no longer wipes synced rows; an explicit `[]` still reconciles to empty (sold everything); both pinned. Remaining P2s closed with tests (price-rounds-to-zero, NAME_MAX truncation, unicode/empty-symbol rejection, deterministic aggregation-name) or recorded as non-issues (round-half-up ≡ round-half-away for the non-negative holdings values). **LIVE NETWORK UNVERIFIED** (no SimpleFIN token in env) — consistent with the existing SimpleFIN/Plaid live-path labeling; the mocked-server integration (10 tests: ingest+cents, net-worth-vs-holdings separation, trades-not-spending #62, idempotent re-sync, sold-position reconcile, manual-preserved-on-collision, absent-vs-explicit-empty, skip accounting) is the labeled end-to-end simulation. The `source` column + the "sync touches only its own rows" invariant is the data-integrity heart: the critic proved "feed wins" silently destroys a user's manual cost basis, so preserving manual is the principled choice — no silent data loss, even at the cost of not live-pricing a manually-tracked symbol (the user can delete the manual row to adopt the feed). Per-share derivation is an inherent model-fidelity tradeoff (documented, sub-cent, never net worth), consistent with how manual entry already represents a holding. P2s fixed: hi grows past one month's interest (no false "unreachable" at pathological APR), de-doubled the over-budget clause, past-date copy ("already behind us"), Save disabled while a question is in flight + button stays mounted on save (focus preserved, no nested role=status), "in N → end of month", "by next/this month" + "done with my debt" routing, non-divisible share-rounding + snowball-monotonicity + high-APR + overspent + non-zero-save tests. | 127 | R7 | Live-provider ingest CONTRACT AUDIT + first money-bug fixes (prompted by the owner correcting a stale claim: Plaid is on PLAID_ENV=production and SimpleFIN Bridge has all their accounts linked, so the mappers — written/tested ONLY against mocks — process REAL money data; the "UNVERIFIED (no token in env)" notes describe the CI suite, not the deployment). Fix: store the SIGNED balance for assets (overdraft stays negative) and `|owed|` for liabilities (SimpleFIN gives NO liability sign convention — a card may report owed-negative, a loan positive-principal — so the magnitude is the robust owed value, unlike Plaid which HAS a convention and preserves the sign); broadened `inferAccountType` with no-keyword card products + a non-card-liability branch (heloc/home-equity/line-of-credit/servicers) checked BEFORE the generic "credit" rule. Each net-worth contribution hand-verified through `netWorthCents` in the test. (My first cut NEGATED liability balances assuming SimpleFIN's −owed convention; the test caught it on a positive-principal loan → corrected to abs-for-liabilities — the Maker→Checker loop working.) KNOWN EDGE (documented): a genuine OVERPAID card credit balance is indistinguishable from owed-reported-positive → treated as a small owed amount (rare). **(B) Plaid APR (audit #7)** — `aprs[]` was never mapped, so EVERY live Plaid card carried `aprBps` null/0 → the debt-payoff + cash-needed engines computed ZERO interest on real cards (corrupting the just-shipped #125/#126 debt-free-by-date + cash-needed figures). Fix: new pure `pickPlaidAprBps` (purchase APR → bps, fallback highest-non-special, integer-rounded ×100 → no float drift) wired into the `/liabilities/get` loop to set `Account.aprBps` even with no generated statement. TRACKED backlog (confirmed real, deferred to individually-verified increments — STATUS): (P1) SimpleFIN pending never reconciled (#4); SimpleFIN holdings per-share round-trip loses the authoritative total → $0 on low-price lots (#5, needs a `Holding.marketValueCents` column); Plaid investment/loan balances freeze at link time (#6); currency never read on either provider (#3/#10, ~N/A for a US user); + 9 P2s (epoch-UTC date boundary, symbol-regex drops options/crypto, all-unmappable-holdings→delete, Plaid null current→0, Plaid last_statement_balance abs, Plaid null minimum→$0, Plaid mortgage/student dropped). Gate (real, measured 2026-06-28): `bash scripts/verify.sh` core → GREEN (1332 unit/105 files, +4; typecheck/lint/build clean). | The owner's correction reframed these from "dormant/UNVERIFIED" to "live on real money data," which makes the mock-written mappers a real correctness risk — exactly where an adversarial contract audit pays for itself (it found a net-worth sign inversion and a zeroed-interest bug live in production). Fixed the two highest-confidence, highest-money-impact clusters now (a wrong net-worth sign and zeroed card interest both directly mislead the owner) and tracked the rest as scoped increments rather than one large risky change — money-path fixes earn their own hand-verification and regression lock. The abs-for-SimpleFIN-liabilities choice is the honest one: with no provider sign convention, the owed MAGNITUDE is robust where sign-preservation would invert a positive-principal loan. |
| 128 | R7 | SimpleFIN PENDING-transaction reconcile (live-ingest backlog #4, the #127 audit's item 1). SimpleFIN sends a stateless per-window snapshot with NO removed[], and the spec lets a pending txn's id CHANGE when it posts — so a pending that never posts lingered forever (the cash-needed engine SUMS pending → overstated "cash needed"/spend) and a pending→posted-under-a-new-id double-counted. Added `reconcilePendingTransactions` (mirrors `reconcileSimplefinHoldings` / Plaid removed[]) after the Pass-2 upsert, in TWO passes: (1) IN-WINDOW — per account synced this run, deleteMany feed-owned PENDING rows with date>=startDate whose providerRef the feed didn't return; (2) AGE-OUT — deleteMany feed-owned PENDING on the user's SimpleFIN accounts older than PENDING_MAX_AGE_DAYS=32, EXCLUDING anything the current snapshot still reports (a corroborated long hold is never falsely deleted). Safety rails (a deleteMany on real money rows): status:'PENDING' (POSTED is institution-authoritative, never touched), providerRef:{not:null} (manual/seed rows are feed-unowned, never touched), isSplitParent:false (a user split is never orphaned), passes date-disjoint (ageOutFloor<startDate) so `removed` can't double-count. No DB FK points at Transaction (Correction/CategoryPrediction link by id-string only) so the delete can't FK-violate; orphaned analytics-log rows are harmless and match the Plaid removed-path. SyncResult.removed now carries the count (no UI consumer; demo never connects SimpleFIN → golden byte-identical, seed untouched). Hostile critic wf_35ef0562 (3 dims + adversarial verify) → 0 refuted, 2 P1 confirmed+FIXED: (P1-1) an aged hold drifting past the narrow 5-day incremental window was unreconcilable (linger + double-count) → the age-out pass; (P1-2) the `=== undefined` omitted-field guard let an untrusted `transactions: null` reach `for...of null` → TypeError → whole sync aborted (regression from the prior `?? []`) → `if (!acct.transactions) return;` (falsy catches null+undefined, [] still reconciles). Gate (real 2026-06-28): bash scripts/verify.sh → VERIFY GREEN, 1343 unit/106 files (+11 known-answer, proven fail-before/pass-after), typecheck/lint/build clean. ACCEPTED residuals (P2): a hold that drifts past the 5-day overlap then re-posts under a NEW id can briefly double-count until age-out (<=32d, self-healing) — eliminating it needs a wider per-sync fetch (more re-categorization churn/bandwidth for a rare self-correcting case); an account ABSENT from a sync isn't in-window-reconciled (its aged pendings are still swept by age-out); orphaned Correction/CategoryPrediction log rows on delete (Plaid parity, harmless append-only). | Reconcile-by-absence is the only option without a removed[] feed; two passes (narrow-window precision + age-threshold backstop) cover both the fast-post and the multi-day-hold cases, while the corroboration guard keeps the age-out from ever deleting a still-real hold — strictly safer than widening the fetch window, which would expand the existing re-sync re-categorization clobber. |
| 129 | R7 | SimpleFIN holdings AUTHORITATIVE total — live-ingest backlog #5 (the #127 audit's item 2). #124 stored ONLY a per-share priceCents and the engine recomputed marketValue=round(quantity×priceCents), which LOSES low-price/high-quantity lots (a 1,000,000-sh penny lot → $0 and vanishes; a 10,000-sh $50 lot → $100; the documented VOO −1¢ drift). Fix: persist the feed's TOTAL as a new nullable `Holding.marketValueCents` (db-push, golden-safe: null for manual/seed → engine derives → demo $142k byte-identical); `valuePosition` uses it verbatim when present (explicit 0 honored; fail-loud located throw on a negative/non-integer total — critic ENG-1), else derives round(qty×price). Mapper emits+bounds it; reconcile persists it; getInvestments selects+maps it; addHolding writes null (manual is price-derived, clears any stale feed total for THAT edit). NET WORTH UNAFFECTED — net worth/coach.portfolioCents/FI/retirement/goals use account.currentBalanceCents; summarizePortfolio's ONLY consumer is /investments, so this moves ONLY the position breakdown. Hostile critic wf_844918ca (3 Checkers + adversarial verify): 0 P0, **1 P1 confirmed+FIXED** — marketValueCents Int = Postgres 32-bit (max $21,474,836.47/position); a total above it overflows the column + is swallowed by the reconcile catch → silently vanishes in prod (SQLite CI blind) → mapper now bounds every persisted cents value to MAX_DB_CENTS=2,147,483,647 (over-ceiling skipped+counted, not stored), boundary-pinned. **3 P2 FIXED**: engine self-validates the total; pure `isPerShareApproximate` marks the /investments per-share "≈" so a sub-cent row never contradicts its exact total; softened the addHolding "price wins" comment to the immediate edit (#124 re-ingest unchanged). Confirmation Checker: SHIP. | A tested mapper must carry the SOURCE's authoritative figure, not a lossy reconstruction; and every persisted value must fit the column it is stored in, failing VISIBLY (skip+count) not silently (swallowed overflow). Money correctness on the owner's real connected brokerage. |
| 130 | R7 | Plaid per-sync account-balance REFRESH — live-ingest backlog #6 (the #127 audit's item 3). `PlaidProvider.syncTransactions` refreshed balances ONLY for accounts echoed in the `/transactions/sync` `accounts` array (depository/credit with transaction activity); INVESTMENT and LOAN accounts carry no Transactions product, so their `currentBalanceCents` — and the owner's net worth — FROZE at link time (only re-fetched at link via `exchangePublicToken`). Fix: at the start of each item's sync, call the already-existing `syncAccountsForItem` (`/accounts/get` → `upsertPlaidAccounts`, which returns EVERY account on the item) once per item, BEFORE the cursor loop; the loop's `page.accounts` echo (fresher-or-equal) still wins for active accounts. Best-effort + audited (`plaid.accounts.refresh.failed`): a refresh failure (e.g. ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED) must never block transaction ingest, the higher-value path, and the per-item catch still retries the item. Reuses the audit-endorsed `/accounts/get` (cached balances, no per-call fee) over `/accounts/balance/get` (real-time, billable). Golden-safe (demo never uses PlaidProvider). Added the FIRST mocked-server integration test of the Plaid network orchestration — `tests/unit/plaid-balance-refresh.test.ts` (real PlaidProvider vs a stubbed Plaid server; proven fail-before/pass-after) — the socket itself stays UNVERIFIED against live Plaid, consistent with the existing labeling. **Hostile critic wf_25be9884 (3 lenses + adversarial verify) → 0 P0, 1 P1 CONFIRMED + FIXED + regression-locked:** now that investment/loan balances refresh every sync, Plaid's documented-nullable `balances.current` run through the mapper's `?? 0` would OVERWRITE a real balance with $0 (silently cratering net worth until a later non-null sync self-heals) — and the change is the exact path that newly subjects investment/loan to this. Fix = map a null `current` → null (UNKNOWN, not 0) and OMIT `currentBalanceCents` from the UPDATE data when null so Prisma preserves the last-known-good value (CREATE falls back to `?? 0` — a brand-new account has no prior to preserve); this ALSO closes the same pre-existing hole on the depository/credit echo path (shared mapper) and is robust to either `/accounts/get` or the `/transactions/sync` echo writing null (last-writer preserves). Independent confirmation checker: SHIP, 0 P0/P1. Gate (real, measured 2026-06-28): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → VERIFY GREEN, 1369 unit/107 files (+5: 3 backlog-#6 + 1 null-preserve integration + 1 mapper null), typecheck/lint/build clean. No e2e surface (server-only sync; demo never connects Plaid — the mocked-server integration is the labeled end-to-end, per #124/#128/#129). ACCEPTED P2s (documented): per-sync audit-log noise (a skipped row each sync for an unmappable account; double rows refresh.failed + item.sync.failed on a full item outage — cosmetic, zero ledger/net-worth impact); token decrypted twice + item re-fetched per item per sync (negligible at hourly cadence — kept surgical rather than widen the `syncAccountsForItem` signature); `availableBalanceCents`/`creditLimitCents` still write-through on null (nullable by design + non-net-worth — null is a legitimate value for them, unlike `current`); CREATE null→0 covered by the pure mapper test + reasoning, not a dedicated integration test. | Net worth must reflect the institution's current balance each sync, not freeze at link — a stale brokerage/loan balance silently misstates real net worth. Reusing the already-tested `syncAccountsForItem` (the audit's first-named fix) is the simplest correct change and adds the first integration coverage of the Plaid socket. The critic's P1 is the sharper lesson: making a balance refresh every sync turns a latent null-handling shortcut (`?? 0`) into active money corruption, so null must mean UNKNOWN (preserve) not $0 — and fixing it in the shared upsert closes the same hole on the depository path for free. A money-path change earns its own hand-verified fail-before/pass-after lock plus an independent confirmation pass. |
| 131 | R7 | "Plan in Words" retirement slice — INVERSE retire-at-age planner (AI Differentiation Plan §1.1; the THIRD and final Plan-in-Words slice after debt #125 + savings #126, completing the owner-set sequence debt→savings→retire-at-age; "continue"). State a retirement AGE ("can I retire at 60?") and the app SOLVES for the minimal monthly contribution that makes the portfolio LAST through the plan-through age, with honest affordability. **Engine-first:** new pure `engine/solve/retire-at-age.ts` `solveRetireAtAge` — unlike the flat savings twin the portfolio COMPOUNDS, so there is no closed form: it BISECTS the BOOLEAN `projectRetirement(...).outcome==='sustained'` (the #122 decumulation engine, assembled through the SAME `buildRetirementInputs` the /investments outlook uses — the solver originates NO compounding/withdrawal/depletion math, only the contribution by bisection). Bisecting the boolean (not a cent value) is EXACT because the engine's per-month half-away-from-zero rounding makes the balance only WEAKLY monotone, but the depleted→sustained flip is one-directional (proven by induction: `round` is monotone, +monthly and −W preserve order, the zero-floor preserves ≥). Honest `outcome` (already-on-track / reachable / unreachable{age-in-past, age-after-end, cannot-sustain}) + share-of-safe-to-spend bps (computed on the ADDITIONAL money, required − current) + `withinSafeToSpend`, the same shape as #125/#126. Pre-checks guard the engine's throws; an `accumMonths===0` short-circuit + an HI_CAP-bounded hi-doubling keep the search from overflowing the engine's `assertSafe`. **Grounding (no-fabrication soul):** new Ask intent `retire_at_age`; a deterministic `parseTargetAge` extracts the user's stated age (the LLM supplies ONLY the kind; the age is re-derived in `intentFromKind`), and the server route grounds every figure in `getCoachData.fi` (portfolio/savings/spend/return/SWR — byte-identical to /coach + the outlook) + the User planning dials (ages/inflation ?? RETIREMENT_ASSUMPTIONS) + `getSpendingPlan` safe-to-spend. **Save path (option a, least-duplicative):** "Confirm & save as plan" persists the chosen age to the EXISTING `User.retirementAge` dial via a thin `saveRetirementAge` (re-validates bounds [18,110] + the dials cross-field ordering, ownership-scoped, audited) — NOT a flat `Goal`, which would CONTRADICT the compounding engine (the EDGE_CASES "card consistency" precedent inverted from #126); the outlook card already recomputes the contribution live from this dial, so nothing drifts or duplicates. `AssistantGoalAction` became a discriminated union (`save_retirement_age` carries `targetAge`, not a `targetDate`); ask-view's save dispatch is a type-safe switch with retirement-specific copy ("Save as my plan" → /investments, vs "Save as a goal" → /goals). GOLDEN-SAFE: the Ask path is read-only; the demo User's planning columns are null → coalesce to 40/65/95/250 → byte-identical to #122/#123. **Hostile critic (wf_c5d22775, 4 dims → adversarial verify of every P0/P1): 0 P0 / 0 P1 confirmed.** The lone P1 candidate (the routing gate + parseTargetAge's first regex were anchored on literal "retire", missing the inflections "retiring"/"retired") was adversarially DOWNGRADED to P2 (the canonical "Can I retire at 60?" + "retire at age 65 / by 67 / when I'm 62" all work; no correctness or data harm) — FIXED anyway + regression-locked (broadened to `retir(e/es/ed/ing/ement)`). Two more P2s FIXED: (grounding) the strict `targetAge > endAge` after-end guard let `targetAge == endAge` return a vacuous savable "on-track" that `saveRetirementAge` would REJECT → changed to `>=` so the answer and the save validator agree; (ux) "your current saving" → "savings". ACCEPTED P2 (STATUS): the solver guards `targetAge` against current/end but not a structurally-invalid PLANNING age (current≥end, non-integer) — those reach projectRetirement and fail LOUD, which is correct (the server only ever supplies app-validated User columns or the documented defaults; matches the #122 / STATUS #13 API-consumer precedent). Gate (real, measured 2026-06-29): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → VERIFY GREEN (typecheck/lint/build clean, 1409 unit/110 files, +40); ask.spec e2e 8/8 incl. the new retire-at-age flow + axe AA (the only full-suite e2e failure was the documented phase2-triage:82 throughput flake on an untouched page under my own back-to-back-run load — STATUS #16/#17, proven by isolated rerun, NOT a regression). | The final Plan-in-Words slice, completing the owner-sequenced trilogy. Retire-at-age is the one slice whose model COMPOUNDS, so it earns a bisection over the tested #122 engine rather than a closed form — and bisecting the boolean sustained-predicate (not a cent value) is what makes it exact under the engine's weak cent-level monotonicity. Persisting the age to the existing dial (not a new flat Goal) keeps a single source of truth the outlook already reads — the inverse of #126's "a flat Goal is correct there", because here the real math compounds. The critic earned its keep again: it caught an answer-vs-save inconsistency (offering a Save the server rejects) and a natural-phrasing coverage gap, both cheap to fix before ship. |
| 132 | R7 | Plaid credit-liability statement-field correctness — live-ingest backlog (the #127 audit's remaining credit-card P2s), on the owner's REAL connected Plaid cards. `mapPlaidLiabilityToStatement` had two field bugs corrupting the cash-needed headline (the killer feature): (1) `last_statement_balance` ran through `plaidDollarsToPositiveCents` (abs), so a statement CREDIT / overpayment (negative balance) flipped to an amount OWED — a card the holder overpaid would DEMAND cash it doesn't owe; (2) a null `minimum_payment_amount` collapsed to $0, UNDERSTATING the MINIMUM-path cash needed below the engine's own no-statement estimate. Fix: map the balance with the sign-preserving `plaidSignedDollarsToCents` (the engine's `floorAtZero` on remainingDue/minimumDue then yields a correct $0 obligation for a credit), and when no usable (>0) minimum is reported on a positive balance, mirror the engine's EXACT estimate by reusing a now-`export`ed `estimateMinimumPayment` (max $35 / 1% of balance) — ONE definition, no drift; a credit/zero balance owes $0; isEstimated stays false (balance + dates are real). GOLDEN-SAFE by construction: the common path (positive balance + provided positive min) is byte-identical, and the demo never connects Plaid. Hostile critic wf_edd3d8f3 (4 dimension critics → adversarial verification of every P0/P1): **0 P0 / 0 P1 confirmed**; 3 distinct P2s — TWO FIXED + regression-locked: (a) a PROVIDED 0 (or sub-cent) minimum on a positive balance reproduced the same understatement → a "usable" minimum is now >0, so a reported ≤0 falls through to the estimate (conservative: paying at least an estimated minimum is always safe; matches the engine's posture, and the min only drives the MINIMUM scenario); (b) the $0 guarantee for CONTRADICTORY feed data (a credit balance reported with a positive minimum) was unpinned → pinned with a mapper known-answer + a mapper→computeCashNeeded e2e under both scenarios. Gate (real, measured 2026-06-29): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → VERIFY GREEN, typecheck/lint/build clean, 1417 unit/110 files (+8: known-answer for both branches + provided-0 + a mapper→cash-needed ENGINE end-to-end proving credit→$0 and null-min→$80, all proven fail-before/pass-after). No e2e surface (server-only mapper; demo never connects Plaid → the labeled unit + engine end-to-end is the coverage, per #124/#128/#129/#130). ACCEPTED/DEFERRED P2 (documented, STATUS): an estimated minimum is presented with `isEstimated:false` and no per-card "minimum estimated" disclosure — honoring the cardinal "assumptions inline" rule here needs a PERSISTED `Statement.minimumIsEstimated` column threaded through the sacred cash-needed engine + types, disproportionate to the rare trigger (Plaid omitting the minimum on a card that HAS a generated statement); the estimate is conservative and equals the engine's own no-statement formula. | Money correctness on the owner's real connected Plaid cards: a statement credit must never be abs()'d into an amount owed, and a missing/zero minimum must never understate the conservative cash-needed headline — both directly corrupt the killer feature. Reusing the engine's `estimateMinimumPayment` (one definition) guarantees the mapper's estimate can't drift from the engine's own estimate path. The critic earned its keep: a PROVIDED 0 is the same understatement as a null one (a usable minimum is >0), and the $0-on-credit cap deserved an explicit lock against future engine drift. |
| 133 | R7 | SimpleFIN all-unmappable-holdings DATA-LOSS guard — live-ingest backlog (the #127 audit's P2), on the owner's REAL linked brokerage. `syncFromSimplefin`'s INVESTMENT branch reconciled holdings whenever `acct.holdings !== undefined`; `mapSimplefinHoldings` SKIPS un-mappable positions, so a NON-EMPTY feed whose positions ALL fail to map returned `holdings:[]` → `reconcileSimplefinHoldings(accountId, [])` → `syncedSymbols.length===0` → `deleteMany({accountId, source:'simplefin'})` WIPED the owner's entire synced /investments breakdown, mistaking a format glitch / all-unsupported-types feed for a sell-all. Fix: reconcile only when `holdings.length > 0 || acct.holdings.length === 0` (positions to write, OR an EXPLICITLY empty feed = a genuine sell-all); a non-empty feed that maps to zero leaves existing rows intact (counted as skipped, nothing written or DELETED) and self-heals on the next sync that maps any position — the same conservative stance as the OMITTED-field guard (#124 P2). NET WORTH UNAFFECTED (uses account.currentBalanceCents; holdings are a within-account breakdown). GOLDEN-SAFE (demo never connects SimpleFIN). Hostile critic wf_8a9d99dc (2 dims → adversarial verify; one dim hit a mid-response API error, the other returned the finding): **0 P0 / 0 P1**; **1 P2 FIXED + regression-locked** — the outer guard tested `!== undefined`, so an UNTRUSTED feed sending `holdings: null` (not omitted) slipped through to `mapSimplefinHoldings(null)` → "null is not iterable" → ABORTED the whole sync (the exact `transactions: null` failure class fixed in #128), and a `holdings: ""` would even WIPE via `.length`; changed the guard to `Array.isArray(acct.holdings)` so undefined/null/any-non-array all route to "leave rows intact". Gate (real, measured 2026-06-29): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → VERIFY GREEN, typecheck/lint/build clean, 1419 unit/110 files (+2: a non-empty all-unmappable feed preserves rows + reports skipped:2; a non-array `null` feed stays ok:true with rows intact — both proven fail-before/pass-after). No e2e surface (server-only sync; the mocked-server integration is the labeled end-to-end, per #124/#128/#129/#130). | Data integrity on the owner's real linked brokerage: a within-account breakdown must never be silently wiped by a glitchy/partial feed; only an EXPLICITLY empty feed is a real sell-all (reconcile-to-empty needs the RAW feed empty, not just the mapped result). The critic's keep is the sharper lesson — the same untrusted-feed null-hardening (#128) the transaction path already has must apply to holdings, and `Array.isArray` is the one guard that handles undefined, null, AND any non-array, preventing both a sync-abort and a `.length` mis-fire. |
| 134 | R7 | Plaid mortgage/student loans → calendar + reminders (the BIGGEST remaining #127 live-money item; owner picked the surface via AskUserQuestion = "Calendar + reminders", NOT the cash-needed dollar headline). Two parts. **(A) Ingest:** `syncLiabilities` now reads `liabilities.mortgage[]` and `liabilities.student[]` (previously ONLY `credit[]`), populating each loan Account's `aprBps` + `minimumPaymentCents` (the fixed monthly payment) + `dueDayOfMonth` via pure `mapPlaidMortgageToLoanFields` / `mapPlaidStudentToLoanFields`. Each mapped field is null when Plaid didn't report a usable value, and the UPDATE OMITS null fields (preserve-on-null, the #130 discipline) — a deferment-state loan never blanks a known rate/payment. Plaid `loan/mortgage` subtype now maps to a distinct `MORTGAGE` type (excluded from the debt snowball, like a manual mortgage / Ramsey BS6); `student`/auto/other → `LOAN` (included). Outstanding balance is unchanged (the account balance, already net-worth-correct); the mappers deliberately ignore `origination_principal_amount` (ORIGINAL, not current). Student `account_id` is NULLABLE per Plaid → an unjoinable row is skipped, never throws. **(B) Surface:** a NEW pure engine `engine/loans/obligations.ts::selectLoanObligations` derives the next dated payment per LOAN/MORTGAGE with a positive payment + a due day (raw `nextDayOfMonth`, `effectiveDueDate` = `priorBusinessDayIfNonBusiness` clamped ≥ today — the SAME business-day rule cards use; `nextDayOfMonth` moved into `dates.ts` as the single tested date utility, rule #3). It is a SEPARATE surface: the cash-needed ENGINE is UNTOUCHED (its `type==='CREDIT'` filter, headline `requiredCents`/`cardsDueCount`, and projection are byte-identical). `cashNeededFromSnapshot` computes `loanObligations` once; the calendar adds a `loan-due` event kind (Landmark icon + 'due' badge, counted in `totalOut` + `reminderDates`), and `selectPaymentReminders` is generalized with an `obligationType:'card'\|'loan'` discriminator (PaymentReminder fields renamed cardId/cardName → accountId/accountName; copy "card payment" → "payment"). Seed: removed the `sched-autoloan` stand-in (a hand-authored checking outflow that faked the loan into cash-needed) — the loan now surfaces as a first-class loan-due exactly as a real Plaid loan would; demo has no shortfall so the cash-needed headline stays byte-identical. **Accepted boundary (owner's choice):** loans are a calendar/reminder SIGNAL only; the cash-needed projection/shortfall stays card-focused (the "headline too" option was declined). Tests (+25): loan-obligations (weekend+observed-holiday rollback, clamp-to-today, exclusions, sort), dates.nextDayOfMonth, plaid-loan-liabilities (pure mapper + subtype), plaid-loan-liabilities-sync (mocked-server: populate + preserve-on-null + null-account_id skip), reminders loans (merge/sort/window/email), phase4 demo loan-due lock + sched-autoloan-gone, e2e dashboard "Auto Loan" reminder. **Hostile critic wf_d388bf4b (3 lenses — Plaid data-integrity / loan-engine math / surface-golden — → adversarial verify): 0 confirmed P0/P1.** 2 mapper money-bugs FIXED + regression-locked: (F1) the `> 0` guard ran on the PRE-rounded input, so a sub-cent payment / sub-bps rate wrote a fabricated 0 (ZEROING a stored value) → now rounds FIRST then `> 0` (mirrors #132); (F2) a huge finite payment threw via cents() safe-integer assert (aborting the item’s whole liability sweep incl. its cards) despite the "non-throwing" comment → now magnitude-bounded to the Postgres Int ceiling BEFORE rounding → null. Accepted/documented residuals (STATUS #134, owner-gated NEXT — the loan is representable as a recurring-detected scheduled outflow AND the new loan-due, with NO de-dup): the demo /forecast drops the loan after the sched-autoloan removal (real users unaffected); a connected MORTGAGE/STUDENT recurring-detected as a non-transfer outflow could double-display (NOT the auto loan / not a transfer payment); a reported-$0 payment is preserved (per #132). Gate (real, measured 2026-06-30): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → ✅ VERIFY GREEN — typecheck/lint/build clean, 1446 unit/113 files (+27); e2e calendar/reminders/a11y 15/15 clean (axe AA), dashboard reminder surfaces the Auto Loan. | The owner runs real connected loans; today they're a balance with APR 0 (debt-payoff mis-computes) and an invisible payment/due-date. Reusing the loan Account's existing `minimumPaymentCents`/`dueDayOfMonth` fields + the cards' business-day rule, in a SEPARATE pure engine that never touches the card-framed cash-needed math, is the surgical way to surface "your $385 loan is due Jul 2" without perturbing a single headline golden. Preserve-on-null keeps a deferment/missing field from cratering a known rate; mapping mortgage→MORTGAGE keeps the snowball convention. Removing the seed stand-in makes the demo model a loan the same way production does — one code path, no double-display. |
| 135 | R7 | Currency guard — withhold non-USD accounts from net worth + spending (the #127 live-ingest audit's item #3/#10, "currency never read"). The app does NO foreign exchange, so a non-USD / zero-decimal (JPY/KRW) feed balance was summed into net worth at a fabricated 1:1. **Design:** persist a NULLABLE `Account.currency` (null = legacy/demo/manual = assumed USD → golden-safe; non-USD canonicalized + stored). Pure helpers `src/lib/providers/currency.ts` — `canonicalizeCurrency` (3-letter ISO upper-cased; any other token, incl. a SimpleFIN non-ISO URL, kept as-is so it can never equal 'USD'), `resolvePlaidCurrency` (ISO preferred over the unofficial/crypto code), `isSupportedCurrency` (null\|'USD'). Both mappers persist it (`mapSimplefinAccount` ← `acct.currency`; `mapPlaidAccount` ← `balances.iso_currency_code`/`unofficial_currency_code`, added to the `PlaidAccount` wire type); both sync writers store it (create+update). **Withhold a non-USD account AND all its rows at every account-scoped read:** `DemoProvider.getFinanceSnapshot` (delegated to by Plaid) filters `accounts` by `isSupportedCurrency` and `transactions`/`scheduled`/`balanceSnapshots` by the surviving account ids — so net worth, cash-needed, forecast, coach, the assistant, reports, trends and spending-plan all inherit it; `getAccountsView`, `getInvestments`, `getTransactions` (register), triage (`getTriageItems`/`getReviewCount`/`similarTransactionsWhere`), `/budgets`, and `refreshRecurringForUser` carry the equivalent Prisma predicate `OR:[{currency:null},{currency:'USD'}]`; and all ~15 first-run `prisma.account.count` empty-state gates (13 pages + export + cron) count SUPPORTED accounts so the gate agrees with the filtered snapshot. **Two hostile-critic cycles.** Cycle 1 (wf_74fc0808, 4 dims → adversarial verify): **4 P1 bypasses confirmed + FIXED + regression-locked** — (P1-A) `getInvestments` rolled non-USD holdings into the USD "Portfolio value" at 1:1 → filter by currency; (P1-B) the count gates counted RAW rows while the snapshot filtered → an all-non-USD user skipped the empty state and hit `resolvePaymentAccount`'s throw (6+ pages error-boundary) + a 500 from the export route → all gates now count supported; (P1-C ×2) the snapshot filtered only `accounts`, leaving a non-USD account's TRANSACTIONS in `snap.transactions` (summed 1:1 in reports/trends/coach/spending-plan/assistant) + the register → filter transactions at the source + the register/triage predicate. Plus a P2 fail-open FIXED: `resolvePlaidCurrency('','BTC')` let a blank iso shadow the crypto code (`iso ?? unofficial` only falls through on null) → canonicalize EACH then coalesce. Cycle 2 / confirmation (wf_bda5c45a, 3 lenses): 2 lenses fixes-hold; the completeness lens found **2 MORE direct transaction reads of the same P1-C class, both FIXED + locked** — `/budgets` per-category spend and `refreshRecurringForUser` (a foreign subscription would persist a scheduled row on the USD payment account at 1:1, leaking into forecast/cash-needed). Tests (+22): `currency.test.ts` (canonicalize/resolve/supported + the fail-open guard) and the `currency-guard.test.ts` integration suite (net worth, snapshot transactions, `getReviewCount`, `getAccountsView`, `getInvestments`, the direct-read predicate, and the supported gate-count — each proving a EUR/GBP account and its rows are excluded while a null-currency legacy row is counted). **Accepted/deferred P2 residuals (documented):** (a) the transactions-CSV export still lists a foreign account's rows (a faithful raw per-row dump, no summed figure); (b) account PICKERS (settings payment-account selector, /transactions/new, /transactions/import) may still list a non-USD account (cosmetic; no sum; a foreign payment-account choice falls back to a USD account); (c) NO UI disclosure that a foreign account was excluded — and for a LIABILITY the withhold flatters net worth ("a withheld figure beats a silently wrong one"; a "N accounts excluded — no FX yet" banner is the right follow-up); (d) SimpleFIN HOLDING-level currency still unread (a non-USD position inside a SUPPORTED account rolls into the /investments breakdown at 1:1; net worth uses the authoritative account balance, so bounded); (e) numeric ISO codes (e.g. '840') are withheld not mapped (fail-safe; neither provider emits them); (f) the categorization backfill + settings transaction-COUNT still touch foreign rows (non-figure: a hidden categorize / a cosmetic count). Gate (real, measured 2026-06-30): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → ✅ VERIFY GREEN — typecheck/lint/build clean, **1465 unit / 115 files** (+21). No e2e surface (server/read-path only; the `currency-guard` integration suite is the labeled end-to-end, per #124/#128/#130/#132/#133). | The owner runs REAL Plaid+SimpleFIN money; a foreign balance summed at a made-up 1:1 is a silently-wrong headline — the no-fabrication soul says withhold what we can't denominate, never invent a USD figure. A single canonical `isSupportedCurrency` predicate, applied at every account-scoped read (and mirrored as a Prisma `OR` for the DB-side reads), keeps net worth AND spending consistent; null = USD makes every existing/demo row a byte-identical no-op. The two critic cycles earned their keep: the "two filters cover everything" premise was wrong — account balances and transactions enter through getInvestments, the count gates, /budgets, and the recurring refresh too — and only an adversarial sweep (find → verify → confirm) surfaced all of them. The remaining residuals are cosmetic, non-summed, or fail-safe, and the highest-value follow-up (an excluded-account disclosure so a hidden foreign liability can't quietly flatter net worth) is recorded for a UI increment. |

| 140 | iOS focus-zoom fix (owner report on #139 in prod: "formatting is terrible — it zooms in with that dropdown box"). Root cause: iOS Safari force-zooms the viewport when a focused form control's font-size is <16px; the app has NO shared Input component — every raw input/select is Tailwind text-sm (14px), so the entire app's forms zoom on focus on iPhones, and the register category-menu (which AUTOFOCUSES its search on open) zoomed the moment it appeared. Fix at the ROOT, not the call sites: a documented globals.css rule floors form-control text at 1rem under `@media (pointer: coarse)` — `input[class]:not([type=checkbox]):not([type=radio]), select[class], textarea[class]` (the [class] selector reaches 0-1-1 specificity, outranking the 0-1-0 text-sm/text-xs utilities without !important); checkboxes/radios excluded; desktop (fine pointer) keeps compact 14px, so the rule is inert everywhere the bug cannot occur. Also: the register category-menu widens w-56→w-72 (a 224px dropdown hosting a create form on a 380px phone was genuinely cramped) with max-w-[calc(100vw-2rem)] so it can never exceed a narrow viewport. e2e-locked: computed font-size ≥16px asserted in the touch-emulated mobile project for the triage search + write-in name input AND the register search (proves (pointer:coarse) matches in the Pixel-5 emulation — measured, not assumed). Gate (real 2026-07-01): verify.sh → ✅ GREEN 1476 unit/116 files; triage write-in spec GREEN 7.7s incl. both zoom locks; register race GREEN 4.6s; register happy path witnessed green through the zoom lock + prefill + confirm pane — its once-click tail is the session's documented environmental stall (A/B'd at HEAD ×2 today). | One media query kills the whole bug class app-wide (auth, manual entry, settings, statement forms all had it) instead of patching the one dropdown the owner happened to touch; per-control text-base sm:text-sm would have needed ~20 edits and still missed future raw inputs. |

| 146 | Checker cycle-2 fixes (20/20 confirmed against the cycle-1 stack). CONCURRENCY DESIGN: all five check-then-act guard sites (Plaid + SimpleFIN sync guards, the pending→posted transplant, fileMerchantGroup, recategorize) now run through `serializableTx` — one helper in src/lib/db.ts issuing the interactive transaction at SERIALIZABLE isolation with a bounded (3-attempt) retry on P2034 only; every other error fails loudly on attempt 1. Why Serializable over compare-and-set: the preserve predicate depends on Correction rows, which have NO relation/FK to Transaction, so an updateMany WHERE cannot re-assert "corrected"; and CAS cannot stop two concurrent group-files from both missing the rule-dedupe findFirst. Postgres detects the write-write conflict (first-updater-wins on the row) even when the OTHER writer runs at READ COMMITTED, and SSI predicate locking catches the read-then-insert dedupe race between two serializable transactions; a detected conflict re-runs the whole fn against fresh state (DB-only side effects at every site). Probed 2026-07-02: the better-sqlite3 driver adapter ACCEPTS the explicit level (semantically a no-op — single-writer), so one uniform code path. SPLIT LIFECYCLE INVARIANT: "a split parent's children always sum to its amount, and children share the parent's status; when the bank breaks the sum (amount drift), the split DISSOLVES to one row on the fresh verdict, back into review" — enforced across Plaid id-churn transplant (carries the split: new container, children re-pointed + posted, corrections follow), same-id refresh in both providers (drift dissolves; preserved splits post their children), and buffered removed[] (a canceled split charge cascades its children — phantom spending otherwise). Splitting PENDING rows stays SUPPORTED (critic2 F1 models the seeded pending Zelle split) — the status-guard alternative was rejected as a capability regression. Removed[] is now buffered per item and applied after the page loop (Plaid doesn't guarantee the removed/added twin share a page). Create/create sync races: catch P2002 → guarded-update fallback (both providers — restores CQ-2 without losing the cycle-1 verdict guard that forced the upsert split). Merchantless file scope pins merchantId (IS NULL) so the raw: card's action partitions exactly like groupKey; aggregates stay descriptor-only (one agg: card legitimately mixes merchantless CSV + merchanted synced rows). ensureUnconditionalRule (shared by fileMerchantGroup + recategorize) dedupes on the FIVE condition columns being null. groupEmptied is derived BEFORE dispatch from committed state (React updaters are pure; eager evaluation is an optimization, not a contract). | Alternatives: per-site CAS updateManys (rejected — cannot express the corrected predicate, leaves the dedupe race); a DB unique constraint on (userId,merchantId,categoryId) (rejected — NULL condition columns make the constraint either too strict for future conditional rules or a no-op, cross-provider NULL-distinct semantics); forbidding pending splits (rejected — documented capability, critic2 F1); useEffect-based mode reset (rejected — one frame of empty-singles flicker; pre-dispatch derivation is exact under discrete-event flushing). HONESTY: the PG races are closed by isolation-level reasoning + the helper's contract locks, NOT reproduced on the SQLite test env — labeled UNVERIFIED-on-PG in STATUS pending a PG integration environment. |
| 147 | Checker cycle-3 fixes (16/16 confirmed on the cycle-2 increment). SPLIT-LIFECYCLE COMPLETION (P0): SimpleFIN pending→posted NEW-ID churn has no id link, so the split cannot follow — reconcilePendingTransactions now DISSOLVES stale pending split parents (both passes: in-window per synced account + the 32-day age-out) by deleting parent AND children inside a serializable tx with the parent list read in-tx; pass 2's fresh posted row goes through normal ingest. Deletion IS the dissolve here: the alternative (heuristic matching of the new posted row by account/amount/date to transplant the split) was rejected — the house already ruled heuristic identity out (cycle-1 residual) and a wrong match would misfile real money. This also invalidates DECISIONS #128's "self-healing ≤32d" premise FOR SPLITS — the old isSplitParent exclusions made the stale split immortal. DISSOLVE-FORCES-REVIEW (P1): all three dissolve sites now write needsReview:true + confidenceBps:null over the pipeline verdict — a destroyed user decision re-decides UNCONDITIONALLY; the user's own merchant rule still supplies the SUGGESTION (categoryId) but never the auto-file. THE SIXTH WRITER (P1): applyCategory rebuilt as ONE serializableTx with fresh in-tx reads (fromCategoryId from current state; rule mint via the shared helper; row update + prediction truth in-tx) — it was four bare statements on the highest-traffic path (singles fileRow, recategorize scope:'one'). makeRuleFromCorrection likewise. RETIRE-ON-CHANGED-MIND (P1): ensureUnconditionalRule now SUPERSEDES unconditional rules of the same merchant to a DIFFERENT category before reuse-or-mint — "this merchant files to C" is a statement about the merchant, not just the pair — closing the stale-rule-wins tie-break (probed: coffee→dining recategorize previously kept filing coffee forever). Conditional rules are never superseded (narrower intent). Undo of the new correction cannot resurrect a retired rule — accepted: re-minting is one tap, a silent wrong-category auto-file is not. WIRING LOCK (P1 gate): tests/unit/serializable-wiring.test.ts pins the guard-site wiring (spy on the four triage actions + provider source pin ≥3 serializableTx sites and zero interactive prisma.$transaction) — the checker's sed-strip experiment now fails. P2s: removed[]-cascade read moved in-tx; guardedVerdictRefresh skips deleted-in-window rows (was: P2025 aborted the whole SimpleFIN pass-2 loop); audit events gated rule.create vs rule.reuse on minted (provenance-honest); ledger miscounts corrected in place. | Alternatives: forbidding pending splits (rejected again — capability, critic2 F1); keeping deleted-rule tombstones for undo-resurrection (rejected — schema change for a one-tap re-mint); catching P2025 outside the tx (rejected — the read belongs inside; skip-on-null is exact); heuristic split transplant for SimpleFIN churn (rejected — no id link, wrong-match misfiles money). |
| 148 | Cycle-5 fixes (OWNER-AUTHORIZED beyond the 4-cycle cap; cycle-4 confirmed 1 P1 + 8 P2). REVIEW PIN (the P1): `Transaction.reviewPinned Boolean @default(false)` — set by every sync DISSOLVE (both providers' same-id drift + the Plaid transplant dissolve), respected by the preserve predicate (preserve = isSplitParent || reviewPinned || (corrected && !needsReview)), carried ACROSS id churn by the transplant (a pinned predecessor re-creates pinned — otherwise churn was a pin-laundering path), and cleared by every USER filing action (applyCategory, fileMerchantGroup, recategorize, applyToAllSimilar, splitTransaction's claim). Why a column: a dissolved row was representationally IDENTICAL to an undone row (needsReview:true + corrections + null confidence), and the cycle-1 "undone takes the fresh verdict" rule — which is correct for undo — re-applied the user's own merchant rule one cron interval later (cycle-4 probed it: sync N review, sync N+1 silently auto-filed). No non-schema encoding survives: sentinel corrections pollute undo, and any in-band flag combination collides with a real state. | 151 | Loan payments folded into the /forecast balance projection (#134 follow-up — the owner-gated de-dup design decision, delegated by "do all recommended"). GROUND TRUTH (understand workflow wf_aae820f1): a loan payment has TWO representations — a loan-due obligation (keyed by the loan Account → calendar+reminders) and, potentially, a recurring-detected checking scheduled row (→ forecast+calendar) — and there is NO structural key linking a checking-account scheduled row to the loan Account it pays (a scheduled row carries only accountId=checking + free-text description; the loan side keys on the loan Account's type/minimumPayment/dueDay). A cross-source de-dup would require heuristic money-matching (amount/servicer/cadence) — the class the house has repeatedly rejected (a wrong match misfiles money). DECISION = **Option D**: the forecast (a balance trajectory) UNDER-projected checking because a loan payment really debits checking but is NOT a scheduled row (the demo's $385/mo auto-loan went invisible after #134 removed the `sched-autoloan` stand-in); feed loan obligations into the forecast from their ONE safe structural source (the loan Account) via a pure `loanObligationsToScheduledFlows` (MONTHLY outflow on the RAW `dueDate`, never `effectiveDueDate` — a business-day-shifted anchor would drag the shift into every future month). `getCashFlowForecast` concats them onto the checking scheduled flows (same holiday+`selectLoanObligations` derivation as finance.ts:59-76). NO de-dup attempted (none is safe). Rejected: A (loan-due canonical + suppress the scheduled row) & B (scheduled canonical + suppress loan-due) — both need the nonexistent key → heuristic; C (split by surface + re-add a seed scheduled row) — reintroduces the calendar double-display the seed removed. The companion carve-out removal (detect.ts:83-85 `'auto-loan'`) was DECLINED as out of scope: `refreshRecurringForUser` runs ONLY on real Plaid/SimpleFIN sync (never for the seeded demo), so the "latent post-refresh double-count" is NOT demo-reachable, and removing the carve-out would churn ~8 recurring goldens for zero demo benefit — recorded as an optional owner-gated follow-up. Golden-safe: no unit/e2e pins demo forecast milestones (verified); the forecast e2e pins only the unchanged $3,400 starting balance + now the "Auto Loan" upcoming label. Hostile Checker (wf_1a6616ee, 3 lenses → adversarial verify; money-math ⇒ maker/checker): **0 P0/P1**, probe-confirmed the loan folds in exactly 3× at −$385, correct sign, currency-guard-filtered, no golden moves. P2s FIXED pre-commit: (P2-A honesty) EDGE_CASES §LO-H relabeled — the $3,015/$2,630/$2,245 are the loan's ISOLATED contribution / the `forecast.test.ts` known-answer, NOT the on-screen milestone (which also carries payroll/rent/savings; a maintainer diffing them would read a phantom regression); (P2-C) a quantitative server-path test added (`forecast-server.test.ts` drives `getCashFlowForecast('user-demo')` → Auto Loan ×3 @ −$385, closing the wiring coverage gap the text-only e2e left). ACCEPTED residuals (STATUS #134): (1) the narrow non-transfer-ACH double-count (a loan whose ACH is also a recurring checking row now doubles on forecast too — same population already doubling on the calendar, no new victims, no safe fix); (2) a day-31 loan anchored in a short month sticks a day early (pre-existing `expandScheduled` MONTHLY-clamp property, not demo-reachable, ≤1-day shift). | 152 | #127 live-ingest tail — two P2 edges closed. **(a) SHARED TICKER VALIDATOR:** the SimpleFIN holdings mapper and manual `addHolding` each carried their OWN copy of the ticker regex (the #127 audit flagged them as drift-prone coupled rules); extracted ONE `parseTicker`/`TICKER_RE` (`src/lib/engine/investments/ticker.ts`) used by both, and WIDENED the charset to accept "/" so slash share-class + crypto-pair tickers (BRK/B, BTC/USD) are no longer silently dropped by the sync mapper (and are now enterable manually). Gate (real 2026-07-02): verify GREEN **1570 / 124** (+7: parseTicker 5 + slash-holdings + epoch-boundary). | 161 | **Categorization LEARNS from repeated user corrections (passive learning — owner: "the categorization should learn from users' inputs; the user shouldn't have to recreate the wheel each time")**: today a `Correction` is per-transaction and consulted by NOTHING at categorize time — it only helps future rows if the user manually promotes it to an explicit "Always" rule (a step easy to miss, and BLOCKED outright for aggregates), so a descriptor the user re-files every sync ("credit card paid" / "check paid" -> transfer) never sticks and the 159-row triage inbox keeps re-serving it. New pure `deriveLearnedRules(corrections)` (src/lib/engine/categorize/learn.ts) turns a user's correction HISTORY into synthetic `RuleLike[]` ("learned rules") APPENDED — in the server loader (src/server/rules.ts `loadUserRules` = explicit ++ learned, ownership-scoped) — to the SAME `rules[]` array `categorize()` already applies at every ingest + backfill path; `categorize()` core is unchanged. Every learned rule keys on an IDENTITY-PRESERVING descriptor SIGNATURE (src/lib/engine/categorize/signature.ts `computeDescriptorSignature`): the raw descriptor with ONLY dates + money amounts stripped and everything that identifies the counterparty KEPT (account / phone / check / reference numbers). Two occurrences of the SAME payee therefore share one signature ("CREDIT CARD PAID 07/01" and "...08/01" -> "CREDIT CARD PAID"; "ZELLE PAYMENT TO LANDLORD" recurs verbatim) while two DIFFERENT payees NEVER do (a differing phone/account number is a different signature) — that STRUCTURAL property, not any word list, is the load-bearing over-generalization guard. Safety is EARNED BY REPETITION: a signature must be corrected to the SAME category >= LEARN_THRESHOLD (2) times across DISTINCT transactions with ZERO conflicts; the #44 sign guard holds at derive time AND at match time (`learnedSignOk` in pipeline.ts — an income-learned merchant's later refund/fee falls through to review, never books as income); a secondary `hasDistinguishingToken` guard refuses a genuinely payee-less residue ("CHECK PAID", "CARD PAYMENT"); latest-correction-wins folds undos and change-your-mind sequences. Computed ON THE FLY from the undoable, user-visible Correction rows — NO schema change, NO DB writes — so the demo seed (zero corrections) derives ZERO learned rules and every golden is BYTE-IDENTICAL; undo is automatic (it changes the history, so the derived rule updates or disappears on the next read). A learned rule auto-files at `LEARNED_RULE_CONFIDENCE_BPS` (8500, the FLAGGED band) with the visible "AI" badge + needsReview:false — a correctable guess, NOT the silent 9900 an explicit "Always" earns — which is the structural backstop bounding any over-broad learned signature to a VISIBLE guess, never a silent misfile. Also shipped the two owner-reported normalize misses: Google One -> software, Round1 (arcade) -> entertainment (normalize.ts KNOWN_MERCHANTS + adversarial-eval corpus rows). Gate (real, 2026-07-04): `bash scripts/verify.sh` -> VERIFY GREEN **1704 unit / 128 files** (+30 over the 1674/126 baseline: learn.test.ts known-answer canaries + hostile-critic regressions, learn-loader.test.ts drives the real recategorize action -> loadUserRules -> categorize chain on a throwaway user), tsc/eslint/next build clean; adversarial `eval:categorize` 100% auto-file precision / 0 confidently-wrong (43 descriptors, Google One + Round1 now auto-file). | Chose compute-on-the-fly learned rules appended to the existing `rules[]` over a schema/DB-materialized rule table: zero migration, zero golden movement (demo has 0 corrections), free undo (re-derive), and it reuses the tested `categorize()` rule path unchanged. Chose the identity-preserving signature over (a) the collapsed merchant CANONICAL — that is exactly what an explicit "Always" already does merchant-wide, and it cannot express date-fragmented or aggregate descriptors; and (b) an earlier strip-the-number-then-enumerate-channel-words design that cycle-1/2 hostile review DEFEATED by putting a phone number in the payee slot (the module header now calls that "an unwinnable whack-a-mole"). Hostile Checker — FOUR cycles (Workflow maker/checker, dimension critics -> refute-by-default adversarial verify of every P0/P1): **c1** 6 P0/P1 over-generalization (enumeration defeated by numeric payees; unguarded canonical branch; no match-time sign guard) -> adopted the identity-preserving signature ++ hasDistinguishingToken ++ match-time sign guard; **c2** 2 (enumeration still fragile on SEND/MONEY/BANKING; HMSHOST bucket canonical) -> REMOVED canonical mode entirely, so distinct payees are structurally un-mergeable regardless of wording; **c3** 1 P1 (payee-less generic labels DIRECT DEBIT / POINT OF SALE / SERVICE CHARGE / LOAN PAYMENT passed hasDistinguishingToken) -> extended NOISE_TOKENS ++ made learned rules auto-file with the AI badge so any missed label is a visible correctable guess, not silent; **c4 (final)** ripple dimension over the confidence/AI-badge change (backfill isUnsure, triage, accuracy, goldens, explicit-rule precedence) CLEAN with 0 findings, and 1 P1 reproduced end-to-end — bare payment-FREQUENCY / card-ENTRY labels ("AUTOMATIC PAYMENT <date>", "SCHEDULED/REGULAR/PERIODIC/GENERAL PAYMENT", "PIN PURCHASE") are a payee-less-AND-number-less residue with no number to keep distinct billers apart, so one biller's learned rule could transplant its category onto an unrelated biller's identical bare label -> FIXED by extending NOISE_TOKENS with the 11 common payment-frequency adjectives ++ card-entry modes (AUTOMATIC/AUTOMATED/AUTO/SCHEDULED/REGULAR/PERIODIC/PREARRANGED/GENERAL/STANDARD/PIN/SIGNATURE), each verified brand-safe (GENERAL MOTORS->MOTORS, AUTOMATIC DATA PROCESSING->DATA, SIGNATURE PROPERTIES->PROPERTIES survive) -> **0 P0/P1 after the fix**. | 169 | post-5 | **Triage accuracy metric recovers on undo** (#166/#168 open follow-up (e), resumed on "continue"). Filing a review row stamps its `CategoryPrediction.actualCategoryId` as ground truth (DECISIONS #37) so the /triage accuracy card can score predicted-vs-actual; but `undoCorrections` restored the transaction to `needsReview:true` and removed any minted rule while NEVER clearing `actualCategoryId`, so `getCategorizationAccuracy` (counts predictions WHERE actualCategoryId is not null) kept counting a decision the user retracted and the displayed accuracy never recovered after undo — the exact "file an ambiguous group -> accuracy drops -> undo doesn't restore it" symptom. Fix = ONE write inside the existing per-correction `$transaction`: `categoryPrediction.updateMany({ where:{ transactionId: correction.transactionId, userId }, data:{ actualCategoryId: null } })`, atomic with the inverse-correction insert + transaction restore + transfer-pin + rule cleanup. The invariant is now symmetric with the four filing writes (applyCategory / applyToAllSimilar / fileMerchantGroup / recategorize): a `needsReview` row carries NO confirmed label. `undoSplit` deliberately UNCHANGED — `splitTransaction` sets categoryId=null and never labels a prediction (its children are brand-new rows with no CategoryPrediction), critic-verified. Nulling removes the sample from BOTH numerator and denominator, but only for the retracted transaction and only on a real undo, so it cannot dishonestly inflate accuracy (the sample nulled is exactly the one this correction stamped; triage rows are unlabeled at file time). Fresh-context hostile Critic (adversarial, refute-by-default): scoping (transactionId @unique -> <=1 row; userId session-trusted), over-revert (null is the ONLY consistent label for a restored review row — restoring a prior label would be the bug), undoSplit, undo-funnel completeness (only 'corrections'/'split' undo kinds; recategorize/applyToAllSimilar have no undo path bypassing undoCorrections), idempotency/atomicity (alreadyUndone continue + undoesId P2002 rollback), golden-safety, metric-honesty — ALL CLEAR, **0 P0/P1/P2**. Gate (real output 2026-07-07): `VERIFY_E2E=1 bash scripts/verify.sh` -> VERIFY GREEN — tsc/eslint clean, **1845 unit / 136 files** (+2/+1 over #168: tests/unit/accuracy-undo.test.ts), build clean, **FULL e2e 76/76 (47.7s)** incl. the existing "accuracy card shows a measured value (DECISIONS #37)" spec. Fail-old/pass-new PROVEN by stash-run: with the fix stashed the new test fails 2/2 (label stays 'dining' after undo, and the un-nulled sample even contaminates the sibling test's count 2!=1); restored -> 2/2 pass. | The accuracy card is the app's one honest "the AI is improving" signal (LOOP prime directive: the AI never originates a fact, every claim traces to ground truth); a metric that drops on a filing and then LIES by staying dropped after the user takes the filing back undermines exactly that. The fix restores the file<->undo symmetry the four filing writes already assumed, and the critic confirmed nulling (not prior-label-restore) is the correct semantics because the row returns to undecided. |

SW v4 gains `push` + `notificationclick` (still NO fetch handler — the #166→#170 abort class is untouched). | 182 | post-5 | **Multi-device session invalidation + PII-free deletion record** (Competitive-Gap Gap 6 §3, the two items PRIVACY.md §Deletion listed as deferred "real-auth release" limitations). Resumed on "continue" in the Fable lane; a full-codebase reconciliation first found the plan STALE (Cash Flow Radar / web push / weekly digest were already BUILT — plan annotated, see COMPETITIVE_GAP_PLAN §2 reconciliation), leaving Gap 6 §3 as the highest-value UNBLOCKED, in-session-verifiable, rule-3 slice. MECHANISM: a per-user `User.sessionEpoch` (Int @default(0), golden-safe) stamped into the JWT at sign-in and re-checked on every Node-side session resolution. Pure decision core `isSessionCurrent(dbEpoch|null, tokenEpoch?)` in `engine/auth/session.ts` (strict `===`, missing user → false = fail-closed; absent tokenEpoch → 0) + `hashUserRef` (salted sha256), both unit-pinned against independently-computed vectors. Node enforcement in `server/session-guard.ts` (`currentSessionEpoch` + `isSessionEpochCurrent`, one DB source so stamp and check can't diverge), called from a Node `session`-callback override in auth.ts that strips `user` on a stale/absent epoch → `requireUserId` throws Unauthorized on EVERY device. Edge middleware stays Prisma-free (coarse route gate only; all DATA access re-resolves through the enforced Node callback, so a stale token passing middleware leaks nothing). Triggers: (a) `revokeOtherSessions()` bumps the epoch (Settings → "Sign out of all devices"; signs out this device too — honest "everywhere", no password-change flow exists yet to hook); (b) account deletion (user gone → existence check fails everywhere, free). DELETION RECORD: new `DeletionRecord` model with NO User relation (survives the cascade), holding only `hashUserRef(id)` + timestamp — written ATOMICALLY with `user.delete` via array-form `$transaction` so it exists IFF the deletion committed. Hashed with a SECRET salt (AUTH_SECRET, else DELETION_REF_SALT) so low-entropy ids (a Google id embeds an email) aren't dictionary-enumerable. FRESH-CONTEXT HOSTILE CRITIC (Fable, refute-by-default): **cycle 1 FAIL — 1 P0, 2 P1, all FIXED, then re-verified**. **P0-1 (CONFIRMED, serious):** demo + Google tokens were minted at a hardcoded epoch 0 (demo authorize returns none; Google edge-jwt returns early), so one "sign out of all devices" would BRICK those accounts — a fresh sign-in re-minted epoch 0 ≠ the bumped DB epoch → infinite redirect (violates CLAUDE.md rule 4). FIX: dropped the edge/authorize epoch stamp entirely; a Node `jwt` override now stamps `token.epoch = currentSessionEpoch(sub)` from the DB at sign-in for EVERY provider uniformly, so re-sign-in always reads the current epoch. Regression-locked by a round-trip test (revoke → old token dead → fresh stamp == bumped epoch → valid). **P1-1 (CONFIRMED):** deletion record + delete were non-atomic (a failed delete left a false "deleted" record) → wrapped in `$transaction`. **P1-2 (CONFIRMED, coverage):** the stamp↔check seam was untested → added the round-trip regression above (mechanically catches P0-1). **P2s:** deletion hash now keyed by AUTH_SECRET (was public-salt-enumerable for Google ids); overclaimed "non-enumerable" comments softened to honest "pseudonymous unless secret salt"; per-request findUnique accepted (indexed PK, negligible beside the per-render snapshot load, consistent with the codebase); `db push` migration convention noted (Postgres deploy must push before the new code runs, else the authorize/guard 500s on a column-short DB). Gate (real 2026-07-08): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → ✅ VERIFY GREEN — tsc/eslint clean, **2010 unit / 150 files** (+2 files: session-lifecycle 8 + session-invalidation, incl. real-DB revoke/delete/round-trip), build clean; touched e2e `account-deletion.spec.ts` 2/2 (adds a render-only Sessions-control assertion — never clicks revoke, which would bump the shared demo epoch). Full `VERIFY_E2E=1` still can't exit 0 on this Windows machine (documented mobile-380 viewport flake). | The two deferred PRIVACY limitations were the only genuine security/data-integrity gaps left unbuilt-and-unblocked (error tracking is env-gated, backups owner-gated), and they were real: today a deleted or compromised session keeps working until JWT expiry. Choosing the epoch mechanism over DB-strategy sessions avoided ripping out the deliberate no-adapter design (#43, the Account-model name collision). Enforcing at the Node data boundary rather than the edge keeps middleware Prisma-free while still protecting all data. The critic's P0 was exactly the kind of silent, provider-specific bug the hostile-critic gate exists to catch — the demo account (CLAUDE.md rule 4) would have bricked on first use — and unifying epoch-stamping to a single DB-sourced Node path made the mechanism both correct and simpler. The PII-free record closes the compliance gap without weakening the "nothing recoverable is retained" promise, and atomicity keeps the record honest. |
