ACCEPTED P2 (documented, STATUS): Plaid's per-item success write keeps the same merged-write shape — the identical self-healing edge is left as-is because the Plaid live path is UNVERIFIED (no sandbox creds) and adding control-flow to that untested loop for a self-healing edge is disproportionate; the SimpleFIN (verifiable) path is airtight. TESTS: pure engine (classify/select/copy, incl. "very old attempt + NO error is still ok, never broken" and "message never echoes the raw reason"); `safeSyncErrorReason` fuzz incl. a credential-URL-bearing error → allow-listed reason, never the URL; SimpleFIN mocked-server failure→persist→clear round-trip (asserts sanitized reason + last-good date preserved + no credential leak in the returned message); real-server `getConnectionAlerts` (both providers, clear-silences, ownership isolation); e2e render+axe of the alert for a throwaway broken connection + the demo count-0 negative. The honest way to surface a "broken connection" was to first PERSIST a real failure signal rather than infer one from staleness — inferring "broken" from a quiet feed would manufacture a fact the app can't observe, the exact cardinal-rule violation this codebase exists to avoid. Separating the freshness question (data age) from the connection-state question (did a sync fail) keeps two independent, individually-honest claims instead of one overloaded heuristic. SAFETY: only the flagged/review boundary moves — `aiBadge` still compares against the global `AUTO_SILENT_BPS`, so no flaggedBps value can create a new SILENT filing (test-locked for absurd inputs). Accepted P2s: live committed labels are corrections-biased (miss-heavy) until an explicit confirm surface exists, so live tuning mostly TIGHTENS (the safe direction — loosening needs positive evidence, and inferring hits from silence would fabricate labels); one extra indexed findMany per categorize read path. | The audit's loop-4 gate demanded exactly this shape: bounded, recompute-from-scratch, auto-revert, user-visible. Thin server `server/return-moment.ts` reads `lastSeenDate`, measures the civil-day gap via `daysBetween`, stamps today (only when changed → no write amplification; skips the count query below the threshold), and — on a real return — counts silently-auto-filed predictions (`confidenceBps >= AUTO_SILENT_BPS`, `createdAt >= previous-visit midnight-UTC`, userId-scoped) then calls the engine with the page's already-fetched `coach.review`/`coach.opportunities`/`radar.radar` (NO re-fetch, NO new money math). Client `ReturnMomentCard` (dismissable; also self-retires once the visit stamps today) renders under THE cash-needed answer. Maker→Checker (proportionate inline pass — display-only + benign last-seen write, no money/authz/routing): no P0/P1; accepted P2s — the auto-filed `since` boundary is midnight-UTC-approximate (a count, not money, errs toward inclusion); card copy not yet in the guardrail-scan set (trends-copy precedent); positive card RENDER not browser-tested (proven by engine + integration tests — the shared fixed-today demo user can't seed a >7-day gap without racing the auto-stamp). | 192 | post-5 | **Cross-provider duplicate-account guard (advisory).** The app has NO cross-provider dedup — Plaid/SimpleFIN/manual each mint their own `Account` row and `Transaction` dedup is `@@unique([accountId, providerRef])` (account+provider scoped), so the SAME real account via two providers double-counts in net worth/spending/cash-needed (verified against the ingest + `netWorthSeries` paths). New pure engine `engine/account/duplicates.ts` (`detectDuplicateAccounts`) flags cross-provider pairs that share account `type` + `currency` AND ≥1 signal (matching last-4 → high, identical non-zero balance → high, shared distinctive name token → medium); `demo`/seed rows never compared; zero-balance name-less pairs never flagged (false-positive guard). Surfaced as a **display-only** amber `role="alert"` card on /accounts (`duplicate-accounts-warning`) — never auto-deletes (which side to keep is the user's call; they disconnect via existing flows). Wired through `getAccountsView` over the currency-guarded `supported` set so the warning never references a hidden row. Matching is heuristic by necessity: SimpleFIN carries no mask, so no exact cross-provider identity key exists. Gate (real 2026-07-09): `bash scripts/verify.sh` → ✅ VERIFY GREEN — tsc/eslint clean, **2085 unit / 158 files** (+14/+1: 12 known-answer engine + 2 real-`getAccountsView` integration incl. demo-user=0 golden-safety), build clean. | Prompted by the owner's "will running both be redundant?" — the honest answer is worse than redundant (silent 2× money math), and the app happily lets you double-link with no guardrail. A detector that warns but never mutates fits the coaching guardrails (surface the issue, let the user decide) and the no-fabrication ethos (a doubled balance is a fabricated net worth). Advisory-not-destructive because the fix is a judgment call (which provider to keep) and auto-merge across providers with no shared key would risk deleting the wrong side. |

Three additive tables (`Household`, `HouseholdMember` with `@@unique(userId)` = one household per user in v1, `HouseholdInvite` with hashed one-time code) + one additive Boolean `Account.sharedToHousehold @default(false)`. All 41 existing actions keep their `userId` authz untouched (~7 new household actions added); the only widened read reach is one central unit-tested `visibleAccountsWhere` helper used by exactly four v1 surfaces (/accounts shared section, register shared rows, cards/calendar household dues, dashboard joint cash-needed toggle) — coach/reports/budgets/goals/digest/dials/personalization stay per-partner (the audit's relational-shame fix is scope, not copy). Joint cash-needed = pure `mergeSnapshots()` over the viewer's snapshot + per-partner `getSharedSnapshotSlice` whose every query carries the share predicate in its `where` (unshared rows never enter memory); the Cash-Needed Engine itself is untouched (already pure/userId-free). Departure/deletion lifecycle is deterministic LAZY REPAIR at read (`requireViewer` self-heals ownerless households, memberless ones unreachable + lazily reaped) — NOT transactional choreography, because `deleteMyData` is deliberately an array-form `$transaction` (#46) and `Household` has no FK path from `User`. Invite acceptance = out-of-band code (hashed, attempt-capped) AND DB-row email match (never session claims; allowlist explicitly not trusted — dormant by default). Demo/golden-safe by construction: demo user never in a household; `'mine'` scope degenerates to today's exact semantics (degeneracy unit). Rejected: tenant householdId-on-User (all-or-nothing sharing, 41-action blast radius, backfill), pure per-account ACL (no "we" anchor for joint surfaces), shared login (destroys audit/dials/epoch). Open owner questions (non-blocking for slices 1–3): partner triage on shared accounts (v2 fork), household digest vs per-partner, naming. | Owner green-lit household (#196) and said schedule the spike early — authz retrofit cost only grows (audit §2b #4, Persona B). Additive-visibility beats re-tenanting because the codebase's uniform per-user ownership discipline is its strongest safety property: v1 grants NO cross-user writes, so the entire existing authz surface is preserved, and the one new read path is a single audited helper. Read-only sharing ships Persona B's actual ask (Jen sees it; "what do WE need by Friday") while deferring the genuinely hard two-teachers learning problem with its design direction recorded. The hostile-critic cycle earned its cost: all five P1s were real design flaws (two would have shipped data-lifecycle orphans, one a vocabulary leak, one an invite takeover path, one a single-line-of-defense confidentiality boundary) caught before a line of product code exists. |

Fixing them before commit, rather than shipping and discovering them via a support ticket about a partner's recategorization "not sticking," is exactly what routing this to a fresh-context Fable critic is for. |

A member with a LIVE partner now receives ONE household-scope weekly digest instead of the personal one: dues computed at household scope via the slice-4 merge (getCashNeeded(user, PAY_IN_FULL, household)), plus a shared-account movement summary and the SS4.4 assumptions copy. New pure engine src/lib/engine/household/digest.ts (summarizeSharedMovement: inclusive window, excludes transfers + split parents + PENDING, the same exclusion set as coach.ts/radar.ts so a shared total can never disagree with the register). New server read src/server/household-digest.ts (getHouseholdDigestContext), Prisma-only like household-finance.ts so the cron import graph never pulls in NextAuth. buildWeeklyDigest gains an optional household field that flips the subject + dues header and appends the movement + assumptions block; absent it, the personal digest is byte-identical (T6, deep-equality locked). Joint copy lives in HOUSEHOLD_COPY and the guardrail scan is now EXHAUSTIVE over that object keys, so no household string can ship unscanned. Dedup key deliberately UNCHANGED (weekly_digest:<monday>), so joining or leaving a household mid-week can never yield a second digest that week. DEVIATION from a literal reading of #201(b), recorded deliberately: the joint email is composed PER RECIPIENT, not as one byte-identical message to both partners. | A byte-identical joint email is not constructible. Household scope is viewer-relative BY DEFINITION in SS4.4 (your accounts + accounts your partner has SHARED), so the two members honest household answers differ. The only two ways to make one identical email are (1) build it from the union of ALL both members accounts, which leaks a partner UNSHARED card and violates T1, the hard privacy invariant, or (2) build it only from the SHARED accounts, which silently drops each member OWN private-card dues from their weekly overview. (CORRECTED by the slice-7 hostile critic, finding F2: that second leg was overstated as "would cause missed payments". The standalone reminders cron still emails every user their OWN dues at 'mine' scope, so a private-card REMINDER is not lost from the product, only from the weekly overview. The binding constraint is the other leg: the personal Money Review and the receipts tally reach members ONLY through the digest, and neither can enter a byte-identical joint email without either leaking one partner analysis to the other (SS4.5) or dropping it entirely.) The critic also raised, and this slice FIXED, a P1 of its own: household-scope dues were rendering a partner card through the second-person reminderLine ("you'll pay $600.00 yourself", "keep the funds in your account"), telling a member they must personally pay their partner card and inviting a double payment. Partner-owned dues now render through HOUSEHOLD_COPY.digestPartnerDue: owner-attributed, stating whose account it sits on, and explicitly saying Aimplifi does not decide who pays. The owner-label map covers every shared account of any TYPE (a shared LOAN reaches the digest via loanObligations and would otherwise fall back to the same false second-person line). Per-recipient composition satisfies both: no leak, no dropped reminder, and the one genuinely symmetric section (shared-account movement, computed over every member shared accounts) IS identical in both inboxes, which is the substance of what one joint digest was asking for. The Money Review stays personal (SS4.5): it reaches only its own subject inbox and is disclosed inline as personal so nobody assumes a partner is reading their coaching. Movement copy is descriptive only (a count and two totals, no merchant, no partner attribution, no budget comparison), because SS4.5 relational-shame guardrail must hold in the one place both partners read the same words. Accepted limitations, both meaning "both partners receive it" is a default rather than a guarantee (critic F4): (a) a member who owns ZERO accounts is still skipped by the sweep no-accounts guard even if a partner shares cards with them, because resolvePaymentAccount throws on an empty snapshot and a funding account must be the viewer OWN (slice-4 P0); (b) a member with no Money Review and nothing due gets no email that week, since household movement is deliberately never a send trigger on its own (parity with the receipts tally) - so one partner can receive the joint digest in a week the other does not. A non-USD shared account is withheld from every figure by the #135 currency guard but is now COUNTED and DISCLOSED in the email (critic F3), never silently dropped. Inherited and NOT fixed here (critic F5, routed to slice 8): if two partners each connect the SAME real bank account through different providers and both share it, no guard fires - the #192 duplicate detector input is each owner OWN account set - so movement and dues double-count it. The digest is the first surface to mail that doubled number. |
| 221 | post-5 | **Slice 8 full-surface household hostile critic (T1-T12): 3 fresh-context Fable critics (authz state machine / read-visibility+mutation boundary / money surfaces) found 0 P0, 7 P1, 10 P2 — all P1s fixed, all actionable P2s fixed, verify green.** Key decisions bundled here: (1) F-5 same-real-account-connected-twice is mitigated by DETECTION + DISCLOSURE (detectHouseholdDuplicateAccounts: personal #192 signals with the same-provider skip relaxed to same-provider-AND-same-owner; advisory pairs on the scope toggle across dashboard/cards/calendar and one line in the joint digest) — the figures are deliberately NOT adjusted, because the heuristic has false positives and silently dropping a REAL account from money math is worse than a disclosed possible double-count; a fail-old test locks the merged snapshot still containing both twins. (2) F-3 household headline: the ATTRIBUTION was fixed, not the number — 'Needs $X across <household>' replaces 'in <viewer account>' at household scope, with the autopay-drafts-from-partner sentence added to scopeAssumptions; subtracting partner autopay from the engine projection was rejected as riskier arithmetic for the same honesty gain. (3) F-9 cron digest: household reads degrade to the PERSONAL digest (audited digest.household.degraded), dues+context succeeding or degrading TOGETHER so household-scope reminders can never render without owner labels. (4) In-app partner-due copy (F-1/F-2) reuses the digestPartnerDue stance via new HOUSEHOLD_COPY keys, all under the exhaustive guardrail scan + the partner-due ban. (5) Critic A-F3 recorded as accepted design: an invite from a member who later departs stays redeemable into the household (standard team-invite semantics; the code factor still gates). | Slice-8 charter (HOUSEHOLD_ARCHITECTURE §5.8): fresh-context pass over T1-T12 with fixtures in place; REGRESSION_LEDGER entries added per finding (2026-07-12). The recurring disease across F-1..F-4/F-6: household scope bolted onto surfaces whose copy and disclosure assumed everything on screen is the viewer's own — the digest fix (slice 7) had cured email only. |
| 222 | TASKS 2.1 | **Ask conversation frame — deterministic ellipsis resolution.** New pure `src/lib/engine/assistant/frame.ts`: `frameFromIntent(intent)` distils the previous turn's RESOLVED intent into a slot frame `{kind, timeframe, target, merchant, limit}`; `resolveEllipsis(question, today, frame, custom)` rebuilds the same intent with the one slot the fragment names swapped ("what about last month?" / "and groceries?" / "at Costco?"). **Consulted ONLY when `parseAssistantQuery` returns `unknown`**, so a self-sufficient question is never re-interpreted and every pre-existing route is byte-identical with or without a frame. It originates nothing: each slot comes from the already-validated frame or from the user's own words via the parser's OWN helpers (`parseExplicitTimeframe`, `resolveSpendTarget`, `extractMerchantPhrase`) — `parseTimeframe` was split so "names no timeframe" is distinguishable from "says this month", and the merchant tokenizer + the #168 payment-method guard are now shared, never re-implemented. **Session state lives in the answer, not the server:** `AssistantAnswer.intent` is echoed to the client and handed back on the next ask, so there is no table, no session store, and no cross-tab coupling; the returning value is UNTRUSTED and goes through the same `validateIntent` gate the LLM's proposals do before it becomes a frame (a forged frame can only re-ask a question about the caller's own ownership-scoped data). Order is parser → frame → LLM, so a deterministic answer always beats a model call (rule 5). Frame-resolved asks ARE written to the UnknownQuestion ledger but tagged `frame:<kind>`: the phrasing is context-DEPENDENT, so TASKS 2.3 must never mine it into a context-free vocab rule, while a mis-resolution still leaves a trail. Deliberate NON-support: "same for Amex" abstains — #168 established that payment methods are not merchants, and the frame reuses that guard rather than contradicting it. | Audit §3 idea #2 (impact 9): the assistant had amnesia — every follow-up had to restate the whole question. Pure parser state beats a conversational LLM here: it is deterministic, testable, free, and it cannot invent a figure. |
| 223 | TASKS 2.1 | **Frame hostile-critic cycle 1 (fresh-context Fable): FAIL — 3 P1 + 4 P2, all fixed in-cycle.** The P1s were all the same disease — the frame answering a question the user did not ask: (1) a timeframe-FIRST fragment ("and this month at costco?") silently dropped the merchant and answered the carried category; (2) negation was unmodelled, so "restaurants not groceries" answered GROCERIES (synonym order is first-match-wins); (3) the assistant's own vocabulary became merchant probes ("what about income?" → "No spending at Income"), which also STOLE the question from the LLM classifier that routes it correctly. Fixes: merchant search anywhere in the fragment; NEGATION_WORDS abstain (a LEADING "no," stays a correction cue); INTENT_NOUNS + PRONOUNS abstain on the merchant path; QUESTION_WORDS ("why", "should", "vs", "is") abstain the whole fragment before ANY slot extraction (P2-4 — the guard had existed only on the merchant path); `largest_purchases` dropped from SPEND_FAMILY, because "what about at Costco?" after "my biggest purchase" means the biggest purchase AT Costco, which no engine computes — abstain beats silently answering a merchant TOTAL (P2-5); `validateIntent` now bounds month (01–12), label (≤40) and merchant (≤64) — it had never been a client-facing boundary before this slice (P2-6); a CARRIED deictic window is re-labelled against today, since "this month" framed in June is a false name in July, though the window itself never moves (P2-7). Every guard fails SAFE: a false hit abstains to the honest `unknown` and the LLM rescue still gets its turn. Accepted limitations: one-turn memory (an intervening `unknown` clears the frame); no comparison intent exists, so "compared to last month?" answers a lone figure; a novel non-category noun ("what about my raise?") can still read as a merchant and answer "No spending at raise" — exactly what the parser does today for the same word, so the frame introduces no new sin. | The critic earned its keep: all three P1s were confident-wrong money answers that the 29 happy-path tests missed, and each is now locked by a fail-old regression test. |
| 224 | TASKS 2.1 | **Frame hostile-critic cycle 2 (same critic, re-executed repros): PASS — 0 P0, 0 P1.** All 3 P1s and all 4 P2s from cycle 1 confirmed CLOSED by re-run repros; a 19-case false-abstain sweep found no legitimate ellipsis broken by the new guards. Two new P2s found and fixed: (a) **`validateIntent` derived nothing about `target.label`** — a client-echoed frame could label the TRAVEL group "Groceries" and put a true figure under a false name in a money headline, so labels are now RE-DERIVED from the target's own identity (`canonicalTargetLabel`: system name from `CATEGORY_BY_ID`, the user's own name for a custom id, the synonym table's phrasing for a group, and a closed-set match for an umbrella's id set — an unrecognised umbrella is rejected, not relabelled); (b) a stray "at" manufactured merchants ("at least", "at work"). Also fixed P3s: "save"/"cut"/"back"/"high"/"low" left the question-word guard (each was redundant against the interrogatives, and each is a real store-name word — "and at Save Mart?" must resolve), and a carried TRAILING window is now re-named once today leaves it ("the last 3 months" → "April 2026 – June 2026"). ACCEPTED, not fixed: the open-vocabulary merchant tail — "what about my 401k?" still reads as a merchant and answers "No spending at 401k", which is (i) a self-disclosing $0, never a wrong figure, (ii) byte-identical to what the shipped parser already does for "how much did I spend at 401k", and (iii) now observable in the ledger as `frame:merchant_spend` rather than silent. A denylist can only cover what someone thought of; the honest fix (require the merchant to exist in the user's own transactions) would put a data read inside a pure engine, so it waits for a deliberate design. | The second cycle earned its keep too: the label-derivation hole was the same class as the cycle-1 P2-6 fix, in the same validator, and the first pass missed it. |
| 225 | TASKS 2.3 | **Learned vocabulary — the weekly mining loop (audit §4.2 loop 2).** New additive `VocabEntry` (per-user, `@@unique([userId, phrase])`, cascade) + pure `src/lib/engine/vocab/vocab.ts` (`normalizePhrase`, `matchVocab`, `mineVocab`) + Prisma-only `src/server/vocab.ts` + weekly `/api/cron/vocab` (Mon 16:00). **The load-bearing safety property: an entry supplies an intent KIND and nothing else.** Every parameter — timeframe, category, merchant, amount, age — is re-derived from the asker's own words by `intentFromKind` and re-validated by `validateIntent`, which is byte-for-byte the contract the LLM classifier has lived under since #75. The vocab layer is therefore exactly as powerful as the model route it replaces, minus the model call: a wrong entry can route to a wrong KIND, and can never inject a figure, a window, or a category. Routing order is parser → frame → **vocab** → LLM (deterministic beats a model call, rule 5), consulted ONLY on a parser-`unknown`, so every existing route is byte-identical and the demo (which learns nothing, see #226) is untouched. **The key** is the PII-scrubbed question: the scrub already masks digits and amounts, so one key spans "retire at 60" and "retire at 67" — exactly the spans the answer path re-derives. **PER-USER by construction, a deliberate deviation from the audit's "≥N distinct users" clustering:** a phrase is user-authored text that has been scrubbed, not anonymized (names, employers and clinics survive), so pooling it would copy one person's language into another's routing table and onto their screen; the audit's own "/asks" branch is satisfiable per-user, and a wrong global rule has N users' blast radius instead of one. **The ladder:** shadow (minted from ≥3 unanimous INDEPENDENT rescues; not served, accruing held-out evidence) → flagged (≥2 held-out agreements, zero disagreements; served with the same "I interpreted your question" hedge an LLM answer carries) → active (≥2 disclosed serves, no rejection; served, disclosed as learned) → retired (TERMINAL tombstone). **Self-confirmation is structurally impossible:** rows the loop's own entries resolved are tagged `vocab:<kind>` in the UnknownQuestion ledger and count ONLY as `served`, never as evidence, so a bare kind always means an independent resolution; `frame:`-tagged rows (#222) make a phrase permanently ineligible, since a context-free rule for "what about last month?" would be a bug. Every count is recomputed from the ledger each run, never incremented. Undo is first-class and terminal: "Not what I meant" on the answer, "Forget this" on Settings → AI trust, and the miner can never re-mint a retired phrase from the evidence the user just rejected. | Audit §4.2 loop 2: the parser gets smarter every week with zero deploys, and — because the rule is deterministic and free — it keeps working with no LLM key at all, no 7s timeout, and no rate limit. Whole-question keys rather than the audit's n-gram clusters: an n-gram rule can fire on a question it was never mined from, which is precisely the audit's own named failure mode ("promoting a rule that would have mis-routed a later question"), and there is no real ledger data yet to tune a clustering threshold against. Learn the narrow thing that is provably safe now; earn the general one later, on evidence. |
| 226 | TASKS 2.3 | **Vocab hostile-critic cycle 1 (3 fresh-context Fable critics in parallel — routing/money, the learning loop, authz+privacy): FAIL — 0 P0, 5 P1, 11 P2. All P1s and every actionable P2 fixed in-cycle and regression-locked.** **P1-1 (privacy, the worst): the shared demo account learned.** The demo login is credential-free and ONE-CLICK, so every anonymous visitor is the same user row — the miner would have mined one stranger's typed question ("can melissa and i afford ivf at …") and rendered it in the next visitor's /settings, under copy promising it is shared with no one. Fixed: `learningDisabled()` short-circuits mine/serve/list for `DEMO_USER_ID`, which moved to a NextAuth-free `src/lib/demo-user.ts` so the cron import graph stays clean (#220); the household feature had already fenced the demo user off for the same reason (#210), and this is now the second instance of the rule *anything that accumulates a user's own INPUT and shows it back must opt the demo user out*. **P1-2: `VocabEntry` was an undisclosed store** — PRIVACY.md now describes it and lists it in the deletion cascade. **P1-3: the miner could clobber a tombstone.** It reads, decides, then writes; a "Forget this" landing inside that window was overwritten by the stale decision — and since the rejection lives nowhere else, it was gone for good, the entry resumed serving, and it self-promoted. Fixed with `status: { not: 'retired' }` in the write filter: a tombstone always wins the race. **P1-4: the loop went blind at the exact moment it started affecting answers.** A served entry short-circuits the LLM, so no independent row about that phrase could ever be written again — held-out evidence froze, flagged→active promoted on the entry's OWN serves, and the "one disagreement retires it" rule was unreachable. That fails audit constitution (d)+(e) outright. Fixed with `auditServableEntries()`: once a week, off the answer path, every SERVED phrase is replayed against the classifier that never sees the rule, and a disagreement retires it. Fails safe both ways — no key or any error means no opinion and no change, and a FALSE retire costs the user nothing (the question simply returns to the route it had before it was ever learned), which is why one disagreement is enough. **P1-5 (pre-existing, in the parser): "how much did I spend at 星巴克 last month" answered the ALL-SPENDING TOTAL, undisclosed** — both #166 abstain guards are ASCII-only, so a store named in a non-Latin script tokenized to nothing ("at 星巴克" → the total) or was silently mangled ("with Zürich Café" → merchant "zrich"). A true figure under a false question: the cardinal sin, in a file this slice touches. Now abstains before the tokenizer sees it. **P2s fixed:** the learned kind must ROUND-TRIP (`[num]` masking collapses "pay off my car by 2027" and "…by 65" into one key, and `intentFromKind` silently degrades `debt_free_by_date` → `debt_payoff` when no date parses — so a rule mined from the date form was answering the age form with a different question; it now abstains); `normalizePhrase` refuses any non-ASCII key (normalization would DELETE it, collapsing questions that name different people into one rule); the held-out boundary is now DATA-derived (`evidenceThrough` = the newest supporting row) instead of the miner's wall clock, which clock skew could otherwise turn into self-validation; `MAX_ENTRIES_PER_USER = 200` caps the routing table; the mint `catch` no longer swallows anything but P2002; the e2e fixture refuses to run outside the throwaway e2e database. **ACCEPTED, not fixed:** `status` is a durable ratchet — an entry whose supporting rows age out of the 2000-row mining window keeps serving with recomputed-to-zero counts. Deliberate (a rule that passed its gates should not be un-learned because the ledger scrolled), and safe because every DOWNWARD path stays open forever on no evidence budget at all: user rejection, held-out disagreement, a `frame:` row, and the weekly independent re-check can each retire a serving entry at any time. | Three parallel critics with different lenses was the right call: the routing critic proved the kind-only claim held, and then the OTHER two found what it could not see — that the loop's back half (undo durability, post-flag monitoring, demo privacy) was where the design actually leaked. Every P1 was invisible from inside the feature's own happy path, and two came with failing repros. |
| 227 | TASKS 2.3 | **Vocab hostile-critic cycle 2 (fresh context, every cycle-1 repro re-executed): all 11 cycle-1 findings CONFIRMED CLOSED — and 1 NEW P1, in the cycle-1 FIX itself.** The non-ASCII abstention guard tested the first whitespace token after the preposition, but `extractMerchantPhrase` skips leading articles (`the`/`a`/`an`/`my`) and reads up to four tokens — so guard and tokenizer disagreed about where the merchant starts, and the cardinal sin was one article away: **"how much did I spend at THE 星巴克 last month" still answered the ALL-SPENDING TOTAL**, and "at the café zurich" still mangled to merchant "caf zurich". Root cause fixed rather than patched: both now walk ONE shared token stream (`merchantTokenStream`), so a guard can never again inspect different input than its subject. The same fix closed a P2 the cycle-1 guard had CAUSED: a curly apostrophe (the iOS keyboard default) is non-ASCII but carries no meaning, so "at mcdonald’s" had started returning `unknown` — silently breaking every phone-typed possessive store name, with no LLM rescue possible because `merchant_spend` is not in the classifier's closed set. Smart quotes are now folded to ASCII before the readability test. Two more P2s fixed: the weekly re-check retired a serving rule SILENTLY (indistinguishable at rest from the user's own undo) and now writes `vocab.retired.recheck` with the model's verdict — constitution (a) covers un-learning too; and PRIVACY.md promised a description of the AI routing service that **did not exist anywhere** (the raw-question send had never been disclosed at all, since #75), so a "What leaves the app" section now states exactly what is sent, when, and that nothing is sent without a key — and the settings copy's literal claim "Nothing here is shared with anyone else" (falsified by the weekly re-check's own outbound call) was corrected to "never shared with another person". P3s: `maxDuration = 300` on the one cron that makes outbound calls. ACCEPTED, not fixed: an audit-retire is TERMINAL like a user's rejection, so one stochastic misclassification permanently forecloses a phrase — the cost is that the question returns to the LLM route it had before it was ever learned (zero user-visible harm), and the asymmetry against a false PROMOTE (a true figure under a false question) justifies the bias; it is now at least visible in the audit trail rather than silent. Retired tombstones and the demo user's UnknownQuestion ledger both grow without bound (storage hygiene; nothing is rendered from either). | Cycle 2 earned its keep the hard way: it found that **my fix for cycle 1's cardinal-sin P1 did not actually fix it**, and that the fix had broken working input on the way past. Two lessons, both now in the ledger: a guard must walk the same input as the thing it guards, and a safety abstention that widens beyond its target is a regression wearing a safety jacket. |
| 228 | TASKS 2.3 / 2.6 | **Vocab critic cycles 3 and 4 — the 4-CYCLE CAP was reached on a PRE-EXISTING parser defect, and the remaining findings are ESCALATED (docs/STATUS.md §OPEN, TASKS 2.6).** The vocab slice itself PASSES: cycle 4's sweep found no P0/P1 in the vocab engine, server, cron or UI. What kept failing was the Ask parser's spend family, which this slice touches. Cycle 3 broke the cycle-2 fix (the stop-word test ran on the STRIPPED token, and "星巴克last" strips to "last", a timeframe cue — so the scan ended before the guard saw the raw bytes) and found the conversation FRAME had no guard at all. Cycle 4 broke the cycle-3 fix twice more: an object made ENTIRELY of glyphs the tokenizer deletes ("at ⓒⓞⓢⓣⓒⓞ", "on 🍕") is not a LETTER, so the deliberately-narrow predicate said nothing about it and the object vanished into the all-spending total; and DECOMPOSED "café" (NFD) matched the `cafe` synonym — because a combining mark is not a word char — answering ALL coffee-shop spending for a question about one store, while the composed spelling of the same question abstained. **Cycle 4's diagnosis is the decision recorded here: `spend_total` is the unconditional SINK of the spending family, and every guard is a filter scoped to one sentence shape, so each hardening moved the bug one syntactic inch.** Fixed accordingly, not with a fifth filter: readability is now a PRECONDITION of routing (the check runs before `resolveSpendTarget`, so an unreadable object cannot reach the category route either); input is NFC-composed; "the object was present but nothing survived stripping" is itself unreadable; the guard scans past the tokenizer's 4-token window (else "at big apple corner store 星巴克" answers for a shop the user never named); `spend_total` must now EARN its answer via a `containsUnreadableName(q)` licence, which closes fronted objects, sentence breaks and ZWSP glue for every letter script; `intentFromKind` refuses an unreadable question, so the LLM cannot re-answer what the parser just abstained on (its only expressible reading of a store-scoped spend question is `spend_total` — `merchant_spend` is not in its closed set); and the frame abstains on exactly the input the parser abstains on. **STOPPED at the cap with these OPEN, per CLAUDE.md:** the same sin for a FRONTED *ASCII* object ("At Costco, how much did I spend?" → the all-spending total) — pre-existing, and the durable fix is the positive-licence inversion, a real parser change that deserves its own slice (TASKS 2.6); "at home depot" → the "home" GROUP (rent + mortgage inside a one-retailer figure); punctuation after the preposition; a non-ASCII custom-category name being unreachable; and the vocab re-check's inability to distinguish the classifier's "none" from a network fault. | Four cycles on one defect is exactly what the cap exists to stop. The honest accounting: the vocab feature is done and safe, the parser bug is older than it, and each of my three fixes made the tree strictly better while missing the structural point that the fourth critic finally named. Patching the filter a fifth time would be the definition of the loop the cap is there to break — so the inversion is scoped as its own task, with its own critic budget, and the owner gets to see the finding rather than inherit it silently. |
| 229 | TASKS 2.6 | **The spend_total inversion shipped: the sink now EARNS its answer via a positive licence, enforced in all three routes.** New shared primitive `unconsumedSpendObject` (intent.ts): every at/with/on/in object ANYWHERE in the question must be consumed-class — timeframe/total cues, a small documented idiom set ("at least", "at the end of last month", "on track"), question machinery, a day/year digit only right after a month token — up to a closer (sentence punctuation or a timeframe cue), or `spend_total` is refused by the parser sink, by `intentFromKind` (so neither the LLM nor a learned vocab rule can re-answer what the parser abstained on), and by the frame. The set is deliberately conservative: a missing word costs an honest redirect; a wrong word costs the cardinal sin. The old verb-anchored #166 on-object guard is subsumed. Bundled siblings fixed: tier-3 verbatim-group fallback is word-bounded and not name-extended ("at home depot"/"at homegoods"/"at home and garden" → merchant_spend, never rent+mortgage; "on home" keeps the group); preposition punctuation glue tolerated and punctuation-only tokens skipped as filler ("at - costco"/"at... costco" → merchant "costco"); custom categories match NFC + Unicode-boundaried (JS \b has no boundary after "é"), with EXACT-OBJECT carve-outs through the unreadable-name abstains in parser and frame — equality including the tail ("at café in 星巴克 town" abstains), labels trimmed. Frame additionally: a guard-REFUSED merchant candidate (payment method, intent noun, prose) now BLOCKS the whole fragment instead of falling through to the timeframe-only swap ("with amex in june" answered the carried question's June total; "income in june" answered a SPEND figure to an income question), while PRONOUNS still fall through ("that in june" = the carried question); the unreadable-object guard runs on the cue-stripped rest over the full leading-preposition set. Two fresh-context Fable critic cycles: cycle 1 FAIL — 2 P0 (one root: the licence's FIRST consumed token licensed the whole object, so "at Best Buy/Top Golf/All Saints/5 Guys/76" still took the total through every route), 1 P1 (the frame's on/for silent drop), 2 P2, 2 P3 — ALL fixed in-cycle. Cycle 2: all 7 CLOSED by re-executed repros, PASS (0 P0/P1), 2 new P2 ("Do It Best" — a store spelled entirely in licence tokens; carve-out prefix-equality dropping an unreadable tail) — both also fixed and regression-locked. DEFERRED to STATUS §OPEN: tier-1 synonyms inside store names ("at travel lodge" → Travel, "at total wine" → alcohol, "at 24 hour fitness" → fitness); "biggest purchase at costco" ignores the merchant scope; bare-year/numeric-date windows ("in 2025", "on 3/5") now abstain honestly instead of answering a this-month total under a different-window question — a timeframe-parser follow-up would let them earn real answers. | The 4-cycle #226 history proved filters in front of an unconditional sink cannot win — each hardening moved the bug one syntactic inch. The inversion makes absence-of-evidence insufficient: the total must be positively licensed, and the licence primitive is SHARED so parser, frame, vocab and LLM can never disagree about what was asked. The critic's cycle-1 P0 against my own first licence (one licensed word licensing the words behind it) is the same lesson one level down — and is why the critic re-executed every repro in cycle 2 before this row was written. |
| 230 | TASKS 2.7 | Ask timeframe follow-up + largest_purchases merchant scope shipped (STATUS §OPEN items 3-4 from the 2.6 escalation), built + criticized on Fable. (a) parseExplicitTimeframe learns bare years ('in 2025' → that calendar year; the current year → Jan..today labeled '2026 so far'), 'since <year\|month\|last month\|last year>' (through-today windows), year ranges ('between 2024 and 2025' → 'in 2024–2025'; a range ending in the CURRENT year is labeled 'since <lo>' so the frame's staleness relabel covers it), and numeric dates ('3/5' US M/D, '3/2025', '3/5/2025' → the containing MONTH window, mirroring the shipped worded 'on March 5' rule). | 231 | TASKS 2.8 | **Tier-1 synonym-inside-store-name (STATUS §OPEN item 2): ATTEMPTED, proven MERCHANT-DB-BLOCKED across three fresh-context Fable critic cycles, and REVERTED — nothing shipped; tree back at #230's b062e5a, verify GREEN.** The bug: a curated category synonym inside a store name ("at travel lodge" → Travel group, "at 24 hour fitness" → fitness) outranks the merchant reading and answers the whole CATEGORY — a nonzero, wrong-SCOPE figure (the category is a SUPERSET of the store), never a $0. The attempted fix routed these to `merchant_spend` via a `resolveSpendTarget` guard aligned with `extractSpendMerchant` (verb-anchored, `extractMerchantPhrase`-bounded), span-based `synonymCoveredTokens`, and curated `GENERIC_PLACE_TAIL` / `MERCHANT_MODIFIERS` sets, firing only when a DISTINCTIVE token sat adjacent to a synonym. **Cycle 1 FAIL (2 P0 + 3 P1):** the first cut scanned the whole sentence tail, so trailing junk ("at the gas station THIS MORNING") minted merchants and idioms/fronted objects broke — all fixed by anchoring to the router's own extractor + adjacency. **Cycle 2 FAIL (3 P1):** the adjacency rule had no notion of a generic MODIFIER, so "at my local gym"/"at the nearest gas station"/"with my gas CARD" fabricated merchants — fixed by a modifier set, widened tails, an all-token tender check, and an internal-preposition guard. **Cycle 3 FAIL (5 P1) — DECISIVE:** the same $0-fabrication disease reappeared on phrasings as common as plurals ("at gas stations" → "No spending at Gas Stations"), ordinary adjectives ("at the fancy/big/old coffee shop"), and mid-phrase articles. Root cause is fundamental and un-fixable by enumeration: **"SHELL gas station" (brand) and "FANCY gas station" (adjective) are structurally identical `[X][synonym][tail]`; separating a brand token from a generic modifier IS the merchant-identification problem** (item 3's dependency), which needs a merchant database this app does not have. **Decision: do NOT ship.** The precision fix REGRESSED common, currently-correct category questions into confident $0 fabrications (the cardinal-sin direction) in exchange for fixing rarer store questions — a net-negative trade. Reverted `intent.ts` + tests to HEAD; folded item 2 into item 3's merchant-DB-blocked class in STATUS. A future NARROW slice could soundly fix only the un-ambiguous sub-cases (possessive "gold's gym", digit-bearing "24 hour fitness") with zero $0 risk, but every headline name is in the ambiguous class. | The safe pre-existing behavior (category superset — nonzero, directionally-correct) is NOT the cardinal sin; a confident $0 for spending the user actually has IS. A fix that converts the former into the latter on common input fails the "net-positive, zero-new-cardinal-sin" bar, so the honest deliverable is the FINDING (this class is merchant-DB-blocked) rather than the code. The 4-cycle cap exists for exactly this: three cycles were enough to prove the approach cannot separate brands from adjectives without data, so the block is surfaced to the owner as a scope decision (build a merchant DB, or accept the documented residual) instead of shipping a regression or burning cycle 4 on an approach already proven leaky. |

Cycle 2 re-executed both repros independently (incl. tsc-level rejection of a meta-less call), fuzzed 400 iterations clean, PASS with 3 P3s: expectedHeadlineCents-optional trap (slice-2 constraint (c), consider required when the first caller lands), a dead cast (removed), and a custom-Income-group observation (income ignores meta on both surfaces — lockstep holds; revisit when custom categories meet income). Slice-2 binding constraints recorded in GLASSBOX_PLAN §Sequencing: per-figure tappability (detail totals/share-% non-tappable), largest runner-ups non-tappable, server threads mergeCategoryMeta + expectedHeadlineCents. | The plan's sharp failure mode is a trust feature that lies — EITHER direction: a false "can't reconcile" on a correct number, or a green check on a wrong one. Sharing the engines' own row predicates makes reconciliation true BY CONSTRUCTION (not by a parallel derivation that can drift), and the runtime check turns any genuine drift into an honest disclosure instead of a silent wrong number. Both critic P1s were the same lesson at API scope: an optional parameter whose omission silently degrades correctness is a trap — meta is now required, and the tapped figure is checked when provided. Engine-before-UI held: the entire money surface is proven pure + seed-grounded before a single tappable element exists. |

Triage/`SharedTransactionList` deliberately UNTOUCHED — ai-guess rows are auto-filed (LLM overlay files ≥ AUTO_SILENT_BPS, needsReview false) so they live in the register, never the review queue; triage suggestions come from the LIVE pipeline, a different provenance path than the persisted prediction the resolver reads; and provenance never enters the partner `SharedTxnRow` (the second-person-copy fence, #221). **DEMO FENCE:** the confirm write persists to the shared `user-demo` row exactly as register `recategorize` and triage filing already do — a reversible/reseedable category pick (the class assistant.ts:324 / #234 established as NOT needing a fence, distinct from the typed-input class vocab/household must fence); a session-only fence would show a confirm that doesn't stick under the reload-is-truth commit. **DEMO AI-GUESS FIXTURE (critic-driven honesty pivot):** criterion 8 needs one demonstrable ai-guess row. The demo has NO auto-filed unknown-merchant row (every real-category row is a KNOWN merchant → merchant-default, which always beats the LLM), so relabeling one's `source` to 'llm' would FABRICATE an impossible origin — the exact dishonesty this feature prevents (Fable critic P2-1, caught a real fall-through in the first draft). Fix: the seed PROMOTES one uncategorized, unknown-merchant review row to an llm-resolved row (real category 'shopping', conf 9500, needsReview false, source 'llm') — the authentic production overlay path (resolve an unsure row the deterministic pipeline couldn't place). This moves ONE row out of uncategorized/review: the slice's one deliberate golden change, and it broke ZERO money/accuracy/e2e goldens (full suite + 23 register/triage e2e green — the promoted row was in no exact golden). **Fresh-context Fable hostile critic: cycle 1 PASS, 0 P0/P1** (cardinal rule upheld — no AI guess shown as fact, no fabricated origin/confidence, partner path clean, verbatim-value clean). | 252 | AI plan §Later #11 | **Money Signature shipped = §11 reshaped exactly per its adversarial verdict — hysteresis before any label change, stable axes decoupled from a responsive weather state, habit framing not personality (owner's "continue" at the #251 fork; picked as the last M-size groundable §Later item after reconciliation: Threaded Ask #21 superseded by #222/#230, double-bill timestamp-blocked, #17 drift needs the transfer-pair engine, #13 is XL behind a snapshot-coherence engine).** Pure `engine/fi/signature.ts` — NO LLM, and deliberately NO persistence: hysteresis is a RETROSPECTIVE WALK over the monthly flow series (`resolveConfirmedLabel`), so the confirmed label at any point is a pure function of history — no schema change, no demo-fence, no consent state to leak or race, and the oscillation blocker dissolves by construction. Axis 1 saving habit: shareBps = floor(saved×10000/eligible) over the trailing ≤12 eligible (rate ≠ null) months, steady ≥ 7500 / variable ≤ 5000, ≥ 6 eligible months else 'forming'. Calendar gaps materialize as $0/null-rate months (creep's zero-fill convention); the partial current month is dropped inside the engine. | 261 | #261 / owner-reported password symptom + a secrets-in-git finding | **The owner's "it's just not remembering the password I entered earlier" is still NOT diagnosed, and this slice deliberately does not claim it is.** Two things were done instead. (1) **A precautionary RESTORATION of the password field, per CLAUDE.md rule 0** ("when the app is broken, restore a known-good state first, then diagnose"): `PasswordInput` (#258, the only change to that field on the reported day) left the input at `type="text"` whenever the viewer was open at submit time, and a browser password manager decides whether to offer to save by inspecting the form at submission. The component now registers a capture-phase `submit` listener on its own `el.form` that writes `el.type = 'password'` imperatively and then `setVisible(false)` to keep React state and DOM agreeing — the `type` write must be imperative because a state update does not reach the DOM before the submit handlers run. Net effect: the form the browser sees at submit is byte-identical to the pre-#258 form, while the viewer the owner asked for survives. This removes #258 as a *possible* cause by construction; it does **not** establish that #258 *was* the cause, and whether the browser now offers to save is **UNVERIFIED** — no password manager runs in this environment. Locked at e2e (`auth.spec.ts`: toggle to `text` → submit → assert `type` is `password` and `aria-pressed` is `false`), driven through the wrong-password path because it is the one submit that stays on /sign-in to be inspected. (2) **Recorded, but not acted on, the highest-value unanswered question**: `AUTH_SECRET` is the JWT signing key, so *rotating it signs every device out at once* — which reads exactly like the reported symptom and is genuinely env-caused, matching the owner's own "we did lots of things in env variables today". The previous session's note correctly established that no env var can invalidate a stored password (per-password salt lives inside the stored `scrypt$salt$key` string) but did not connect `AUTH_SECRET` to session survival; that mechanism is now written down beside the field-`type` one, with the note that the two have different fixes. (3) **New finding, owner-gated:** `docs/DEPLOY.md:54–55` commits literal generated `AUTH_SECRET` and `DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY` values, introduced in `ca23eac` (2026-06-21) and never removed (`git log --all -S` finds one adding commit, no removing commit), on a branch pushed to GitHub. Whether that repo is public, and whether production uses these exact values, cannot be verified from here — DECISIONS #198 records only that Production already had the variables set, not their values. Rotation is owner-gated because each step has a visible cost: `AUTH_SECRET` rotation signs everyone out and kills outstanding reset links, `DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY` rotation makes stored Plaid/SimpleFIN tokens undecryptable (re-link required), and removing the lines fixes HEAD but not history — the values are burned either way. | Rule 0 is explicit that a blocked owner gets a known-good state before a diagnosis, and the minimal restoration here keeps the requested feature rather than reverting it, so nothing is traded away while the real reading of the symptom is still unknown. Claiming the fix would have been the exact failure the rule was written for: the DOM state is executed-and-verified, the browser behaviour is not, and the two are reported separately. The secrets finding surfaced while chasing "which env vars changed" and is strictly more serious than the reported symptom, so it is filed as its own OPEN item rather than folded into it — and left for the owner because both the blast radius (every connected bank) and the facts needed to size it (repo visibility, deployed values) live in dashboards this environment cannot see. |
| 263 | Wave M (mobile UI) | **Owner's /accounts mobile overflow — the reported "doesn't format correctly."** Owner sent two iOS-Safari screenshots (M.0 deliverable): /accounts Assets ($1,735,553.08) and Liabilities (−$997,970.24), money values + the Delete control clipped at the right edge, truncation clearly off. Diagnosis under CLAUDE.md rule 0 (verify, don't guess): built an objective overflow gate (`tests/e2e/mobile-overflow.spec.ts`, scrollWidth<=clientWidth at 360/393/430) and FALSIFIED, in order, every default-environment hypothesis — the net-worth summary line (two big figures fit at 360), a long manual name (ManualRow already truncates), a long SYNCED name seeded via better-sqlite3 into the e2e DB (LinkedRow fit), the WebKit engine (added a `mobile-webkit`/iPhone-13 project; still fit), and text scaling to 150% (root 24px; still fit). SW ruled out too (no fetch handler, purges caches on activate → no stale-PWA-cache). Remaining cause: **real iOS Safari's stricter flexbox `min-width:auto`** — `LinkedRow`'s `<Link>` lacked min-w-0, so lenient engines (Chromium, Playwright WebKit 26.4) shrink the flex item and truncate the name while the owner's iOS Safari does not, pushing balance+Delete off-screen. Fix = canonical truncation hardening: min-w-0 down the LinkedRow chain (`<li>`→`<Link>`→name flex), ManualRow amount cluster shrink-0, net-worth summary line flex-wrap. Money/copy untouched (layout-only; #221/#250 guardrails stay green). | The locking test is an honest **regression GUARD**, not fail-old/pass-new: Playwright's engines don't reproduce the Safari quirk, so on-device owner confirmation is the final check (and, if it persists, the still-open M.0 text-size/zoom variable). Chose a dedicated multi-width spec + one WebKit project over 4× viewport projects (which would 4× the whole 143-test suite for marginal gain). Scoped the code fix to the reported file (accounts-list) only; the app-wide M.2 tap-target primitive and the other M.3 overflow sites remain their own slices to avoid a throwaway partial. |

| 266 | 4.6 spec (#266) | Speced cross-provider account reconciliation (docs/PROVIDER_RECONCILIATION_ARCHITECTURE.md) to answer the owner's SimpleFIN-to-Plaid migration question. Chosen design: keep BOTH account rows and add an additive AccountReconciliation link (predecessor = disconnected/stale e.g. SimpleFIN, successor = live e.g. Plaid) with a single half-open cutover date — predecessor owns transactions with date <= cutover and contributes ZERO balance, successor owns date > cutover and its live balance. Applied once in the shared getFinanceSnapshot assembler so every engine inherits it. Rejected: re-pointing transactions or swapping a row's provider (destructive, hard to reverse); a general fuzzy cross-provider transaction dedup (money-integrity risk). Never automatic (offered only when exactly one side has a live connection), always user-confirmed and undoable, demo/golden byte-identical. 6-slice engine-first plan with Fable hostile critic on the assembler-boundary, cash-needed, and full-surface slices; invariants R1-R10 each mapped to a locking test. | The verified failure is a double-count: net worth sums every account row with no staleness filter (networth/series.ts, demo.ts:42) and transaction dedup is per (accountId, providerRef) (schema.prisma:275), so the same real account via two providers counts twice in balances AND overlapping transactions; the only current fix (delete the stale row via account-delete.ts) cascades its history + corrections. A single date boundary makes each real transaction date owned by exactly one side, so NO fuzzy matching is needed — the place a money bug would otherwise hide is designed out. Keeping both rows + an inert-when-undone link is fully reversible and additive, honoring the never-auto-delete-money-data and demo-byte-identical constitution. The residual risk is a <=1-day boundary straddle when two providers date the same purchase differently; documented for the critic (accept-and-disclose vs a narrow boundary-local dedup), never a general fuzzy match. |

The one fact that DID differ — how many accounts each connection feeds — was spent only inside the post-tap confirm prompt, i.e. after the choice the user could not make. FIVE decisions: (1) the card now distinguishes CONNECTIONS, via a pure pair-level view module (src/components/finance/duplicate-card-view.ts) that computes BOTH sides together, renders each as its own block with its connection identity ("Plaid: U.S. Bank · connection 1 of 2 · last synced 2026-07-24") and manifest ("Also feeds 1 other account: CREDIT CARD ····0977" vs "Feeds only this account."), puts the ordinal and the blast radius on the button FACE, and applies a mechanical " (row 1)/(row 2)" breaker so two identical controls are impossible for ANY input rather than merely unlikely for this data. (2) ZERO server change: the ordinal is POSITIONAL over the payload /accounts already sends (orderBy createdAt asc) and the copy claims no date and no link order, so a reorder — or two links in the same DB second — can never make it false; a linkedOn field and its UTC-skew honesty debt were deliberately NOT added, and the prisma diff is empty. (3) blast-radius COUNT comes from the server's unfiltered per-item accountCount while NAMES come only from the currency-guarded client roster, with the gap disclosed as "plus N in another currency" — keeping #192's promise that the warning never references a hidden row without understating what a Disconnect stops (the old confirm prompt leaked the same gap the other way, claiming a single-account item whenever the item was merely unresolvable). (4) NO "least disruption" recommendation: accountCount measures collateral damage, not which connection is redundant, and #192 keeps the card advisory — it may present consequence, never rank the user's options. (5) the copy now states the two-step truth (verified against removeItem + the unfiltered net-worth sum): disconnecting revokes the token and deletes the PlaidItem but LEAVES the Account row counting, so the duplicate stops double-counting only after the follow-up delete. The old intro's "linked through two providers" was factually false for exactly the case shown and is replaced by a per-pair provider-conditional sentence. |

DELIBERATELY NOT CLAIMED: that the links are CORRECT — whether a second predecessor was matched to the right live account is owner-only knowledge (rule 0); the card makes that answerable and each link separately reversible. Two residuals left OPEN in STATUS (degenerate-cutover claim span; the persistent card omits the confirm-time replaced/dropped-row disclosures) rather than silently widened. |

| 291 | account identity: prevent the duplicate, don't detect it (design, TASKS L.10) | Prevent a same-account re-pull at link time (Plaid update mode + collision interception) instead of writing a second row and printing an advisory; keep two lookalike REAL accounts and make them tellable apart | DESIGN ONLY (docs/ACCOUNT_IDENTITY_ARCHITECTURE.md, TASKS L.10) — no code changed. Owner instruction 2026-07-24 drew the right line: two real accounts that resemble each other (a spouse's card, three cards named CREDIT CARD, Roth vs Traditional) must BOTH exist and only need to be tellable apart; one real account pulled twice is a REFRESH and should never have produced a second row. The app answers both the same way today — write whatever the feed returns, then print an advisory — because it structurally cannot tell them apart: a Plaid account row is keyed on Plaid's account_id (plaid.ts:356-358), which Plaid documents as NOT stable across Items, so the same card re-linked through a second Link session becomes a new row BY CONSTRUCTION; PlaidItem stores a cosmetic institution NAME and no institution_id, so the app cannot tell that a bank is already connected; and there is no subtype column, so a Roth and a Traditional are both just INVESTMENT. Design = prevention where identity is provable, disclosure where it is not, in four layers: (1) a per-connection "Add or fix accounts" control opening Link in UPDATE MODE with update.account_selection_enabled — Plaid's own documented remedy — which makes the owner's "select all accounts" case incapable of duplicating, since already-linked accounts return their existing account_ids and take the update branch, i.e. a true refresh; (2) collision interception after the exchange and BEFORE any row is written — same institution_id plus a proven account match means discard the redundant Item and route to update mode, with a "different login, keep both" escape; (3) a real remedy for the both-live duplicates that already exist (disconnect the losing side, then the shipped confirmReconciliation path — R3 honoured, history kept), closing the gap L.6 recorded and L.8 inherits; (4) everything unproven stays advisory, and cross-provider stays user-confirmed forever. DELIBERATELY REJECTED — creating the rows and merging them: the new Item replays full history under new transaction_ids and the app dedupes per (accountId, providerRef), so a merge either double-posts every transaction or needs fuzzy amount/date dedup, whose failure direction is silent loss (already rejected in the reconciliation work). Discarding the redundant Item costs one extra Link pass and costs the data nothing. ALSO REJECTED — auto-confirming a reconciliation link, which would falsify the AccountReconciliation model's "always user-confirmed (never automatic)" comment; no layer here creates a link automatically. The scoping rule that separates the owner's two cases: within one provider+institution a differing last-4 is a HARD VETO (that is what protects the spouse's card from being refreshed away), while ACROSS providers it must never veto (L.9, owner-confirmed — SimpleFIN 396 and Plaid 5351 are one Schwab account); same comparison, opposite meaning, so it is scoped per provider pair, never shared. And an identical balance never authorises an automatic action, because "one account, two cards" (the owner's E.LEE/M.LEE pair, duplicates.ts:190-196) is a fact only the cardholder knows. Schema additions are additive + nullable: PlaidItem.institutionId, Account.subtype (also answers L.9's open decision (a)), Account.persistentAccountId (Plaid's cross-Item id — TAN institutions Chase/PNC/US Bank, documented for depository accounts only, so useful where present and never the whole answer). Invariants D1-D9 each map to a locking test. UNVERIFIED: no live Plaid connection in this environment — update mode and /item/get are documented, not exercised. |
| 292 | L.10 slice 1 (#300) | Capture identity BEFORE building any identity logic: three nullable, additive columns — PlaidItem.institutionId (Plaid stable ins_* id), Account.subtype (the provider raw subtype, verbatim) and Account.persistentAccountId (Plaid persistent_account_id). Nothing reads them yet; no figure, route or copy changes. They are written by the EXISTING account mapper + upsert and backfilled by the EXISTING syncInstitutions sweep, so every row linked before the columns existed populates on its next ordinary sync with no re-link and no data migration. Both Account columns are PRESERVE-ON-NULL (a fetch that merely omits an identifier can never erase one already stored), unlike currency which is written unconditionally. The institution sweep now buys the id and the name as two SEPARATE Plaid calls: an item that already has a name never re-buys it, and a failed name lookup keeps the id it was resolved from. | The app cannot tell one real account pulled twice from two accounts that merely look alike, because it stores nothing that survives a re-link: a Plaid row is keyed on account_id, which a second Link session at the same bank re-mints (plaid.ts:356-358), the app stored a cosmetic institution NAME but never the stable ins_* id, and there was no subtype at all — so a Roth IRA and a Traditional IRA were both just INVESTMENT, which is how TASKS L.9 came to propose merging one into the other. Layers 1-4 of docs/ACCOUNT_IDENTITY_ARCHITECTURE.md all rest on these three values, so capturing them first makes every later slice a read rather than a schema change, and starts the backfill clock immediately: the owner accounts populate on the daily sweep rather than at build time. Preserve-on-null follows the DECISIONS #130 balance rule and the institution-name idiom for the same reason in a stronger form — persistent_account_id is supplied ONLY by Tokenized-Account-Number institutions, so a response omitting it says nothing about the account, and writing null would erase the single identifier that outlives a re-link. Splitting the sweep call was forced by a test written honestly: the first draft asserted the durable half survived a cosmetic failure while the code discarded both, so the code was changed to match the claim rather than the claim softened. |
| 293 | L.10 slice 2 (#301) | Ship Plaid Link UPDATE MODE as a per-connection 'Add or fix accounts' control on /accounts — layer 1 of the account-identity design. linkTokenParams gains an update variant that sends access_token + update.account_selection_enabled and structurally OMITS products (Plaid rejects them alongside an access_token); a new user-scoped createUpdateLinkToken mints against the item's own decrypted token; the public token from an update session is never exchanged. The OAuth round-trip record became ONE atomic localStorage value stamped immediately before open(), replacing two keys written at mint time. A per-item sync failure is now COUNTED and returned (SyncResult.itemsFailed), so a scoped sync can tell 'nothing new' from 'nothing got through'. All user-facing sentences live in a pure copy module with their own tests. | Three fresh-context critics ran against the first cut and between them found 2 P0, 6 P1 and a long P2 tail; every P0 and P1 was fixed in cycle 1 and locked. The P0 was mine and it was structural: I claimed fence-by-construction for a discriminator held in a SECOND localStorage key, but /accounts renders this control beside the connect front door, which pre-mints a token on mount and after every exit — so a background writer could replace a live update session's record, and the front door's fast path (already-ready, open() straight from the click) never wrote at all. Worst ordering: a COMPLETED new-bank link took the update branch, was never exchanged, and the user was redirected as though it had worked — silent data loss with a positive confirmation. The fix is not a bigger fence but a different shape: one record, one write, stamped by whoever is opening Link, so the stored value always describes the session actually running. The copy findings shared one root too — every false sentence pushed the reader toward connecting a bank they already have, which is the exact action the feature exists to make unnecessary: a failed data pull was reported as a failed update; the flash claimed a page-wide refresh after syncing one bank; it asserted that added accounts were listed when an unsupported currency or unmappable type means they are not; the hint called a second login at one bank a duplicate when it is not; and it named a control that does not exist for a disconnected bank. Two further P1s were pre-existing and fixed because the new copy depends on them: a per-item sync failure was invisible to callers (added 0, no error) so the existing Sync button also told users 'no new transactions' when their bank had refused, and removeItem bought /accounts/get to stamp item linkage and threw away the identity in the same response — leaving every disconnected row permanently identity-less, which is precisely the population the reconciliation flow works on. subtype became write-always because type is DERIVED from it and was already unconditional, so preserving one while recomputing the other let a row settle at type LOAN with subtype mortgage: two stored facts contradicting each other on the pair the identity ladder compares as a unit. Deliberately NOT fixed here and recorded as TASKS L.14: account selection can also DESELECT, and the app never prunes a row whose feed stops returning it, so it freezes, keeps counting and still reads as freshly synced — an honest fix needs a schema shape and its own money critic, so slice 2 ships a disclosure naming the re-tick as the remedy rather than a transient message pretending to be a fix. |
| 294 | L.11(A) savings-rate figure fix (#302) | The savings-rate card's multi-month figure is now a POOLED rate — new pure pooledSavingsRateBps (Sum income - Sum expenses)/Sum income over income-bearing months — replacing an arithmetic mean of monthly RATIOS. Display is floored: any rate past -100% renders 'below -100%' on the headline, the average, and the per-bar tooltip; the average line is shown only with >=2 contributing months; the chart uses a fixed +/-100% scale; the 15% aspiration line shows only when at least one month actually saved. Pure display logic extracted to savings-rate-format.ts with its own tests. | The owner's real dashboard showed 'Savings rate 4-month average of -855105.8%'. Cause: averaging monthly (income-expenses)/income ratios lets a month whose paychecks were not categorised as income (near-zero income denominator) explode to hundreds of thousands of percent and dominate the mean; the data-driven chart scale then let that one month flatten every other bar. Pooling dollars is the correct multi-period savings rate and fixes the common case. A fresh-context critic (Opus, owner out of Fable) then proved pooling ALONE does not kill the symptom: a window with exactly one income-bearing month pools to that single month's rate, so -855105.8% recurred, and a '1-month average' compares this month to itself. The display floor + the >=2-month gate close both — the giant number can no longer render anywhere, and a meaningless single-month 'average' is suppressed rather than shown. The floor is display-only; the engine keeps the true bps so the above/below comparison still orders correctly. Note the ROOT input problem (income under-measured because paychecks are miscategorised) is the same categorisation gap as L.12 — the savings math is now robust to it, but fixing categorisation would remove the near-zero-income months at the source. |
