| 297 | combine two live duplicate connections (#304) | Owner-reported 2026-07-24 with two screenshots and the question "What did you actually fix? I see the same accounts I posted earlier." He was right: #298/#299 taught /cards to TELL two same-named cards apart and to disclose a both-live duplicate, and #300/#301 (L.10 slices 1-2) prevented FUTURE duplicates — nothing removed the two Chase connections both pulling `CREDIT CARD ····0977`, which double-counted $8,539.09 in Liabilities, in the cash-needed headline, and again in every transaction total. The shipped Combine path refuses a both-live pair by design (R3 needs one stale side) and `detectReconciliationCandidates` skipped same-provider pairs outright (duplicates.ts:379), so there was no path at all. SHIPPED: (a) a pure identity LADDER (`engine/account/identity.ts`, design doc §5) that answers same / different / unproven within ONE provider at ONE institution — the only scope where a differing last-4 is a sound veto (across providers it is not: SimpleFIN 396 and Plaid 5351 are one Schwab account, L.9); it never reads a balance (D4 structural — the input type has no balance field) and treats every null as UNKNOWN, never as a difference. (b) a pure PLANNER (`engine/account/combine-connections.ts`) that works at the CONNECTION level, because the only way to make one side stale is to disconnect a Plaid item and an item can carry several accounts; a direction is offered only when every account under the dropped connection is proven the same as EXACTLY ONE account under the kept one, so disconnecting stands nothing up. (c) the action: one SERIALIZABLE claim transaction re-derives the plan, re-applies every suppression the card applies, stamps the bank identity onto the rows, carries autopay across, and DELETES the losing connection row — then, outside it, revokes the token and writes one reconciliation per pair through the shipped `confirmReconciliationFor`. No new money rule: the boundary engine already knows how to make two rows read as one account. THREE fresh-context critics in parallel (money+boundary / destructive-action safety / false-merge+copy). Cycle 1: 3 P0, 6 P1, 9 P2 — ALL fixed and regression-locked, each from an EXECUTED repro. The three that mattered: (P0) two concurrent taps each disconnected a different connection and destroyed BOTH, leaving zero connections, zero links and the duplicate still double-counting — fixed by making the row deletion itself the claim, inside the transaction that reads the plan, so the race is a detected write conflict; (P0) the date split deleted real money in BOTH directions — first the surviving feed's exclusive rows, then, after the cutover was moved, the dropped feed's — because two LIVE feeds are both partial in different places, unlike the cross-provider case this machinery was built for where the predecessor is complete up to its cutover. FIXED NOT BY A BETTER CUTOVER BUT BY A PROOF: every row the split would drop must have a same-day, same-amount survivor on the other side (multiset-matched), or the combine is REFUSED with the amount named and nothing is changed. An honest gap beats a silent deletion. (P1) the dropped row's `AutopayConfig` was filtered out with it, so /cards flipped to "move $8,539.09 yourself" while the bank still pulled it — a double-payment hazard; autopay now follows the account, never overwriting the survivor's own setting. DECIDED: the advisory #192 warning steps aside for a pair that has a combine offer (one message per pair) — and because that warning carried the only "not a duplicate" control, the combine card grew its own, wired to the same dismissal key, so the offer can never be permanent and undismissable. DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: no fuzzy amount/date dedup (its failure direction is silent loss); no automatic action ever, on any signal — an identical balance prompts, never acts (D4); cross-provider stays user-confirmed forever (L.9). Schema: two nullable additive `Account` columns (`institutionId`, `institutionName`) stamped at disconnect, because deleting the `PlaidItem` was destroying the only record of WHO a disconnected row banks with — and that row is exactly the population the ladder works on. |
| 298 | collision interception on the fresh-Link front door (#307) | Owner, verbatim 2026-07-24: *"Why in the heck are you allowed to make 2 of the same accounts... when I try to link same account again, it just refreshes."* Layer 2 of docs/ACCOUNT_IDENTITY_ARCHITECTURE.md §4, wiring the pure `detectLinkCollision` shipped unwired in 4d262c4. **The decision, in one line: refuse a new connection ONLY when every account it can reach is already reachable through another connection that answered over the wire in the same request.** Four choices worth recording. **(a) Decide BEFORE the PlaidItem upsert, not after it** (the checkpoint sketched after): the upsert is keyed on `itemId` and can UPDATE a pre-existing row, so deciding first means the redundant path writes nothing at all and there is no way to delete a connection this link did not create — D6 is satisfied a fortiori (no PlaidItem row either). **(b) The candidate is proven LIVE, not read from the database.** Both fresh-context critics broke the first version identically, and both P0s share this cause: Account rows keep describing a connection long after it can no longer reach them. An expired login still has all its rows, so re-linking to REPAIR it — the commonest reason anyone re-runs Link — discarded the credential that had just fixed it, kept the dead one, and said it had "refreshed". A row the feed stopped returning (L.14) and a row predating the `plaidItemId` stamp (#256) fail the same way in both directions. Cost: one extra billed `/accounts/get` per candidate, and ONLY when the user already has a connection at that bank. An irreversible action may not be authorised by a snapshot nobody re-checked. **(c) Wholly-redundant is computed against ALL connections at the bank, not the one the collision names** — with three connections the winner's unmatched set contains accounts a sibling already carries — and an account whose type the mapper rejects (Plaid's `other`) counts in NEITHER total: it never becomes a row, so keeping a second Item to preserve it would duplicate every visible account to protect something invisible; if the mapper ever learns that type the link stops being redundant by itself. **(d) A failed `/item/remove` keeps the item** rather than walking away: a visible duplicate the user can combine beats a live billed Item whose token the app has forgotten, and the audit write is best-effort AFTER the revoke it describes so it cannot report "linking failed" for a connection that is already gone. **D7 amended to D7a and the residual recorded as L.16:** there is no prompt and no remembered "keep both" — the escape is structural, which covers the ordinary different-login case but NOT a login whose account set is entirely shared, nor a tier-A last-4 collision. Both are recoverable (disconnect, link again) at the cost of an orphaned row. Shipping an irreversible action without the confirmation its own invariant asked for is a deliberate trade, argued and written down, not an oversight. **Critics: 2 P0 + 5 P1 + 6 P2, all fixed cycle 1**, including four copy defects — a notice that named a control by position ("below") while rendering on /cards, /settings and the dashboard onboarding panel where no connection list exists AND, on /accounts itself, below the list it pointed at; an OAuth-path notice discarded entirely unless the user happened to start from /accounts, then fired stale on a later unrelated visit; a sentence ending "until you combine them" when the state that triggers it guarantees the combine planner refuses (both directions strand); and a sibling hint still threatening that re-connecting "makes a second copy", which this change made false, pinned in place by an e2e asserting that exact phrase. Engine fix too: `identityKey`'s fallback now carries `subtype`, since a Roth and a Traditional sharing a mask collapsed to one key and silently undercounted matches. **UNVERIFIED against live Plaid** — no credentials in this environment; every request shape is exercised against a mocked Plaid server. |
| 299 | the two remaining duplicate-creating paths (L.17, #308) | Closes the two residuals both fresh-context critics of #307 raised, each REPRODUCED before it was fixed (executed; output in PROGRESS.md). **(a) A connection linked before the `institutionId` column was invisible to the collision check.** Candidates were selected by that column, which is null on every pre-#300 item, so the door was a silent no-op at exactly the banks the owner already had. DECIDED: resolve on demand rather than wait for the sweep. A candidate that cannot name its bank is asked (`/item/get`), and the answer is written back exactly as `syncInstitutions` would, so the id is bought at most once per connection and only while a link at another bank is in flight. A candidate that cannot answer takes part in no match and the new link is kept. **(b) Two concurrent Link sessions at one bank both persisted.** DECIDED: a unique-keyed lease (`PlaidLinkClaim`, `@@unique([userId, institutionId])`) makes the DECISION exclusive, held across the classify-and-write window and released in a `finally`. Deliberately NOT a unique constraint on `PlaidItem`: two connections at one bank are legitimate (a spouse's own login), so what must be exclusive is the decision, never the outcome. The lease carries an `expiresAt` and a takeover path, and the takeover deletes by the id it just read, so a rival cannot delete the live claim its winner went on to make. **The wait is 4s, and failing to get the lease PROCEEDS UNPROTECTED** — a link exchange runs inside one serverless request that has already spent seconds on Plaid calls, and timing it out would leave a billed Item whose token was never stored, which #307 established as the worst failure on this path; proceeding yields at worst a duplicate the app discloses (#306) and can combine (#304). `exchangePublicToken` was split so the lease brackets one method (`decideAndPersistItem`) instead of an inline block. | The failure direction is the whole argument in both halves: a missed collision is a visible duplicate, a wrong one throws away a real bank connection. (a) is the an-empty-set-is-not-a-fact rule applied to a COLUMN — a null `institutionId` means "never asked", not "different bank" — and the fix self-heals every future link at that bank by persisting what it had to buy. (b) is invariant D1 finally holding by construction rather than by sequence, with the residual written down instead of claimed away: a lease is not a distributed transaction, so a link whose institution never resolves takes none (nothing to be exclusive about, and the collision check abstains on it anyway), and a claim leaked by a killed process is reclaimed by the next link at that bank rather than by a sweep. `PlaidLinkClaim` holds a user id and a public `ins_*` id, no financial data, cascades on account deletion, and is disclosed in PRIVACY.md. Gate: `bash scripts/verify.sh` GREEN — tsc 0 / eslint 0 / 4006 unit / 264 files / build clean; duplicate-connections e2e 8/8 serialized. Schema additive (one new model; no change to any existing table). **UNVERIFIED against live Plaid** — no credentials here; the concurrency repro drives two real `exchangePublicToken` calls against a mocked Plaid server and real Prisma. |
| 300 | the six surfaces that rendered a duplicated card and said nothing (L.15, #309) | Closes the class opened by the L.8 sweep: /cards (#299) and the dashboard hero + reminders list (#306) disclosed a both-live duplicate; the calendar, the reminder email, the weekly digest, web push, the Ask cash-needed answer and the Glass-Box trace did not. **Stance unchanged — DISCLOSE, NEVER ADJUST (#289, from #192/#221): not one figure moves.** **(a) One plumbing point, not six.** `getCashNeeded` now returns `cardDuplicates` via a shared `displayedCardDuplicates` helper, and `detectDisplayedCardDuplicates` stays module-private so no caller can hand it the household-MERGED snapshot — a partner's card can never pair with the reader's, by construction rather than by six correct call sites (`fence-by-construction-not-per-call-site`). It resolves over the union of dated + undated cards; each surface narrows that superset through `resolvePairs`, which already drops any pair whose two cards are not both displayed. No extra query for a user with no candidate pair. **(b) Detect per run, never a stored flag.** A flag written last week describes connections that may since have been deleted, combined, or dismissed as 'not duplicates', and unlike a page an email carries no control to correct itself. Cheap enough to be current beats cached and stale. **(c) One sentence per surface, because only the MONEY CLAIM differs.** Positional and figure-quoting copy written for a page is FALSE in an inbox: the email variant names no position and no on-screen number ('in this email', 'Open Accounts in Aimplifi'); the Ask note names the two figures that answer actually prints (the amount and the card count) and nothing else; the trace names rows; the calendar names the money-out total and the payment count its month summary prints, and deliberately does NOT claim the two events share a date (two providers can report different due dates for one card). **(d) Push discloses; it does not suppress.** Suppression is an ADJUSTMENT that asserts two rows are one card — the claim only the user can make — and its failure direction is a MISSED PAYMENT on a genuinely separate card, against one redundant notification for disclosing. The note also makes no claim about how many notifications will arrive, since `selectNotifications` filters each side independently (autopay-covered, out of window, already sent), so 'you will get two' is false whenever one side is filtered out. **(e) `CalendarEvent` gained `accountId`.** The disclosure must name only cards on THIS month's grid; an obligation due outside the month emits no event, so re-deriving the set at the call site would have named a card that is not there. The engine already knew the id. **(f) The copy module moved into the engine tree.** Three of the six consumers are pure engines and no `src/lib` module had ever imported from `src/components`; `card-duplicate-view.ts` moved to `lib/engine/account/`, taking `renderSafe` into `lib/engine/account/render-safe.ts` (re-exported from its old home, so every existing importer is untouched and there is still exactly ONE sanitizer). | **(g) The positional collision breaker became opt-in (cycle-1 critic F1).** Whether a surface can be pointed at positionally is a fact about THE SURFACE: /cards, the hero and the reminders card paint `cardIdentityLabels`' ordinals into the heading itself, so "1. CREDIT CARD" names something on screen; an email prints bullets, a push has no list, and the Ask answer paints no card names at all. Run unconditionally the breaker quoted labels that appear NOWHERE, on the default reported shape. `resolvePairs` now requires `numbersRows`, and a surface that cannot renumber gets `sameLabel` and a sentence that names the shared string once. **(h) The radar is the SEVENTH surface (cycle-1 critic P1-2)**, found only by sweeping beyond the six the task enumerated: it repeats every obligation across 90 days, so a duplicate manufactures a critical dip alert that would not otherwise exist and a $33,100 move instruction instead of $13,050. Disclosed through its existing `assumptions` channel AND carried on `RadarResult.duplicateDisclosure`, because the alert push composes its own body and never reads `assumptions`. **(i) The radar disclosure is gated on the COUNTERFACTUAL its own sentence asserts — three critic cycles to find the boundary.** Cut 1 resolved the pair against `cashNeeded.cards`, so a PAID-OFF pair (in no projected cycle) hedged a genuine overdraft push. Cut 2 narrowed to `dues`, the rows the projection repeats — and a third critic falsified that too, with the useful observation: being IN the projection does not make the sentence true. It claims the dip date may be earlier and the amount to move larger, and both are fixed by the WORST POINT of the 90-day walk, not by the presence of dues elsewhere in it. An ordinary state separates them (a real crunch this week from a DIFFERENT card, the pair due next month and absorbed by payroll): removing the duplicate moves neither figure, yet the reader was told the $2,900 they must move in four days might be imaginary. DECIDED: re-walk the projection with one side of each pair removed and speak only if the dip date or the cover amount actually moves. `computeRadar` is pure, so this is one extra in-memory walk, paid only by a user who has a projected duplicate. It also settles the `status === 'ok'` case with no second rule — with no dip, nothing differs, so the radar no longer prints "the dip date may be earlier" under a header reading "Clear". Nothing is ADJUSTED: the counterfactual decides only whether to SPEAK; every displayed figure comes from the real walk, duplicates included. Failure direction runs OPPOSITE to (d) here — on this surface the cheap failure is silence, because a false hedge on a real dip is what makes someone under-fund and overdraft. | Rejected: a single flag-shaped `duplicatePairCount` like the household digest's — it names no card, and on channels with no control the reader needs to know WHICH two rows. Rejected: reusing `cardDuplicateListView`'s wording on the email — 'listed below' is a claim about a page. Rejected: suppressing the second push (failure direction, above). Rejected: leaving the copy module under `components/` and importing it from engines — that inverts the dependency the repo has never inverted. Residual, recorded: an UNDATED pair is out of scope in the Ask answer, where it is in neither the figure nor the count, so the sentence would be false about it; the other five surfaces disclose it correctly. |
| 301 | an account the bank stopped sharing: disclose, adjust nothing (L.14) | **The defect.** Plaid Link update mode ships with `account_selection_enabled`, so a user can untick an account. `upsertPlaidAccounts` only ever creates or updates, so nothing noticed: the row kept its last balance, kept counting toward net worth / cash-needed / /cards, and kept LOOKING current, because a Plaid row's freshness is graded from its BANK's last sync (#293) and the bank was still syncing. It also could not be deleted — the guard said “Disconnect the bank first”, whose premise (the next sync would bring it back) is false for a row the feed no longer sends. L.10 slice 2 shipped a sentence in the post-update flash; a transient flash is not a fix for a figure that stays wrong forever. **The decision: DISCLOSE, ADJUST NOTHING** — the same stance as #192/#221/#289/#299, but reached here on failure DIRECTION rather than precedent. Excluding the row would be its own money claim, and the expensive direction is exclusion: an unticked card whose statement is still genuinely owed would vanish from cash-needed and the app would stop telling someone to pay a bill they still owe. Keeping it over-funds slightly; dropping it silently risks a missed payment. Only the user knows whether the account still exists, so the app surfaces the decision with BOTH remedies (re-tick, or delete) instead of guessing. What changes is what the app CLAIMS. **Evidence discipline.** Absence counts only from a complete, wholly-readable `/accounts/get` census scoped to rows provably belonging to that connection — the #290 “prune only on a clean run” rule. Never from the `/transactions/sync` echo, which carries only accounts with transaction activity and would therefore freeze every quiet loan, card and brokerage on the first sync after deploy; never on an empty list (an error state, not a mass unshare); never on a list with one unreadable entry; and never re-stamped, because the first observation is what the disclosure claims about the past. Ids are extracted BEFORE mapping, so an account Plaid types `other` (unmappable, never a row) still counts as present. **Residuals, argued not hidden:** a row whose `plaidItemId` predates #256 is out of scope (it cannot be proven to belong to this connection, and it is indistinguishable from a disconnected row), and SimpleFIN is unwired — both fail toward the pre-existing behaviour, never toward a false drop. |
| 302 | what a frozen balance may be used FOR (L.14 critic cycle 1) | Amends #301 in the one place it was wrong. #301 argued “keep counting, disclose” entirely over LIABILITIES — a stale card balance merely over-funds, while excluding a still-owed card risks a missed payment. Two fresh-context critics independently found the inverse case: the user's own PAYMENT account can be feed-dropped, and there a balance frozen HIGH reports shortfall $0 with no transfer recommendation while the real account cannot cover the autopay — the exact missed payment #301 set out to prevent. **The refined rule, and the one to carry forward: ask the failure-direction question per ROLE, not per class of value.** A frozen number is conservative as a debt and dangerous as a source of funds. Concretely: (a) TOTALS keep counting it and say so — only the user knows whether the account still exists; (b) an INSTRUCTION built on one account's balance may not be built from a frozen one — Cash Flow Radar withholds it as a transfer source and states the withholding, because “move $2,900 from Rainy Day Savings” is an action whose failure is a bounced transfer and an overdrafted card payment, not a figure the reader can weigh; (c) a PROJECTION resting on one frozen balance is disclosed at the surface giving the instruction, never adjusted — inventing a lower balance would fabricate. Also settled here: a disclosure is resolved against the same post-processing as the figures it qualifies (a reconciliation predecessor is zeroed and hidden, so announcing it as “still counted” was false), “absent from the payload” may not be read as an event unless the SUBJECT was in that payload's scope (the holdings sweep deleted an unticked brokerage's positions under its own clean-run rule), and a remedy may only name a control that exists in the state the message renders in. Four P1s remain OPEN and are recorded as TASKS L.18 rather than bulk-patched, because pasting one sentence onto four surfaces is the L.15 failure verbatim. |
| 303 | the fact rides the money, and every surface states its own claim (L.18) | **The defect.** #301 disclosed a frozen account once, in the cash-needed engine's `assumptions`, and its comment claimed that reached "/cards, the dashboard hero, the calendar, the Ask answer and the weekly digest". Exactly two surfaces render that array. The four it named as covered were four of the surfaces that were silent — still printing "pay $X by DATE", a net worth, an FI projection and a runway from balances that had stopped moving. **The shape.** `frozenSince` now rides the OBLIGATION (`CardObligation`, `UnknownDueDateCard`, `LoanObligation`, `PaymentReminder`) and `fundingFrozen` rides `CashNeededResult`, all REQUIRED, so the fact travels with the money to every consumer with no new query and no argument a caller can forget. **The copy.** One module owns every string, and three things are required arguments rather than inferences, each because guessing it shipped a defect: `role` (a FIGURE the reader weighs vs an INSTRUCTION they act on — #302's axis), `nextStep` (what this surface can honestly point at: an email controls no position and holds no button, a push body has no room), and `ownership` (`reader`/`partner`/`unknown` — the third state exists because the pure engine is handed a household-MERGED account list and cannot know whose card it holds, so it says "the bank" rather than defaulting to a claim it cannot support). **Two corrections to #301's own copy, both from re-reading the engine rather than the comment:** a frozen card WITH a statement does not derive its figures from the frozen balance (the engine reads the statement and never touches `currentBalanceCents`) — what is actually missing is everything since the drop, including a payment already made, which is the claim that covers both paths; and `requiredCents` never reads the funding balance, so "every figure here is projected from it" named a dependency that does not exist. **A correction to the brief, proved by execution:** L.18 said the frozen balance drives the FI number. It does not — `fiNumberCents(annualExpenses, swrBps)` reads no balance at all, so /coach qualifies the portfolio-derived and cash-derived figures and deliberately leaves that one alone. **Also closed here:** Cash Flow Radar disclosed a frozen account as a transfer SOURCE (#302) and said nothing about the balance its entire 90-day walk STARTS from — the number that decides whether there is a dip, when, and how large the transfer is, and whose frozen-HIGH case produces no alert at all. |
| 304 | a loan can go stale too, and a claim about an absence must name the right mechanism (L.19) | **The gap.** #303 made `frozenSince` ride every obligation, LOANS INCLUDED, and then built each surface's frozen list from cards alone. Two consequences, both live. **(1)** The all-clear — "You're all caught up" on the dashboard, "a clear week ahead" in the weekly digest — is a claim about `reminders`, and `selectPaymentReminders` mixes loans into that list; a frozen loan's stored `dueDayOfMonth` is precisely the field the bank stopped confirming, so it is the likeliest reason the all-clear is wrong, and it was the one row that could never reach a qualifier. **(2)** /calendar said nothing at all, on the page whose entire product is a dated amount to pay. **The shape.** `FrozenNothingDueRow.kind` is REQUIRED, because the two claims are about different mechanisms and cannot share a sentence: a card's gap is a STATEMENT that could not reach us; a loan issues no statement, and its gap is a payment amount and a due day that stopped being confirmed. `frozenNothingDueNote` splits by kind before it splits by ownership, so a household with a frozen card and a partner's frozen mortgage produces four correct clauses instead of one wrong one. **One builder for the row set.** The dashboard and the digest each hand-rolled the list, near-identically — which is how ONE gap opened in TWO places. `frozenNothingDueRows` now builds it once; the ownership map stays at the call site because only the caller knows whose scope it read. It also closes a defect neither copy could see: `result.cards` holds one obligation per STATEMENT, so a card with a current statement AND a next-cycle estimate appeared twice and would have rendered as `2 cards (all of them named "Chase Sapphire")` — the disclosure telling the reader two accounts are unreadable when there is one. **A pre-existing P1, found while mapping and fixed here because /calendar depends on it:** `frozenLoanNote` hardcoded "Your bank" and the imperative "Check it with your lender before paying", and `payment-reminders-card.tsx` was ALREADY calling it on a partner's shared loan — only the next step was ownership-aware. #303's critic P1-1 closed exactly this for cards and left the loan branch untouched. **A copy correction the tests forced out:** the multi-row all-clear said "N of the cards here", but every caller of that builder is inside an all-clear branch, which lists NOTHING — "here" had no antecedent on any of the four surfaces that printed it, and no test pinned the string. **What /calendar states and what it does not.** Role is `instruction` and not negotiable; the set resolves against the due events the displayed MONTH actually paints, so paging to a quiet month is silent; the title counts ACCOUNTS, not events, because one card can paint two due dates in one month. The summary-line note claims INCLUSION and no direction — a payment already made makes the money-out total too high, while an estimate off a frozen balance can be wrong either way, so a single directional claim would be false half the time. |
| 305 | the narrowings that strip the fact, and the loan nobody could name (L.20) | **One disease, three faces.** `feedDroppedAt` rides the money out of the providers and is then dropped at a server-side NARROWING, so three surfaces printed figures from a frozen balance in silence. **(1) The Today feed.** It prints the two sharpest instructions in the app — "About $X short by DATE" and "A transfer of about $X would cover it" — and `Proposal` is a closed field set with no slot for the fact. It now carries `fundingFrozen`, the `autopayCents` idiom: verbatim, per-kind, null on every kind not projected from that balance. The dip takes the RADAR's starting account (labelled as the radar labels it) and the shortfall takes `cashNeeded.fundingFrozen` completed with the surface's own `paymentAccountName` — never each other's, because the two engines are handed the same account today and nothing guarantees it, and a disclosure naming the wrong row is worse than none. **The half that matters is the silent one:** a balance frozen HIGH reports shortfall $0 and produces no dip, so BOTH builders return null and the feed prints "Nothing needs you today." over a projection that cannot see the account it is projecting — the /calendar P1-1 of L.19, one surface along. `NudgeFeed.fundingFrozen` carries it, resolved against `ordered` (what will actually render) rather than the builder locals, exclusive with the per-proposal field so the sentence appears exactly once, and deliberately NOT gated on the feed being empty: one unrelated opportunity at the top would otherwise re-open the hole. It reads BOTH sources, because the radar can be walking a frozen account with no cash-needed result beside it. **A new sentence, not a borrowed one.** `frozenProjectionNote` opens "This projection starts from…" and the feed renders no projection — the antecedent-less phrasing L.19 had to correct twice. `frozenNoWarningNote` is about the ABSENCE of a warning, and may not call the feed empty either, since the fact is carried whenever no proposal states it. **(2) The undatable frozen loan.** `selectLoanObligations` refuses to date a loan without both a positive payment and a due day — correctly; the engine never fabricates a date — but nothing carried the refusal out, so it reached no dues list, no reminder and no all-clear. `selectUndatableFrozenLoans` is its exact complement, and `FrozenNothingDueRow.kind` gains `'undatable-loan'`: a THIRD mechanism, because the loan wording is false about it twice over (it names a stored payment and due date as merely stale when there is none, and implies the gap could close on its own when the bank that would send one has stopped sharing). FROZEN ONLY, deliberately — a live undatable loan's gap has a different remedy, and one sentence for both would name the wrong mechanism. The two loan kinds share a dedupe namespace: a self-contradiction on one screen is a worse failure than a missed row. **(3) The PDF export.** `getDashboardData`'s account map stripped the flag, under a footer asserting "Balances reflect the data source at export time" — affirmatively FALSE about a frozen row, in a durable artifact handed to a lender with no way to correct itself. `feedDroppedAt` is now REQUIRED on the report payload so the compiler asks; the row is marked inline, the summary uses `frozenTotalNote` naming BOTH figures the balance is inside (the headline and the trend) with `nextStep: 'open-app'` since a file holds no control; and the footer no longer claims a currency it was never checking for — rewritten to be true unconditionally rather than branched. The PDF's copy was extracted into pure exports for the reason `today-feed-copy.ts` gives: money copy inside a binary artifact is otherwise testable only by grepping compressed bytes, so in practice it would not be tested. **Deliberately not done, recorded:** the net-worth and transactions CSVs (a two-column machine format has no honest slot for prose — a column or a companion file is a format decision, not a copy one), a LIVE undatable loan, /investments and the debt-payoff path (`DebtInput` needs a widened type, not copy). |

| 307 | Safe-to-Spend becomes guilt-free spending (L.11(C), owner decision #295) | The Spending Plan is reframed in the I Will Teach You to Be Rich sense: leftToSpend = expected income − spending outside credit cards − upcoming bills (per remaining OCCURRENCE — a biweekly paycheck with two paydays left counts twice) − card obligations DUE THIS CALENDAR MONTH (the cash-needed engine's own perDueDate rows, filtered to the month) − max(goal contributions, a new Settings savings-% target `User.savingsTargetBps`, nullable additive). Income and expenses BOTH exclude credit accounts (a card row reaches the plan only through its obligation, so purchases are never charged twice and a cashback is never double-benefited). Disclosures ride the plan (SpendingPlanWithNotes) and are resolved against the summed row set: undated OWING cards, statement-pending cards due this month, both-live duplicate pairs, frozen cards — DISCLOSE, never adjust (#192/#299 stance). Every qualifier's direction word is stated for THE FIGURE THE BRANCH RENDERS and flips on the overspent branch. `cardObligationsEstimated` rides the plan into the Glass-Box trace (5-term identity, savings row labeled by its deciding source). The three inverse planners take REQUIRED `unallocatedSavingsCents` and name the target's reserve instead of declaring "beyond budget" over money already set aside. Copy sweep: dashboard card, /spending-plan (5-segment bar, "What this figure can't see"), Ask answer/facts/chips/llm/learned-phrases, solver share-of copy, conscious buckets (fixed = spent+bills+card payments, label "Fixed costs"), redact label; parser keeps "safe to spend" as a permanent alias and gates the new guilt-free alias off past-tense questions ("Guilt Free Bakery" is a merchant). DELIBERATE: loan/mortgage payments with no detected recurring series are counted ZERO times (STATUS §L.11(C)); a next-month estimate is legitimately out of window; the demo user now honestly reads "Over plan by $2,468.83" (June dues $5,412.33 vs $4,900 income — the hero's balance-based question still shows covered, and each surface states its window); household-scope hero uses the MERGED cash-needed figure and the plan claims coherence only with the viewer's own rows. Two fresh-context critics cycle 1: FAIL — 4 P1-class money findings (cross-month double-reservation; estimate-path card silently excluded; solver double-reserving the target; demo income half-counted via unexpanded BIWEEKLY) + direction-inverted overspent qualifiers + false statement-provenance + alias mis-route + cashback double-benefit + overpaid-card wrong direction, ALL fixed + 8 REGRESSION_LEDGER rows. | Owner-chosen verbatim 2026-07-24 ("Subtract card obligations and predefined savings % goals. You set that up in settings… guilt free spending per the book"); the old card contradicted the cash-needed shortfall because it subtracted only detected recurring series. The month-window rule is what makes a CALENDAR-MONTH budget compatible with statement-cycle obligations without reserving one bill against two months; max-not-sum is what stops the target and the goals counting the same pay-yourself-first intent twice. |
| 308 | the account name a person chooses, and the two places a name is read (L.7, owner-requested 2026-07-24) | Owner, verbatim: *"there should be a way to edit name of accounts myself. Similar to simplifi.com."* His screen carries three cards the feed calls `CREDIT CARD` and two called `Venture`; #296/#297/#298 each spent a build cycle teaching a surface to tell same-named rows apart, and none of them could improve the data itself. He can, in five seconds. **(a) A SECOND COLUMN, `Account.displayName` (additive, nullable), which no ingest path writes.** A user-owned flag on `name` would be reverted by the next cron, because both providers rewrite `name` verbatim on every sync; the survival claim is locked by a test that asserts the real ingest MODULES never mention the column, rather than by a test that re-writes the sync by hand. **(b) THE DEFAULT POINTS AT THE FEED, and the direction is the safety property.** `name` stays the value every module reads unless it explicitly asks for the label through `accountLabel()`. A display site nobody remembered to update therefore shows the bank's name — stale, not wrong — while a comparison nobody remembered to update keeps comparing the strings the bank sent. **Cycle 1 proved why that matters, and cost the P0:** `buildCombineInputs` had been handed `accountLabel(a)`, the combine planner sorts its rows by name, and its direction is order-dependent through a `claimed` set — so a cosmetic rename inverted which Plaid connection the card recommends DISCONNECTING, and confirming that revokes a Plaid item. A label may not decide an irreversible external action. Locked by running the real planner twice over data differing only in `displayName` and requiring equal output, with a vacuity guard proving the planner is still name-sensitive. **(c) TYPE IS NOT EDITABLE HERE** even though Quicken Simplifi edits name and type together — `type` drives the net-worth classifier and the cash-needed engine, so it is a money change wearing a label's clothes. **(d) The last-4 identity line (#298) is untouched.** **(e) A NICKNAME IS PERSONAL AND NEVER CROSSES TO ANOTHER USER.** Both critics broke the first version here: `getSharedSnapshotSlice` read the partner's rows with a bare `findMany`, so a partner who renamed his shared card "Divorce lawyer card" had that string printed to the other member on the household dashboard, /cards, /calendar and in the weekly digest EMAIL. The fix is one boundary, not N fences: the slice now `select`s an explicit column list that omits `displayName`, so it never enters the other member's process — a fetch-then-strip would have re-leaked the day someone adds a second user-authored column. The one exception, added in the same cycle, is the household duplicate advisory, which prints the VIEWER's own name for the viewer's own row and the bank's for the partner's, so the page stops calling one account two things. `docs/PRIVACY.md` and the sharing-consent copy now say all of this. **Two helpers were built and then WITHDRAWN, both recorded rather than quietly kept:** `accountEvidenceLabel` (appending *(your bank calls this "X")* to identity-card labels) stacked parentheticals inside prompts and aria labels, asserted a bank for MANUAL rows that have none, and attributed to the bank a SimpleFIN string this app itself composes from org + account name; and `accountSearchNames` (letting Ask match the nickname too) fed a branch that sums every match without separating assets from liabilities, so a second short user-chosen string could turn one right answer into a total adding money owed to money held. Each returns when its prerequisite lands. What remains is deliberately two-valued: `accountLabel(a)` for anything a reader reads, `a.name` for every comparison, every sort a decision depends on, and every surface belonging to someone other than the row's owner. |
| 309 | a window has an edge, and money does not reset when it is crossed (L.11(D)) | Owner-reported 2026-07-25 with three screenshots, verbatim: *"It's worse now."* His dashboard said $18,814.14 was needed by Wed, Aug 5 to pay all 7 cards and, directly beneath it, offered **$22,254.09 guilt-free to spend at $3,709.01 a day**. Cause, read in the code: `getSpendingPlan` sums only obligation rows with `date <= endOfMonth`, and every one of his cards is dated Aug 5 — five days past July 31 — so the term was exactly $0.00. L.11(C) had removed the mirror error (one statement reserved against two months' income); this is its other side, a statement reserved against **no month the reader can see**, because this month calls it next month's business and next month's plan arrives after the money is gone. Each figure was right about its own window and the PAIR was an instruction to overdraft. **The decision: the same `perDueDate` rows, from the OTHER side of the same filter, become a sixth term — and NET of the income that arrives before those payments are due.** Four things worth recording. **(a) A cap was built first, and rejected.** It bounded guilt-free by the funding account's projected low point — the number the dashboard prints beside it, which made the two coherent by construction. Two fresh-context critics broke it independently and identically: it caps a FLOW (a month's income) with a STOCK (one account's worst balance), and because the balance walk records its minimum on day one, the cap became *"never more than what is in checking right now"* — on a $200 pre-payday float with $6,000 landing on the 31st and one $1,000 card it held back $6,000 and reported $28.57/day. Its subtrahend was also a RESIDUAL, absorbing savings sweeps, unarrived income and other accounts, while five surfaces printed it as *held for card payments*. A conservative error is only safe while it is small. **(b) Netting is not optional.** Cycle 2 found the gross version re-committed L.11(C)'s error with the sign flipped: widening the EXPENSE window past the month's edge without widening the INCOME window reserves a full statement every month, permanently, for the commonest issuer pattern (paid the 1st, cards due the 3rd), and reserves a payment dated 30 days out that next month's plan shows on its first day. The term is now the WORST RUNNING GAP walked point by point — income landing after an earlier payment cannot pay it — so what is reserved is only what this month's income must actually cover. **(c) Provenance rides the money, twice.** With every card dated past the edge, `cardObligationsEstimated` is false by construction, so the new row needed its own `obligationsBeyondMonthEstimated` or a figure that is entirely guesswork off current balances would print with the authority of a generated statement (cycle-2 P0, executed). For the same reason the disclosure set (`summedIds`) now resolves against ALL of `perDueDate`, not the in-month half: widening the figure without widening the set left a frozen or duplicated card driving a subtraction that no surface could qualify. **(d) The adjustment had to enter as a ROW.** The Glass-Box panel's whole claim is that its lines add up to the number above; the cap, applied outside the rows, would have falsified that sentence on exactly the screens where it mattered — and forcing the adjustment into the rows is what exposed that its label could not be made honest. Two critic cycles, both FAIL, 1 P0 + 11 P1 across them, every one fixed or explicitly recorded; the surviving residuals (loans have the same unfixed edge; `upcoming` estimates sit outside `perDueDate`; the conscious-spending strip and the three inverse solvers consume the figure without the new fact) are in docs/STATUS.md §L.11(D). |
